II's cell and Postulate 2's last sentence with its first clause, `Space.RingsDrawn`); so `Λ = 3/R_Λ²`. `chain_from_sources` derives the three results that the chain's theorems keep as hypotheses. Mathlib has no Lorentzian geometry, so it can neither construct the solutions of Einstein's equations nor prove the cited theorems of geometry that the route uses; the space at an instant is a term `sp` of a type `Sp`, `embedSp` places the space forms among the spaces, and each cited theorem is a named hypothesis that asserts the theorem for these terms, with its inputs proved or taken from the postulates (`Sources.Geometry`). `cosmological_constant_model` shows that the hypotheses of `cosmological_constant_from_sources` can be met together, in the Fano register with `Bridge.regSix`, a spacetime of one point (`vacuumSpacetime`) and the three-sphere of radius `R_Λ` (`modelSp`), on which the three rings through the cell `(0, false)` are drawn along great circles through `R_Λe₀` in the directions `e₁`, `e₂`, `e₃`, their cells at the angles `jπ/3`, distinct cells at distinct points (`rings_drawn_model`). The file imports `Symmetry.lean`, `Bridge.lean`, `Capacity.lean`, `Qubit.lean`, `Cosmo.lean` and `Space.lean`.

`DiffSet.lean` builds a register from a perfect difference set: a set `D` of residues mod `n` such that every nonzero residue is the difference of exactly one ordered pair of its elements (`IsPerfectDifferenceSet`), the Singer form of a projective plane of order `|D| − 1`, whose lines are the translates `D + l`. A cell is (point, side), and the ring `l` holds both sides of the points `d + l`, `d ∈ D`, running through them in the order of `D` on one side and back through them on the other (`ofDiffSet`, `ofDiffSetPositions`). The first four clauses of Postulate 2 follow from the difference property, and the maps of the clause that no cell differs from another are the translations `p ↦ p + t` and the swap of sides (`dsSym`, `ofDiffSet_homogeneous`), and the register's order as a projective plane is `|D| − 1` (`register_of_diffSet`). `{0, 1, 3}` mod 7 is the Fano case.

`Singer.lean` proves Singer's theorem [J. Singer, Trans. Amer. Math. Soc. 43, 377 (1938)]: for a finite field `K` of `q` elements there is a perfect difference set of `q + 1` residues mod `q² + q + 1` (`exists_pds`). With `E` the cubic extension of `K` (Mathlib's `FiniteField.Extension K p 3`), `ω` a generator of its units and `W` a plane of `E` over `K`, the set is the `k` for which `ω^k` lies in `W`. A subspace of dimension `d` holds `(q^d − 1)/(q − 1)` of the points `ω^k`, `0 ≤ k < q² + q + 1`, its nonzero vectors being those points times the nonzero elements of `K`, each in one way (`card_nonzero`, `card_points`). A pair of the set with difference `t` is a point of `W ∩ ω^{−t}W`, and two planes of a space of dimension 3 share at least a line, so every nonzero difference occurs at least once; the differences of distinct elements number `|D|(|D| − 1) = q² + q`, one for each nonzero residue, so each occurs exactly once. With `DiffSet.lean`, a register of `L = 2(q + 1)` whose cells and rings meet the first four clauses of Postulate 2, with the maps of the clause that no cell differs from another, exists for every finite field and so for every prime power (`homogeneous_register`, `homogeneous_register_of_prime_power`). Whether the paper's `L ≈ 4.4×10⁶¹` itself is of that form is not known, since `L` is fixed only to about a percent. That some value of that form lies within the percent is a theorem: `Main.register_in_band` gives a register with `L = 2(52289¹³ + 1) = 4.36882×10⁶¹`, which lies between the least and the greatest `L_cos` over the box of Planck's `H₀` and `Ω_Λ` (`Numerics.Lof_box`, `Numerics.witness_in_band`, which also certifies that 52289 is prime). That there are many, about one even `L` in 141 near `L_cos` and about 4×10⁵⁷ in the band (the paper's 3×10⁵⁷ counts those within a percent of this `L`), rests on the distribution of primes, for which the paper cites Dusart. It is a citation, not a theorem of these files, and no theorem uses it. `Singer.lean` is the only file that imports Mathlib's extensions of finite fields (`Mathlib.FieldTheory.Finite.Extension`).

`Pairing.lean`, `Conditional.lean`, `Strings.lean`, `Receiving.lean`, `Constant.lean`, `Matter.lean` and `Interpolation.lean` state sentences of the paper that the files up to v18-64 took in words (v18-65).

`Pairing.lean` counts what a pairing of strings measured jointly shows (Sec. IV.A). A pairing gives each position its joint outcome, and the frequency of a joint outcome is the share of the positions that show it (`Pairing.freq`). The positions are the cells of the ring, `L` of them on a largest ring. Every frequency is a multiple of `1/L` (`Pairing.freq_multiple`), the frequencies sum to one (`Pairing.sum_freq`), and no pairing shows more than `L` joint outcomes (`Pairing.shown_le_cells`), so where more than `L` occur every pairing gives one of them the frequency zero (`Pairing.pairing_misses`). A pairing shows a state exactly when every probability is a multiple of `1/L` and the multiples sum to `L` (`Pairing.shown_exactly_iff`). For two strings prepared apart, each fixed, a pairing is a one-to-one map of the positions. The product `m₁m₂/L²` of the two fractions is a frequency only when `L` divides `m₁m₂` (`Pairing.product_multiple_iff`, `Pairing.product_not_shown`), which fails for most fractions (`Pairing.most_products_not_shown`); some pairing lies within one cell of the product (`Pairing.pairing_within_one_cell`), and the strings do not fix the pairing (`Pairing.pairing_not_fixed`). `N` strings read in step keep each its own fraction in every pairing (`Pairing.inStep`, `Pairing.strings_in_step`); there `bits i x` is the bit of the string `i` at the position `x` of the pairing. The block of a string for a joint outcome of the earlier strings is the set of the positions at which they show it (`Pairing.blockOf`), and the frequency of a joint outcome that some position shows is the product, string by string, of the frequencies of its bits on its blocks (`Pairing.freq_chain`). A block of `r` cells holds a conditional probability that is a multiple of `1/r` and a phase that is one of `r` cyclic positions (`Pairing.BlockPhase`, `Pairing.block_resolution`). A state whose probabilities are the frequencies of `N` strings read in step is, up to its global phase, those frequencies with one phase on each block of each string (`Pairing.jointPhase`, `Pairing.state_of_strings`); the phases of that statement are real numbers, of which a block of `r` cells holds `r`. A sample of `n` shots shows at most `n` outcomes (`Pairing.sample_outcomes_le`). A state that vanishes on the outcomes outside a set overlaps any state by at most the probability that the other gives the set (`Pairing.overlap_le`), which `Main.overlap_bound` states for what the register holds, where the conditional states of the last qubit all differ in the state that the circuit calls for, and `Main.return_bound`, with the gates (`Sources.QM.gates`), for the return of the random circuit.

`Conditional.lean` counts the conditional states of Sec. IV.A, a qubit's states given the outcomes of the earlier qubits. The amplitude of a state is written as a product of one factor for each qubit, and the conditional state of a qubit at a reading is its factor at each of its two bits (`Conditional.condState`). In a product state a qubit has one (`Conditional.card_product_states_le_one`). In the cluster state of a graph, whose amplitude is a sign with one factor for each qubit (`Conditional.clusterSign`, `Conditional.clusterSign_eq`), a qubit's factor depends on the earlier qubits only through the product of the bits of its neighbors among them (`Conditional.factor_depends`), so it has at most two conditional states at every `N` (`Conditional.card_cluster_states_le_two`). In a stabilizer measurement, one qubit read along `X` after the others along `Z`, the factor of that qubit is the amplitude of the measurement's outcome (`Conditional.stabAmp`), and its conditional state, `condState` as in the other cases, is one sign times `Conditional.stabState` (`Conditional.stabState_spec`); where the amplitude does not vanish the bit of that qubit is the product of its neighbors' bits (`Conditional.stabilizer_bit`), and it has at most two conditional states (`Conditional.card_stab_states_le_two`). A state in which a qubit's conditional state depends on the earlier qubits only through how many of them read `−1` gives it at most `N` conditional states (`Conditional.card_states_by_count`). Two cells for each of at most `N` conditional states fit a ring of `L` cells where `2N ≤ L` (`Conditional.two_cells_fit`). A joint outcome is not a conditioned outcome. A joint outcome is what one joint measurement shows at a position, the bits of all its strings there. A conditioned outcome is the outcome of one qubit given the outcomes of the others, its bit in the conditional state that they give it. `Main.overlap_bound` and `Main.return_bound` take Postulate 1's possible outcomes (`Sources.P1.outcomes_at_positions`) for the last qubit's conditioned outcomes: a conditional state that occurs with a bit has that bit at a cell of a block that holds the state. The conditional states are those of the state `ψ` that the circuit calls for, and a block is taken to hold the conditional state that `ψ` gives its joint outcome. In a product state, by its amplitudes, any two joint outcomes of the other qubits that occur give the last qubit one conditional state, so its conditional states do not all differ (`Conditional.product_one_state`): strings that are measured jointly are not thereby conditioned. A string with two cells for each different conditional state has a pairing and bits with which every conditional state has either bit at a cell whose block holds it (`Conditional.room_holds`). The premise is then met whatever the register holds, the state that the circuit calls for included, even where the joint outcomes of that state outnumber the cells. In a stabilizer measurement the conditional state of the measured qubit gives the outcome `1` the probability `1` or `0`, a fixed bit (`Conditional.stab_prob`). A block of any size holds it with no rounding, and on a block that holds it the bit at every position is the product of the neighbors' bits, at every `N` (`Conditional.stabilizer_held`).

`Strings.lean` proves six sentences of Sec. II. The bits that the cells hold, one for every pair of a cell and a ring through it, are the same as a family of strings, one for each ring, each of `L` bits (`Horizon.ringStrings`, `Horizon.card_ring`). Under the shift a bit is back at its cell after `L` ticks and not before (`Horizon.Positions.shift`, `Horizon.Positions.lap`). The cells one step from a cell along a ring are two, one ahead and one behind (`Horizon.Positions.two_senses`). Where a law says of a string's state what it says of the state with the direction of the ring reversed, either sense occurs under a law that some state obeys, so the state fixes the sense and the law does not (`Strings.sense_of_state`). A string whose senses are equally likely has them equally likely after an interaction that changes either sense with one probability (`Strings.bit_kept`). Where scattering takes from the strings not yet scattered at the rate of their own fraction, that fraction is `e^{−t}` after `t` scattering times, and fewer than one of `R` strings is left exactly when `t > ln R` (`Strings.unscattered`).

`Receiving.lean` proves that a great circle which crosses a sphere of the three-sphere has exactly two points on the sphere's surface (`Crossing.two_crossings`), the two receiving cells of a string in Sec. IV.C.

`Constant.lean` holds four statements about the constant `Λ`, the solution with a mass and the horizons. On the vacuum with `R_ab = Λ g_ab` (`Einstein.vacuumModel`, what the files see of de Sitter, Minkowski and anti-de Sitter space) the three inputs of Einstein's equations hold for every value of `Λ`, and the equations hold with that value and no other (`Einstein.any_constant_model`). A term of the stress tensor that is a multiple of the metric vanishes on every null vector (`Einstein.quad_add_smul_eta_of_null`), so Jacobson's step does not see a vacuum energy (`Main.vacuum_energy_unseen`). The Schwarzschild–de Sitter solution about a mass `M > 0` with `Λ > 0` has a horizon exactly when `9Λ(GM/c²)² ≤ 1` (`Reg.horizon_limit`), Nariai's limit. The period of an accelerated observer's horizon, in ticks, is to `L` as `c²/R_Λ` is to his acceleration, so his horizon is his own, `L_a < L`, exactly where his acceleration exceeds `c²/R_Λ` (`Reg.La_lt_L`).

`Matter.lean` names the matter a string holds on average, its mean energy less its heat, `u = (1 − ln 2)ε_s` (`Reg.matter`, `Reg.matter_eq`), the unit in which Sec. IV.C counts a body's energy per string. It proves that a string's energy splits into heat and matter in the proportions `ln 2` and `1 − ln 2` only if that energy is the mean (`Reg.split_not_stringwise`). It also proves that in the solution without a mass the proper distance from the observer lies between the areal radius `r` and `r/√(1 − r²/R_Λ²)` (`Reg.radii_agree`), and that a qubit whose two states have different energies holds less than one bit in its thermal state (`Qubit.entropy_lt_of_energies_ne`).

`Interpolation.lean` compares Milgrom's simple interpolating function with Eq. (law) (Sec. IV.C). With one scale `a₀` in both, the acceleration that the simple function gives (`Interpolation.simple`, `Interpolation.simple_root`) is at least that of Eq. (law) and at most `21/20` of it at every `g_N > 0` (`Interpolation.simple_within`), by two bounds on the exponential (`Interpolation.sq_le_exp`, `Interpolation.exp_le_of_one_le`).

`Ledger.lean` prints the axioms of every main theorem.

`Sources.lean`, `Main.lean` and `Premises.lean` make the account of the premises part of the build. `Sources.lean` is the one place where every premise the files take is written down, each as a definition named by its source, with the sentence of the paper it is quoted in its docstring: the namespaces `P1` and `P2` for the sentences of the postulates (with Sec. II's definitions), `Thermo` for thermodynamics, `Geometry` for the cited theorems of Lorentzian and Riemannian geometry, `Condition` for the side conditions that define the quantity a result treats, `Derived` for the results that other results take by name and `Main.lean` derives (`G` from Jacobson's step for any entropy density, and at the count's density to leading order and exactly; Jacobson's step at every point; the horizon at `R_Λ`; the solution about a body at rest; the acceleration `g_N/b` of Eq. (share); the body's energy per string as `g_N/a₀`, Eq. (f); the law, Eq. (law); the speeds on circular orbits; and the horizon and `G` at every time, which are by definition the derived statements at each time), `Approx` for the one step at leading order in `1/L`, the count's density, whose exact form `Main.lean` derives, `Units` for the units the results are stated in, the SI's defining constants `c`, `ℏ` and `k_B`, and `Open` for premises from none of the four sources, of which there are none. Its header lists the structures whose fields carry sentences of the postulates or objects of the geometry (`Horizon`, `Horizon.Positions`, `Horizon.Symmetry`, `Qubit.Op`, `Qubit.Vec`, `Qubit.Tick`, `Reg`, `Einstein.Spacetime`, `Space.SpaceForm`, `Space.SphereQuotient`, with the definitions `Reg.fSdS`, `Reg.EMS` and `Reg.EK`), and says that no law of general relativity is taken: Einstein's equations are derived from the postulates with thermodynamics by Jacobson's argument and the first law (one entropy density on every horizon from the count and Postulate 2, the Clausius relation at the Unruh temperature on every local horizon through a point, the Raychaudhuri equation for the area, energy conserved in every small region for every observer; `Einstein.lean`), and what the files take from general relativity is mathematics, cited: published theorems, each a definition of `Geometry` that states it with its inputs, proved in the files or taken from the postulates, and cites where it is proved (Lorentzian geometry on the manifold Postulate 2 names: the Raychaudhuri equation, the contracted Bianchi identity, the Leibniz rule for a flow, the Newtonian limit of geodesic motion, the curvature of a static, spherically symmetric metric and of the static weak field [Wald 1984; Weinberg 1972; O'Neill 1983]; Riemannian geometry of the space at an instant: the Killing–Hopf theorem with Schur's lemma and Hopf–Rinow [Lee 2018; Hopf 1926]; and Einstein's constraint on the space at an instant [Wald 1984; O'Neill 1983]). The Schwarzschild–de Sitter solution is derived from the vacuum equations (`Main.schwarzschild_deSitter`); the Misner–Sharp and Komar energies are definitions, evaluated on it. Mathlib has no Lorentzian geometry, so none of the cited theorems is rederived. `Main.lean` states the paper's results, eighty-one theorems, from the count to the horizon a mass draws in, a register with an `L` in Planck's band, and the paper's statements that were proved in the other files, with these definitions as their only hypotheses; each applies theorems of the other files, with short arguments joining them. `Premises.lean` defines the command `#premises`, which reads the type of each result, lists every binder with its type, and fails the build if a hypothesis is not a definition of `Sources.lean` (or the class `NeZero` or `Nonempty`) or an object's type is not one of the types it allows; its output, one line per binder, is `premises_output.txt`, which the continuous integration compares with the build's. So a premise that is not in `Sources.lean` cannot enter a result, and the account is complete by construction: a review can dispute whether a listed definition matches the sentence it quotes, not find an unlisted premise. The command also fails the build in three cases. A result takes nothing from a source, that is, no premise of the postulates, of thermodynamics or of the cited geometry, no derived statement and no object of a structure that carries clauses of the postulates, and it does not state that such a structure has an instance, as `Main.register_in_band` states that a register exists. A derived statement is neither the conclusion of a result that takes something from a source and whose own derived premises are concluded before it, nor by its definition such a statement at every time. A theorem of the namespace `Register.Main`, or of a namespace under it, that stands before the command in `Main.lean` is not among the results that the command is given. The block of each result ends with a line that names what the result rests on, and the ledger ends with the result that concludes each derived statement. These checks read what a result takes. Lean's unused-variables linter reports a hypothesis that neither the statement nor the proof mentions, and the workflow builds with `--wfail`, so that its warning fails the build. Neither tests whether the conclusion needs a premise, which depends on the statement: a result may state, beside what its premises give, a step of mathematics that needs none of them, as `Main.coding_step` states the count of a code's conditional states. Where a part of a result is known to need no premise, its docstring says so. A side condition states the case that the paper treats, a positive radius or at least one qubit (`1 ≤ N`), and some conclusions hold beyond their conditions, as the field of `Main.hubble` does at every `r`.

## The inputs, and where they enter

Beyond its two postulates the paper takes three sources, as Sec. III lists, none of them a number: thermodynamics, which gives the thermal horizon and the Clausius relation, for every local horizon (Sec. III) and for the cosmological horizon (Sec. IV.D) with the strings in the thermal state at its temperature, which for free strings is the product of their own thermal states (Sec. II), the spread of the strings' energies at that temperature (Sec. IV.E), the first law for every small region and every observer (Sec. III) and for the cosmic fluid (Sec. VI), the Deser–Levin temperature (Sec. IV.C), and equipartition and the principle of maximum entropy for the galactic law (Secs. IV.C and IV.E); from quantum mechanics, the gates, which act on what the register holds as on any state (Sec. IV.A); and cited mathematics, the theorems of Lorentzian and Riemannian geometry (`Sources.Geometry`). Every one that a theorem takes is a hypothesis, and Lean shows where each enters. The tick of a free string of energy `E` is not among them: it is Sec. II's definition of energy, which is that of quantum mechanics, a hypothesis in the namespace `P1`, which holds Postulate 1 with Sec. II's definitions (`Sources.P1.string_energies`). The gates give the bound on the return above the ceiling, the gate fidelity times the probability that the random state gives at most `L` outcomes, where the conditional states of the last qubit all differ (`Main.return_bound`), and the Porter–Thomas distribution of a random state's probabilities, cited in Sec. IV.A, gives it its numbers (`Numerics.porter_thomas_bound`). The paper also takes the three dimensions of space as observed (Sec. IV.D, where it cites a companion preprint for the number); the dimension is built into `Space.SpaceForm` and into the Killing–Hopf premise. That the energy inside the horizon is the strings' (`hin`) and that a body's energy is held by the strings that carry its information (`hcar`) are Postulate 1's first sentence applied as premises, the thesis (the table); that the body's share at a string enters as an amplitude (`hb`) is Postulate 1 with the probability rule of Sec. IV.A (its own row). The symmetry of space, which versions of the paper up to -6-36 also took from outside, follows in the paper from two clauses of Postulate 2 (Sec. II), with the laws functions of the register and space determined by the rings, both the thesis of the paper applied (the paragraph after the table), and `Symmetry.laws_same` and `Symmetry.lawAt_same` prove the step from the two clauses, each a hypothesis listed below; it enters through the solution, by the step of `Solution.lean` and `Space.lean` with the thesis applied to the geometry (`hgeom`) and to space (`hclose`, `hslice`) as named hypotheses, and through Jacobson's step, which takes one entropy density, one `η`, on every local horizon (`hJ`; `Main.jacobson_G` derives it from the step at every point, the count's density and Newton's law, through `Solution.jacobson_G_from_sources`); the strings of a sphere, for which those versions took it, are counted ring by ring, on average over the sphere's position (`Bridge.sphere_strings`); averaged over the rotations of the drawing carried from the rotations `x ↦ qx` of `ℍ`, the count about any point is that average, in any four-dimensional space (`Center.sphere_strings_centered_of`); every physical law and quantity is defined by qubits, so the drawing is no part of the state and no rotation of it is preferred, and the count about a given body is defined as the average over the rotations with any weighting that prefers none (`Sources.P1.no_rotation_preferred`), which is the unit sphere's measure, normalized (`Weighting.eq_toSphere_of_invariant`, the uniqueness of Haar measure). The history of the bit in Sec. II also rests on the observed hot early universe, an observation and not a hypothesis of any theorem. With the strings' energies spread at the horizon's temperature, a string's energy is `k_BT_dS` on average and the vacuum's share `ln 2` (Sec. IV.E; `string_energy`, `vacuum_share`); `Numerics.energy_today` certifies the energy inside `R_Λ` at the measured `Ω_Λ` for comparison. In the table, each row names its premises as the theorems name them, with their definitions in `Sources.lean`, the theorems of the other files that use them, and every result of `Main.lean` that takes them, as `premises_output.txt` lists them result by result.

| Kind | Input | In the code | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thermodynamics | a horizon is thermal: `k_BT = ℏ/τ`, with `τ` its imaginary-time period [KMS], which is `2πc/a` for a horizon of surface gravity `a` [Unruh, Gibbons–Hawking]; for the cosmological horizon of the solution without a mass, at its radius `r_h`, `a` is the surface gravity there, `−g(r_h)`, which is `a_Λ = c²/R_Λ` (`DeSitter.surface_gravity_eq`, from `hdS`), and the period is then the lap time of the largest ring (`DeSitter.horizon_period`, `DeSitter.thermal_lap`)| `I1` (`Sources.Thermo.horizon_thermal`), taken two or three times by the results on the carriers, under the names `I1dS`, `I1N` and `I1a`, for the de Sitter horizon, the Newtonian field and the acceleration `a`; `I1` of `Main.lambda_with_mass` and `Main.first_law_extended` for the cosmological horizon about a body at rest (`Sources.Thermo.horizon_thermal_family`) | `T_ring`, `T_dS`, `T_Unruh`, `clausius`, `newton`, `equipartition_in_cells`, `horizon_temperature`, `entropy_removed`, `vacuum_energy`, `vacuum_per_string`, `string_tick`, `string_split`, `vacuum_share`, `string_energy`, `fill_fraction`, `radial_acceleration_relation`; in `Main.lean`, `Main.chain`, `Main.newton`, `Main.entropy_removed`, `Main.string_energy`, `Main.vacuum_share`, `Main.temperature_in_cells`, `Main.newton_in_cells`, `Main.carriers_share`, `Main.fill_fraction`, `Main.galactic_law`, `Main.temperature_led`, `Main.frame_of_law`; and for the family, `Main.lambda_with_mass`, `Main.first_law_extended` |
| Thermodynamics | in de Sitter space an observer held at acceleration `a` sees `2πT = (R⁻² + a²)^{1/2}` in units `c = ℏ = k_B = 1`, `R` the de Sitter radius [Deser–Levin, Eq. (8)], a case of the thermality of horizons; the files state it with the surface gravity of the horizon of the solution without a mass, `k_BT = (ℏ/2πc)√(a² + g(r_h)²)`, and derive `−g(r_h) = a_Λ = c²/R_Λ` from `hdS` (`DeSitter.surface_gravity_eq`) | `hDL` (`Sources.Thermo.deser_levin`) | `temperature_floor`, `Main.temperature_floor`; and `Main.temperature_led`, for the alternative that the temperature leads |
| Thermodynamics | Jacobson's step at every point, in three premises, each under its source: the energy that crosses a local horizon through the point, the boost-energy flux of matter, is heat at the horizon's Unruh temperature, `δQ = T dS` (thermodynamics, `Sources.Thermo.local_clausius`) [Jacobson 1995]; the horizon's entropy is the register's density times its area (the local horizon is crossed by largest rings, whose cells are segments of the geodesics there, Postulate 2's last sentence, and its state is a state of those strings, Postulate 1's first sentence applied to a horizon's state, so its entropy per unit area is a law, the same on every horizon by Postulate 2 and the count's at the cosmological horizon; `Sources.P1.local_entropy`, which takes `dS = η₀ dA` with the uniform `η₀` as its hypothesis, the files having no map from the manifold's points to the cells); and its area changes by the Raychaudhuri equation (Lorentzian geometry, `Sources.Geometry.raychaudhuri`) [Wald, Sec. 9.2]. `Main.jacobson_step` derives from them `R(k, k) = κT(k, k)` for every null `k`, `κ = 2πk_B/ℏcη`, for a density `η ≠ 0` (`Sources.Derived.clausius`); `Einstein.lean` derives from it the field equations up to a multiple of the metric; with the first law and the contracted Bianchi identity, Einstein's equations with one constant `Λ`; and, without those two, with the static weak field and Newton's law, `G = c³k_B/4ℏη` (`Einstein.G_of_clausius`). (2018), p. 348; Hopf (1926)]; its inputs are proved (`Solution.geometry_same_slots`) or the thesis (`hclose`) | `hKH` (`Sources.Geometry.space_form`) | `Space.cosmological_constant_of_sources`, `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources`, `Solution.chain_from_sources`; `Main.chain`, `Main.deSitter_horizon` |

| Postulate 1, light crosses a cell in one tick, and the first sentence for the energy of light | a cell is a segment of length `ℓ_c` of a geodesic of space (Postulate 2's last sentence) and a tick is the time light takes to cross a cell (Sec. II), so light moves `ℓ_c` of proper length per tick along a geodesic, at `c`, in every direction, since a largest ring runs through any place in any direction; in the frame the files use at a point a direction of speed `c` is a null direction, so where light passes the direction `k` of its ray is null. The energy of light is a quantity defined by its qubits (the first sentence), and the light crosses a cell in one tick; the files read this as the energy going with the light, from cell to cell, one per tick along the ring, whichever strings carry it. That reading is the step the premise takes: Sec. II defines the energy of a state, the generator of its tick, and places it in no cell, so the register's side enters as the direction of the ray only. On the manifold a quantity carried along the ray has its current along the ray: the energy current of the light for an observer `u`, `T_ab u^b`, is a multiple of `k_a`, for every `u`, since the events of the ray are the same for every observer (`physical`); the point is one where this light alone passes, as the point of `Condition.dust_flow` is one where matter moves as dust; and light carries energy, so its stress tensor is not zero. From these `Einstein.null_dust_of_current` derives `T = ρ k k`, and with the first law and the Leibniz rule the rays are null geodesics | `hlight` (`Sources.P1.light`) | `Main.light_rays` |
| Thermodynamics, the strings' energies (Secs. III and IV.E) | the strings' energies are spread at the horizon's temperature and do not change (Sec. III: "The energies the strings hold are spread at that temperature and, for matter that stays put, do not change"). A string's energy is the same for all its states, since Sec. II's definition of energy ties it to the one factor of the string's tick (`Sources.P1.string_energies`), and by Sec. IV.E it differs from string to string with the mass the string holds, from zero up. Thermodynamics spreads the energies over the strings as the Gibbs distribution at the horizon's temperature, the weight `e^{−E/k_BT}` on the energy `E`, so their mean is the mean of that distribution, `∫₀^∞ E e^{−E/θ} dE / ∫₀^∞ e^{−E/θ} dE` with `θ = k_BT_dS` (`Law.gibbsMean`), which is `k_BT_dS` (`Law.gibbsMean_eq`; on a ladder of equal steps `ε` it is `ε/(e^{ε/θ} − 1)`, within one step below, `Law.gibbsLadder_eq`, `Law.gibbsLadder_within_step`). So a string's energy is `k_BT_dS` on average (`Carriers.string_energy`), `ℏ` over the lap time of a largest ring, and a string's heat, the horizon's temperature times its one bit, is `ln 2` of that mean, with matter the rest (`string_split`). The energies do not change, the paper treating matter that stays put with interactions negligible (Sec. II), so their spread is a difference between strings and not a change in time, and it is no part of the entropy the horizon exchanges, one bit per ring (`Bridge.horizon_entropy`). This input takes the place of Penrose's relation and the reduction clock of the versions up to v18-56: the relation `τ = ℏ/E` then holds for the mean energy with `τ` the lap time, as a consequence | `hGibbs` (`Sources.Thermo.energies_thermal`) | `Main.string_energy`, `Main.vacuum_share`, `Main.fill_fraction`, `Main.galactic_law` |
| Postulate 1, the first sentence, applied to a body's energy (Sec. IV.C) | energy is held only by strings, and a string that does not carry the body's information keeps, at its two receiving cells, the cells where its ring crosses the sphere, the state it has without the body, so the body's energy, `M` all the mass inside the sphere, is held by its carriers alone, in equal shares by equipartition (`Law.share_of_equipartition` gives the sum in finite form); the carriers are the fraction `b` of the sphere's strings, the definition of `b` | `hcar` (`Sources.P1.energy_in_carriers`), `hNcar` (`Sources.Condition.carrier_fraction`) | `Main.carriers_share`, `Main.galactic_law` |
| Thermodynamics, equipartition (Sec. IV.C) | a string's share of a body's energy is proportional to the temperature at which it holds it, `κT`, with one `κ` for every string, carrier or not; with Eq. (newton) at `g_N`, `κ = 2k_B ln 2`, two Landauer energies per string, so the carriers hold two Landauer energies each at the sphere's temperature and `a = g_N/b` | `hκ`, `hκN` (`Sources.Thermo.equipartition`) | `Main.carriers_share`, `Main.galactic_law` |
| Postulate 1 with the probability rule of Sec. IV.A, the amplitude (Sec. IV.C) | a string that carries the body's information is one of its qubits, so the body's share at a string, `E = 2k_BT_N ln 2` in units of the matter a string holds on average, `u = (1 − ln 2)k_BT_dS`, is the fraction `f = E/u` of a whole qubit, and its amplitude is `√f`; a string carries the body's information when this amplitude exceeds the string's threshold, one for the string at both its receiving cells, so the carriers are the fraction `b = 1 − surv(√f)` of the sphere's strings, `surv` the threshold's survival function | `hb` (`Sources.P1.carriers_by_amplitude`) | `Main.galactic_law`, `Main.scale_mean` |
| Thermodynamics, maximum entropy (Sec. IV.C) | the threshold of a string is a positive quantity of which only the mean is fixed, at one qubit, the unit of the amplitude, so its distribution is the exponential of mean one [Jaynes 1957], `P(θ > x) = e^{−x}`; the same principle gives the Gibbs distribution in Sec. IV.E (`Entropy.shannon_le_shannon_gibbs`) | `hME` (`Sources.Thermo.max_entropy_threshold`) | `Main.galactic_law` |
| Postulate 1, the hidden global phase | the global phase of a state is hidden: no law depends on its value. A law of a string's qubit reads the same off a state and off the state times a phase | `hhidden` (`Sources.P1.phase_hidden`); the free tick carries the sentence in `hsense` of `Qubit.Tick` | `Main.free_tick_hidden` |
| Postulate 2, the clause on directions, for a law of a string | no law depends on the direction in which a ring passes through a cell: a law gives the same for a state of a string and for the state with the direction of the ring reversed. For a law that a state obeys or does not, the reversed state obeys it too; for the law of an interaction, it changes either sense with one probability (Sec. II) | `hdir`, `hflip` (`Sources.P2.direction_free`) | `Main.sense_of_state`, `Main.bit_kept` (with `Strings.sense_of_state`, `Strings.bit_kept`) |
| Quantum mechanics, the gates (Secs. III and IV.A) | a circuit of gates acts on what the register holds as on any state: without error the inverse of the random circuit keeps the overlap of every two states and takes the random state back to the initial one, and a run is without error with the probability of the gate fidelity, in which alone the paper counts a return, so the test returns with that fidelity times the overlap of what is held with the state that the circuit calls for | `hgates` (`Sources.QM.gates`) | `Main.return_bound` |

Nothing else is assumed from outside the paper. **Postulate 1's first sentence, as the files take it.** "Every physical law and quantity is defined by qubits" quantifies over laws and quantities, and the files give each the form it has in them, so that the sentence is stated once for each form and applied by unfolding. A law is a function of the register's crossings, `H.Cell → H.Loop → V`, the type every law has. `Sources.P1.qubits` states the sentence for a quantity that varies over the register's items: it is a function of what the register holds at the item (`Symmetry.IsQubits`, which it is); the geometry of space, with the laws and the state at a slot as the content (`hgeom`), and the weighting of the drawing's rotations, with nothing as the content (`hν`), are its applications. `Sources.P1.physical` states it for a quantity to which another description, the manifold or thermodynamics, assigns a value: that value is the register's, a difference being a physical quantity not defined by qubits; the entropy of the cosmological horizon (`hhor`: the law `η` times the horizon's area is `k_B` times the entropy of the state of the register's strings), the entropy of a local horizon (`hent`: Clausius' `dS` is the law `η` times the area), the energy inside the horizon (`hin`: it is the strings' energies, `energy_inside_strings`), a body's energy (`hcar`: it is held by the strings that carry its information) and the energy current of light (`hlight`: the current `T_ab u^b` every observer sees is along the light's ray, the register's side being the ray's direction only, since the register defines no energy at a cell) are its applications, the first two and the last written with `physical` and the other two as the equation it unfolds to; the quantity may be a number or a current, so `physical` takes values of any one type. `Sources.P1.rings_along` states that a ring runs along every geodesic of a space (`Symmetry.ringsAlong`), the geometry being a quantity; the space at an instant (`hclose`) and its space form (`hslice`, `Space.SliceIsQubits`) are its applications at their sets of geodesics. The sentence so applied is a hypothesis of the theorem that uses it, and it is the sentence itself, not a further premise: it asserts of the named law or quantity exactly what the sentence asserts of every one, that it is defined by the register, and nothing more; what follows from it is proved (`Symmetry.IsQubits.eq_of_content_eq`, `Solution.geometry_same`, `Space.slice_sphere`, `Space.sphere_rings`). The instances, each named where it is taken, and no others: the geometry of space at a slot is defined by the laws and the state there (`hgeom : IsQubits …` of `Solution.geometry_same`, `Solution.geometry_same_slots`, `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources` and `Solution.chain_from_sources`, and so of `Main.chain` and `Main.deSitter_horizon`); every geodesic of the space at an instant of an observer at rest is one along which a ring runs (`hclose` and `hslice`; `Symmetry.described`); the entropy of the cosmological horizon, its entropy per unit area times its area, is that of the state of the register's strings (`hhor`, `Sources.P1.horizon_entropy`, a `physical`, of `Main.jacobson_G_exact`, which derives the count's exact density from it); the entropy of a local horizon, the entropy of Clausius' relation, is its strings', the law `η` times its area (`hent`, `Sources.P1.local_entropy`, a `physical`, of `Main.jacobson_step`, with Sec. II's count, the density the same on every horizon by Postulate 2's clauses, `Symmetry.one_density`); the energy inside the horizon is the energy of its strings (`hin` of `Main.vacuum_share`, `energy_inside_strings`); a body's energy is held by the strings that carry its information (`hcar` of `Main.carriers_share` and `Main.galactic_law`, `energy_in_carriers`); the energy of light goes with the light, its current along the ray for every observer (`hlight` of `Main.light_rays`, a `physical` whose register side is the ray's direction only); a law is a function of the register, of the crossing of a ring with a cell (the type of `f` in `Symmetry.laws_same`, Sec. II's definition of a law, of which Postulate 2's clauses speak); a horizon's state is a state of its strings and of nothing else (the type of `Bridge.horizonEntropy`); and the number of strings a sphere holds about a body does not depend on the drawing, which is no part of the register, so it is the average over its rotations with a weighting that prefers none, the unit sphere's measure (`Center.lean`, `Weighting.lean`). The chain runs on closed forms that these places tie to the strings to a stated order: `Shor`, one bit for every ring, which the strings' entropy is to relative order `2/L` (`Bridge.horizon_entropy`), and with it `η` and `EΛ`; and `Ns`, which the average count is to relative order `2/L`. A horizon's entropy depends only on strings: its state is a distribution on the senses of the strings and of nothing else (`Bridge.horizonEntropy`); the strings being free between interactions, the state is the product of their sense distributions, each read off the thermal state of the string's tick (`hfree`, `hq` of `Bridge.horizon_entropy`), and the entropy adds over them (`Entropy.shannon_jointProb`), one bit each (`Bridge.horizon_entropy`). A law is a function of the register (`Symmetry.lawAt`), and space is determined by the rings, the set of geodesics along which some drawing runs a ring (`Symmetry.described`); from these and two clauses of Postulate 2 the symmetry of space follows (`Symmetry.lawAt_same`). The geometry of space at a slot is qubits: it is read off the laws and the state there (`hgeom`, as above, with the state `Solution.crossingState`), so without a mass it is the same at every slot (`Solution.geometry_same`). A ring runs along every geodesic of the space at an instant (`hclose` and `hslice` of `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources` and `Solution.chain_from_sources`, and so of `Main.chain` and `Main.deSitter_horizon`); from these, the last sentence of Postulate 2, by itself and with Sec. II's cell and its own first clause for the rings through the observer's cell drawn in space (`hdrawn`), and the cited theorems of geometry in the table, the cosmological constant follows (`Space.cosmological_constant_of_sources`). The count about a body depends only on the strings and not on the drawing; with no rotation of the drawing preferred, it is defined as the average over the rotations, which `Center.lean` and `Weighting.lean` compute. This is the definition the files take as given; the chain uses `Ns` for the average, to the order above. The vacuum's energy is the horizon's heat, its temperature (`I1`) times the entropy of the strings' thermal state (`Cosmo.EΛ`, `Cosmo.vacuum_energy`), so it rests on the thesis only through that state.

Four of the five identifications that versions of the paper up to v11.1.18 listed as readings of the postulates (its Table II), which the files carried as definitions, are derived; the fifth, the even spread of a string's bit inside a sphere, left the paper and the files with the elastic response it served (v18-53). The count identified with the horizon's area: every largest ring has half its cells inside any observer's horizon, `L/4` cells out, where the solution puts it (`Bridge.horizon_entropy_count`, `Main.horizon_quarter`), so it crosses the horizon (`Horizon.Positions.crossesHorizon`); the horizon counts every one of the register's `R` rings, since its entropy is that of its thermal state, a state of the strings alone (the thesis, above) and so of every string (`Bridge.horizonEntropy`, `Bridge.horizon_counts_every_ring`), and a ring's share of the area, `4ℓ_c²/π` (`shares`), is the solution's area over that count. One string on each ring: from the paper's definition of a cell, which holds one bit of each ring through it, so the bits on a ring's cells form one string of Postulate 1 (the horizon's state in `Bridge.horizon_entropy` is one sense for each ring); a string is one qubit; the two senses, the qubit's two outcomes, have the same energy, since the tick's phase changes alike in either sense, no law depending on the ring's direction (Postulate 2, `hsense` of `Qubit.Tick`), so that the thermal state weights them equally (`Qubit.Tick.prob_state`) and the string holds one bit (`one_bit`); on the string's own thermal state, half the identity since every state of a free string has one energy, the bit is `Main.string_one_bit`; `Bridge.horizon_entropy` sums the bits over the rings. The strings of a sphere that is not a horizon: counted ring by ring, a sphere of areal radius `r` holding, averaged over its position, the number of rings times `(r/R_Λ)²` (`Bridge.sphere_strings`, `Crossing.Drawing.meanStrings_eq`, `HatBox.hatBox`), on average one string for each ring's share of its area, the area law of a screen (Sec. II, "Counting in the bulk"; Sec. III takes the same density on every local horizon); the register's own counts, the same at every distance from the body (`L` cells, with `L/2` rings through each), do not count a sphere's strings, and the rings drawn as great circles do. The pairing of a joint measurement: one measurement reads one position on every string it measures, and the joint outcome a position shows is the strings' bits there (`Capacity.jointOutcome`, `Capacity.ceiling_in_step`), no function chosen. The count fixes the entropy per unit area, `η = πk_B ln 2/4ℓ_c²` (`eta_eq`), and with `S = ηA` the counts of every sphere in the bulk follow: `bulk_counts` gives Eq. (N), and `ratio_of_areas` shows that the number of strings held by a sphere of radius `r` is the horizon's number times the ratio of the sphere's area to the horizon's, `(L²/4)(r/R_Λ)²`; every one of the register's rings crosses every observer's horizon with half its cells inside. `quarter_any_count` shows that Bekenstein and Hawking's quarter follows from Jacobson's coefficient and the measured `G` for any count, and `cell_from_G` that what the count fixes is the cell, `ℓ_c = √(π ln 2) ℓ_P`.

## The solution of Einstein's equation

Jacobson's argument, with the first law, returns Einstein's equations (`Einstein.lean`). About a body at rest, alone, their solution is the Schwarzschild–de Sitter solution, `f(r) = 1 − 2GM/c²r − Λr²/3` [F. Kottler, Ann. Phys. (Leipzig) 361, 401 (1918)], which `Main.schwarzschild_deSitter` derives from the vacuum equations (`Schwarzschild.lean`); here its metric function is the definition `fSdS`. Its constant is that of the solution without a mass, `3/R_Λ²`, since the Clausius relation on the observer's cosmological horizon keeps `Λ` unchanged as the mass grows (`MassHorizon.lean`, `Main.lambda_with_mass`). Of general relativity the paper takes no law (its Sec. III): the cited mathematics of the table, the Misner–Sharp and Komar energies as definitions evaluated on the derived solution, and the perihelion and deflection formulas as numbers only, which `solar_tests` evaluates. The field is read from the solution in the classical weak-field way, `g = (c²/2) df/dr`. The register's rings are the largest rings, of `L` cells (Secs. II, IV.D); every one crosses an observer's horizon with half its cells inside (`Bridge.horizon_entropy_count`), the half that carries the entropy inside the horizon (Sec. IV.D), and the horizon's own entropy is the register's count, every ring counted (`Shor`). With a mass inside, the great circles of its horizons, of `L_M = L − 2α/ln 2` cells at the cosmological horizon and `4πGM/c²ℓ_c` at a black hole's, are circles shorter than a largest ring, outside the count of Postulate 2. The area law, one bit per ring's share of area, `Bridge.sphere_strings` derives for the solution without a mass; with a mass the files do not apply it again, where the paper's Sec. IV.C does, and reach its number another way: the entropy density is one law, the same on every local horizon (`Symmetry.one_density`, Postulate 2), the count's `η`, to which Jacobson's step ties `G` (`hJ`), so the horizon the mass draws in, at the radius the solution gives, holds `η` times its area, `L_M²/4` bits to first order in the mass, the number Eqs. (counts) and (S) would give with `L_M` in place of `L` (`Symmetry.massive_horizon_density`); a sphere's shortfall in area about the mass, `8πGMr/c²` to first order (its rate per unit of geodesic distance, `Galactic.area_deficit`, summed, `Galactic.shortfall_sum`), is `η` times it in entropy, `k_B S_M(r)` (`Main.entropy_removed_sphere`); and a black hole's horizon holds `L'²/4` bits with `L' = 4πGM/c²ℓ_c` (`Symmetry.black_hole_density`). The definitions `Ns` and `ringShare` at those areas read the same numbers (`Galactic.massive_horizon_count`, `removed_rings`, `shortfall_removes`, `Cosmo.black_hole_quarter`), and the loss is `Galactic.entropy_removed`. The files derive from the solution what the chain uses (next paragraph), and the closed spacelike geodesics of de Sitter space (`Geodesics.lean`); the Deser–Levin temperature enters as a hypothesis (`hDL`); the limit up to which the solution has a horizon is evaluated as a number (`Numerics.cosmic_mass_beyond_nariai`). That the solution without a mass has `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` is derived from its space at an instant (`Space.lean`). That space has the same geometry at every place and in every direction, which the symmetry of space (`Symmetry.lawAt_same`), the state the same at every crossing without a mass (`Solution.state_same`) and the thesis (`hgeom`) give (`Solution.geometry_same`), and a ring along every geodesic, so by the Killing–Hopf theorem it is a space form, with `3K = Λ` by Einstein's constraint; its rings make it a quotient of the three-sphere; and its rings, of one length, with the rings through the observer's cell drawn in it, leave no quotient: it is the three-sphere itself (`Space.sphere_rings`). No completeness of the spacetime is assumed. That `f = 1 − Λr²/3` is the static form of the same de Sitter space, with the observer's horizon at its zero, is taken as known with the solution.

The field is `g = GM/r² − Λc²r/3` (`gSdS_eq`). The field the mass adds to the solution without it, at the same `Λ`, its pull, is Newton's law, `GM/r²` at every `Λ` (`pull`, `pull_eq`); the rest is the push. With `I1` the pull gives Padmanabhan's equipartition, `Mc² = 2N_s(r) k_BT_a ln 2`, two Landauer energies per string (`newton`, `equipartition_in_cells`), where `Mc²` is the Komar energy the mass adds (`komar_mass`). Without the mass the solution has its horizon at `R_Λ` exactly when `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` (`deSitter`). `Λ` is the constant of the vacuum equations, and this condition, the hypothesis `hdS` of the theorems that use `Λ`, fixes it; Sec. IV.D derives it from the rings (`Space.cosmological_constant_of_sources`): the space at an instant is the three-sphere itself, and a largest ring is one of its great circles, all of length `2π√(3/Λ)`. The condition gives Eq. (energy): with `hdS` the field of the solution without a mass is `−c²/R_Λ` at the horizon, so the horizon's surface gravity `a_Λ = c²/R_Λ` is derived; the period of a horizon of that surface gravity, `2πc/a_Λ`, is `L` ticks, the lap time `Lt_c` of the largest ring (`horizon_period`), and with `I1` its temperature is Unruh's at `a_Λ`, which is `E_c/L = ℏc/2πR_Λ`, the Gibbons–Hawking temperature (`horizon_temperature`). The proper distance from the observer to that horizon is `πR_Λ/2 = Lℓ_c/4`, a quarter of a largest ring (`proper_distance`). With the mass, the outer horizon lies within `2m²/R_Λ` of `R_Λ − m`, with `m = GM/c²`, and there is no horizon farther out (`sds_outer_horizon`). A sphere about the mass gains area with geodesic distance more slowly than a sphere of the same area in the solution without the mass, at the same `Λ`, by a rate between `8πGM/c²` and `8πGM/c²√f` (`area_deficit`; at `Λ = 0` the comparison is with flat space). The metric function is concave in the radius, so between two radii it is at least its smaller end value, `f_min` (`fSdS_min_le`). Summed from the body's surface `b` out to `r` (`shortfall_sum`), the shortfall lies between `8πGM(r − b)/c²` and that over `√f_min`, and at one bit per ring (`removed_rings`) the entropy it removes lies between `S_M(r − b)` and `S_M(r − b)/√f_min`: to first order, far outside the body, it is `S_M(r) = 2πnα` (`shortfall_removes`; `Main.entropy_removed_sphere`, which takes the areal radius along a radial geodesic, `dρ/ds = √f`), the fraction `2GM/c²r` of the strings' entropy `N_s(r) ln 2`, so a mass changes the count of Eq. (N), that of the solution without a mass, only by that fraction (`removed_fraction`). The galactic law (Sec. IV.C, `Carriers.lean`): the body's energy is held by the strings that carry its information, two Landauer energies each at the sphere's temperature, against Newton's equal share at the Unruh temperature of `g_N`, so `a = g_N/b` with `b` the carrier fraction (`share_acceleration`); the strings' mean energy is `k_BT_dS`, the mean of the Gibbs distribution at the horizon's temperature (`string_energy`), the matter in it `(1 − ln 2)k_BT_dS`, and the body's energy per string in that unit is `f = g_N/a₀` with `a₀ = (1 − ln 2)c²/(2 ln 2 R_Λ)` (`fill_fraction`); the threshold of a string is exponential with mean one qubit, so the carriers are the fraction `1 − e^{−√f}` and `a = g_N/(1 − e^{−√(g_N/a₀)})` (`law`, chained from its inputs in `radial_acceleration_relation`); near the body Newton's law returns exponentially (`Law.near_limit`), far from it `a = √(a₀g_N) + g_N/2 + O(a₀h³)` (`Law.deep_limit`) and `v⁴ = a₀GM` on circular orbits (`Law.tully_fisher`); a classical share would give `a = a₀` and rising speeds (`Law.classical_limit`); a uniform external field adds nothing, the sphere's temperature being that of a proper acceleration (Sec. IV.C, the frame of the law, `Main.frame_of_law`: a field leaves a proper acceleration as it is exactly where it is a common acceleration of the body and of free fall at the observer's place, and under such a field the acceleration, the temperature, the share and Eq. (law) stand as without it; the two-body law is not derived, and the quadrupole the other reading would give is `scripts/cassini.py`, with `Numerics.cassini_total_field`); `a₀` is a fixed fraction of the Planck acceleration (`scale_over_time`), and `Λ = 12(ln 2)²a₀²/((1 − ln 2)²c⁴)` (`Lambda_from_a0`). The field with the boundary condition is the pull less the push (`push`); the zero-gravity radius lies in the deep regime for any mass below `((1 − ln 2)/2 ln 2)³c²R_Λ/G` (`zero_gravity`, `zero_gravity_mass`). At the horizon the Misner–Sharp energy is `E_Λ` and the Komar energy is `−2E_Λ` (`komar_misner_sharp`). The drift of `G` that a Hubble-radius horizon would give, `Ġ = 2H(1 + q)G`, is computed by differentiation (`G_drift`); with `L` fixed, `GΛ` is constant and `Λ̇/Λ = −Ġ/G` (`constancy_of_Lambda`); and the fluid equation, `ρ̇ = −3H(1 + w)ρ`, follows from the first law (`fluid_equation`). With the ring's density `3π²ρ_c ln 2/2L²` constant, as `L`, the cell and the bit per ring are, it gives `w = −1` exactly (`w_exact`, which writes the first law for that fixed density). The constancy itself is derived for a register `Rt s` taken at each time `s`, along Secs. IV.D and VI: `L` and the cell are the constants of Postulate 1, one number and one length at every time (`hL`, `hℓc`), and `c`, `ℏ` and `k_B` are the same at every time (`hconst`); then the horizon's radius `R_Λ = Lℓ_c/2π` is constant (`horizon_constant`), so `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` is constant where the horizon is at `R_Λ` (`Lambda_constant`, with `hdS`: the constancy of `Λ` that Einstein's equations assert is here derived), `G` and `ρ_Λ` are constant (`G_constant`, `rhoΛ_constant`), `GΛ` is constant by `L` alone (`GLambda_constant`, the `hK` of `constancy_of_Lambda`), and the first law written for `ρ_Λ(s)` gives `w = −1` (`w_exact_in_time`) and `Λ̇/Λ = −Ġ/G` (`Lambda_rate_in_time`).

Some hypotheses are not inputs. `hdS`, that the horizon of the solution without a mass is at `R_Λ`, follows from `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` (`deSitter`), which `Space.cosmological_constant_of_sources` derives from the sources (Sec. IV.D): the space at an instant is the three-sphere itself, all of whose great circles have the length `2π√(3/Λ)`, so `Lℓ_c = 2π√(3/Λ)`. The theorems that use `Λ` keep `hdS` as their hypothesis for brevity; `Solution.chain_from_sources` derives it, with Jacobson's step `hJ` and the form of `I1` for the lap, from the sources, and applies them. One ties the chain's `L` to the register's: `R.L = L` (`hRL`). One side condition is the count's: `L ≥ 1` (`NeZero L` where a ring's length enters a bound), `L ≥ 4` where the count itself enters (`hL` of `vacuum_share`, `Bridge.counts_exact`), which `Horizon.four_le` derives from Postulate 2's fourth clause in the paper's words, a field of the register (`misses`): the results of `Main.lean` that use `L ≥ 4` derive it from the register. One is derived elsewhere and taken as a hypothesis for brevity: the constancy of `GΛ` (`hK` of `constancy_of_Lambda`, from `G_Lambda` with `L` fixed, composed in `Lambda_rate_in_time`). Some results derived elsewhere are built into a definition or into a hypothesis's form instead. The horizon at `R_Λ` (`hdS`) is built into `Ahor = 4πR_Λ²`, and so into `η`, `Ns` and `ringShare`, which most theorems that take `hJ` use without taking `hdS`; it is also built into `I1`'s form for the cosmological horizon, `k_BT = ℏ/(Lt_c)`, whose period is the lap time (`DeSitter.horizon_period`; `Solution.chain_from_sources` derives that form from the Gibbons–Hawking one). The rings as great circles of the three-sphere of radius `R_Λ` are built into the drawing `D` of `Bridge.sphere_strings`, `Center.sphere_strings_centered`, `Center.sphere_strings_centered_of`, which `Crossing.Drawing.ofRings` builds from the rings as closed geodesics in their `_of_rings` versions. The dimension four of the space that holds the three-sphere (`hE : finrank ℝ E = 4` of `Bridge.sphere_strings` and of the theorems on the rings as closed geodesics) is the three dimensions of space, which the paper takes as observed (Sec. IV.D). A few theorems take the condition that defines the quantity they treat: the entropy density of the cosmological horizon, its entropy over its area, the count's `η`, to leading order in `1/L` (in `Main.lean` the step `Sources.Approx.count_density`; `Main.jacobson_G_exact` derives its exact form from the horizon's entropy, `Sources.P1.horizon_entropy` and `Bridge.horizon_entropy`); the proper distance, with `s(0) = 0` and `ds/dr = 1/√f` (`hs0`, `hds` and `hcont` of `proper_distance`, `Sources.Condition.radial_distance` of `Main.horizon_quarter`; `proper_distance_exists` shows that `R_Λ arcsin(r/R_Λ)` meets it); the shortfall of a sphere's area, by its rate per unit of geodesic distance, and the areal radius along a radial geodesic (`hD` and `hρ` of `shortfall_removes`; `Sources.Condition.shortfall`, `Sources.Condition.areal_radius`); the vanishing of the field at `r_z` (`hz` of `zero_gravity`; `h3` of `zero_gravity_mass` is the radius it gives, `r_z³ = GMR_Λ²/c²`); a state in which every joint outcome of the other qubits occurs, with the last qubit's bit given it at a probability strictly between 0 and 1, a random state or a product state of superposed qubits (`hrandom` of `ceiling`, `hall` of `ceiling_by_outcome` and `ceiling_in_step`), or `2^N` blocks of a physical string (`hall` of `ceiling_logical`), or, for logical qubits, a random logical state held by the register (`Coding.ceiling_both_cases`: `hp`, `hboth`, `hdist`, `hshare`; `Coding.ceiling_line_code`: `hp`, `hg`, `hdist`, `hshare`, with `hm`, the property of the code that `Coding.surfaceCode_match` proves for the surface code of every distance), with the `L` cells of a string (`hL` of the ceilings) and, for logical qubits, the `2^(N−1)` joint outcomes of the others (`hK`); and the observed bound on `|ρ̇/ρ|` (`hb` of `w_bound`), and the observed ranges of `H_0` and `Ω_Λ` (`hH1`, `hH2`, `hΩ1` and `hΩ2` of `Numerics.log2_planck_band`). `Bridge.sphere_strings_deSitter` takes the largest rings as closed geodesics of the space at an instant of de Sitter space: the last sentence of Postulate 2 in the space of the solution without a mass, the Schwarzschild–de Sitter solution at `M = 0`; the theorem takes the rings in the quadric `form X X = 3/Λ` and derives `Λ > 0` and `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` from their length, the value that `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources` proves from the sources. It returns the count averaged over the sphere's position, with the center weighted by the measure that a Haar measure gives the three-sphere; the count about a given body is defined as the average over the rotations of the drawing, which is the same number (`Center.sphere_strings_of_rings_centered`). `temperature_led` takes as its premise the alternative that Sec. IV.C rejects, that gravity follows the excess of the Deser–Levin temperature over the floor, and derives the coefficient that alternative would give.

## What was checked, and what it found

Every relation of the chain holds exactly as the paper writes it, and every number it computes is certified: each theorem gives an interval every point of which rounds to the value the paper quotes. Observational values and their uncertainties are inputs, not claims. Checking the draft of v11.2 found one slip. Appendix A gave `log₂ L_cos = 204.77`, which is the logarithm of `L_cos` after rounding it to `4.37×10⁶¹`. The logarithm of `L_cos` itself is `204.7648`, and `log2_Lcos` certifies `204.755 < log₂ L_cos < 204.765`, so the paper says `204.76`.

The ceiling theorems are stated for `L_cos`, the de Sitter horizon's ring length, which is the register's (Sec. IV.A); the register's rings are the largest rings whatever mass a horizon holds, and a mass at rest inside a horizon shortens the closed geodesics at its throat, `L_M = L − 2α/ln 2`, not the register's rings. The count of Sec. II is that of the horizon of the solution without a mass: the matter of a ball of radius `R_Λ` at today's mean density has `GM/c²R_Λ = Ω_m/2Ω_Λ = 0.23`, beyond the Nariai limit `1/3√3 = 0.19` up to which the solution has a horizon, a property of the solution taken as known and not derived here (`cosmic_mass_beyond_nariai` certifies the two numbers; `sds_outer_horizon` uses the stricter sufficient condition `13GM/c² < R_Λ`), so it is not a mass at rest inside a static horizon and Eq. (SM) does not apply to it.

Since v11.1, the formalization follows the paper's move to one bit per ring: the entropy is `(L²/4) ln 2`, `ln 2` is a field of `Reg`, and every formula that carries it (`η`, `G`, `ℓ_c`, the shift of the horizon, the crossing, `V_0`, the slope of `V_M`, the apparent mass, `GΛ`, `E_Λ` and `ρ_Λ`) is derived again and its numbers certified again. `π` and `ln 2` are now Mathlib's, with Mathlib's own bounds, where v11.1 took the bounds on `π` as a hypothesis, and the projective plane uses Mathlib's `Combinatorics.Configuration` in place of an extract of it. New in v11.2 are the bridges that join the count to the chain (the exact counts and Eq. (volume)), the shortfall summed into the entropy a mass removes, the paper's construction of a ring that misses a cell, the evenness of `L` from the opposite lying halfway around, the exact count behind the fraction `4n/L` and the count at each distance, the capacity count and its soft form, Palmer's bit count for every `N`, the proper distance to the horizon, the outer horizon with a mass, the deep-regime bound, the temperature floor with Deser–Levin, the Misner–Sharp and Komar energies, the coefficient of Einstein's equation, one bit as the entropy of a doublet, the ratio of the Planck density to `ρ_Λ`, `Λ̇/Λ = −Ġ/G`, and the numbers that go with them. The zero-velocity radii of v11.1 (111 pc and 1.6 Mpc) are gone, as the paper no longer quotes them, and so are the checks of two v11 misprints that v11.1 corrected.

The certified numbers are these. The cell and its units: `ℓ_P = 1.616×10⁻³⁵ m`, `ℓ_c = √(π ln 2) ℓ_P = 2.385×10⁻³⁵ m`, `t_c = 7.96×10⁻⁴⁴ s`, `m_c = 1.475×10⁻⁸ kg`, `E_c = 1.326×10⁹ J`, `a_c = 3.77×10⁵¹ m s⁻²`. The horizon: `H_∞ = 1.81×10⁻¹⁸ s⁻¹`, `Λ = 1.09×10⁻⁵² m⁻²`, `R_Λ = 1.66×10²⁶ m`, `c²/R_Λ = 5.4×10⁻¹⁰ m s⁻²`, `L_cos = 4.37×10⁶¹` with `log₂ L_cos = 204.76`, the ceiling `2²⁰⁴ < L_cos < 2²⁰⁵` so that `N_max = 204`, and `2^(N+1) ≤ L_cos` exactly for `N ≤ 203`, the ceiling for logical qubits in the surface code (`logical_ceiling_cos`), `2²⁰⁴ = 2.57×10⁶¹` and `2²⁰⁵ = 5.14×10⁶¹`, at `0.59` and `1.18` of `L_cos` (`ceiling_margins`), with `log₂ L = 204.65` at the local `H₀ = 73 km/s/Mpc` (`log2_L73`) and between `204.745` and `204.785` across Planck's uncertainties in `H₀` and `Ω_Λ`, a shift below `0.02` over the band and above `0.015` at its corners (`log2_planck_band`; the paper's "about ±0.02"), so the ceiling is `204` either way, Palmer's bit count holding exactly for `N ≤ 211`, Palmer's range of `L` giving ceilings of 212 and 362, `L²/2 = 9.5×10¹²²` cells, `L²/4 = 4.8×10¹²²` rings, the entropy `(L²/4) ln 2 = πR_Λ²/ℓ_P² = 3.3×10¹²²`, `405 < log₂ 10¹²² < 405.5` (the `N ≈ 400` Palmer quotes from Davies) against `2 log₂ L − 2 = 407.5`, the soft ceilings `202`, `200` and `199` for blocks of `10`, `30` and `100` cells (`soft_ceilings`), the average block of `3.4` cells at `N = 204` (`average_block`), the missing fraction `0.15` of the joint outcomes at `N = 205` and `0.58` at `206` (`missing_fraction`), the return bound above the ceiling under the Porter–Thomas distribution, the lightest `15%` of the outcomes carrying `1.2%` and the lightest `58%` carrying `21%`, so the overlap with the random state at most `0.988` at `205` qubits, `0.79` at `206`, `0.54` at `207` and `0.12` at `210` (`porter_thomas_bound`), the bracket `G(2²⁰⁴) = 1.93×10⁻¹⁰` and `G(2²⁰⁵) = 4.82×10⁻¹¹` with the measured `G` inside it and a factor of four across it (and, since `G` falls as `L` grows, `Gof_antitone`, every ceiling of 205 or more has its bracket below the measured `G`, `ceiling_above_204`), `k_BT_dS = E_c/L = 3.03×10⁻⁵³ J`, and `τ* = 3.5×10¹⁸ s = 1.1×10¹¹ yr`. The solar scale: `α☉ = 1.35×10³⁸`, the shift `α☉/(π ln 2) = 6.19×10³⁷` cells, `Lα☉ = 5.9×10⁹⁹`, the Galaxy's mass `2.0×10⁴² kg`, its `α = 1.35×10⁵⁰` and `2α/ln 2 = 3.9×10⁵⁰`, one part in `10¹¹` of `L_cos`, against `GM/c²R_Λ = 0.23` for the matter inside a ball of radius `R_Λ` today, beyond the Nariai limit `0.19` (`cosmic_mass_beyond_nariai`), `n = 6.27×10⁴⁵` and `5.93×10⁻³ m s⁻²` at 1 au, the push `5×10⁻²⁵ m s⁻²` there (certified between `4.85×10⁻²⁵` and `4.95×10⁻²⁵`), the deep regime beginning `7031 au` from the Sun, where `√(GM☉/a₀) = 1.05×10¹⁵ m` (`deep_radii`), `h = √(g_N/a₀) = 7031` at Earth's orbit and `233.8` at Neptune's, where `g_N = 6.56×10⁻⁶ m s⁻²` (`solar_system`), the departure from Newton's law at Neptune `e^{−h} = 2.9×10⁻¹⁰²`, `3×10⁻¹⁰²` of `g_N` (`neptune_departure`), Mercury's `42.98″` per century, and the `1.75″` deflection. The galactic scale: `a₀ = (1 − ln 2)c²/(2 ln 2 R_Λ) = 1.1996×10⁻¹⁰ m s⁻²`, `1.200` to the precision quoted, with the coefficient `(1 − ln 2)/2 ln 2 = 0.2213` (`a0_val`), its uncertainty `±0.011×10⁻¹⁰`, `0.9%`, from Planck's `H₀` and `Ω_Λ` (`a0_uncertainty`), `0.0σ` from McGaugh's `1.20` and `0.1σ` from Desmond's `1.19` with the total uncertainties `0.24` and `0.10` (`a0_sigmas`), `1.30×10⁻¹⁰` at the local `H₀ = 73`, `0.4σ` and `1.1σ` (`a0_local_H0`), the threshold's mean `1.00 ± 0.10` read from the data (`threshold_mean_from_data`), the matter in a string's mean energy, `u = (1 − ln 2)k_BT_dS = 9.31×10⁻⁵⁴ J` with `1 − ln 2 = 0.307` (`string_matter`), the vacuum share `ln 2 = 0.6931` at `1.2σ` above Planck's `0.685 ± 0.007` and `1.1σ` below DESI's `0.7025 ± 0.0086`, with `ln 2/Ω_Λ = 1.012` (`vacuum_share`), the Tully–Fisher speeds `112 km/s` at `10¹⁰ M☉` and `200 km/s` at `10¹¹ M☉` (`tully_fisher_speeds`), the deep regime from `10.8 kpc` for `10¹¹ M☉` (`deep_radii`), the law `a/g_N = 1.04, 1.58, 3.69, 10.5` at `g_N/a₀ = 10, 1, 0.1, 0.01`, `4%` and `58%` above Newton at `10a₀` and `a₀` (`rar_table`), the quadrupole the total-field reading would give, `q₂ = 0.348` carried in from `scripts/cassini.py` so `Q₂ = 4.0×10⁻²⁶ s⁻²`, twelve standard deviations from Cassini's `(3 ± 3)×10⁻²⁷` (`cassini_total_field`), the Galaxy's tide `g_e/R₀ = 7.5×10⁻³¹ s⁻²` (`galactic_tide`), a test body's excess `1.67` at `g_N = 10⁻¹⁰ m/s²` (`test_body_excess`), and the temperature-led `2c²/R_Λ` at nine times `a₀` with its transition 38% above Newton where the observed relation is 4% above (`temperature_led_numbers`). The cosmological scale: `E_Λ = 1.00×10⁷⁰ J`, `ρ_Λ = 5.25×10⁻¹⁰ J m⁻³`, `GΛ = 7.28×10⁻⁶³`, the Planck density of order `10¹¹³ J m⁻³`, 123 orders above `ρ_Λ` (`vacuum_orders`), the zero-gravity radius in the deep regime for any galaxy, group or cluster (`zero_gravity_masses`), `H(1)/H₀ = 1.8` (certified between `1.785` and `1.795`), `H₀ = 6.89×10⁻¹¹ yr⁻¹`, `|1 + w₀| ≲ 4.8×10⁻⁴` for the drift of the `Λ` term of the expansion rate (`9.7×10⁻⁴` referred to the density `Λc⁴/8πG`), and the drift `Ġ/G ≈ 6.5×10⁻¹¹ yr⁻¹` at `q = Ω_m/2 − Ω_Λ = −0.53`, about 650 times the lunar-laser-ranging bound. The qubit ceiling across the entropy per ring: with `s` bits per ring, `L = L_cos/√s`, and the ceiling is `204` exactly for `0.7218 < s ≤ 2.887` (`ceiling_entropy_range`), so one bit, one nat and two bits all give `204`. The energy inside `R_Λ` at the measured `Ω_Λ`, `E_Λ/Ω_Λ = 1.46×10⁷⁰ J`, of which matter `0.46×10⁷⁰ J` (`energy_today`). The bath's scatterings in the bit's history (Sec. II, Appendix A): `ln(L_cos²/4) ≈ 282`, between `281.5` and `282.5`, and the `3.5×10⁻¹⁵ s` that `282` scattering times `ℏ/α²k_BT` take at `1 MeV`, with `α = 1/137.036` (`lnN_val`, `scatter_1MeV`). The files also certify figures that the paper does not print: `2²¹² = 6.58×10⁶³`, `211L = 9.22×10⁶³`, `2²¹³ = 1.32×10⁶⁴` and `212L = 9.26×10⁶³` behind Palmer's `211`; `5.9×10¹¹⁰` for `10¹¹ M☉`; `8.9×10⁻¹²` behind "one part in `10¹¹`"; Mercury's `5.0×10⁻⁷ rad` per orbit and, kept from earlier versions of the paper, the phase grid `1.4×10⁻⁶¹ rad`, fifty-four orders of magnitude below it (`phase_grid`); the Planck density `4.6×10¹¹³ J m⁻³`, `(2 ln 2/3) L² = 8.8×10¹²²` times `ρ_Λ`, with `L² = 1.9×10¹²³`; and the largest mass whose zero-gravity radius lies in the deep regime, `1.2×10²¹ M☉`.

Since v11.2.10, following the review of v12 of the paper: `horizon_radius` takes the closed geodesics of the de Sitter solution as its hypothesis (`hgeo`) in place of `hrest` and `P1`, which had presented as derived what the files took as hypotheses; `I1` is stated as the Kubo–Martin–Schwinger period, `2πc/a`, with `L_a` its length in ticks rather than a ring; and `temperature_is_quantum_over_two_pi` and `tully_fisher_speed` are removed with the sentences they checked, so the ledger holds 166 theorems. The docstrings follow the paper's wording of the readings of the postulates, one string on each ring (`Shor`), the area law of a screen (`Ns`) and the even spread of a string's bit (`Sin`), and of Postulate 1's shift; `aM_val` also certifies Verlinde's coefficient with his `cH₀`, `1.1×10⁻¹⁰`, and that with it the observed `a₀` would exceed the bound, and `aM_vs_observation` the 84% of the bound that the second measurement reaches. After the second review of the paper (29 September), the docstrings follow its wording again: a body occupies cells of the sphere and the counts are made from the center of a sphere drawn about it (since -6-25 the paper treats the body as a point at one cell); the joint measurement of Postulate 1 pairs the bits of the strings and needs no shared cell; and the photon sphere of a mass joined the classical results taken as known (dropped again after the third review, with the check it served; the throat circles of a horizon with a mass inside are then the shorter rings Postulate 1 allows, outside Postulate 2's count, and "every ring is a largest ring" goes). No theorem changed. After the third review (29 September) the dimension check of Sec. II was withdrawn with the drawing it rested on, so `Dimension.lean` and its three ledger lines are removed (166 → 163 theorems); the count identified with the horizon's area is named as a fourth reading, `Ahor` and `ringShare`, and the docstrings no longer speak of a sphere on which the register is drawn.

After the fourth review (30 September, v11.2.11-10, released as v13): the ceiling is stated for every state in which all joint outcomes occur, product states of superposed qubits included, with the paper's blocks by joint outcome (`ceiling_by_outcome`, new; the header of `Capacity.lean` and the docstring of `product_state` say which grouping each theorem uses, and the header no longer says that outcomes leaving the qubit in one state share a block); `G_mul_eta`'s docstring follows Sec. III.C ("Without cells there is no count, and `η` stays free") in place of "A continuum does not gravitate"; the softness of the ceiling below `204`, the average block at `204` and the missing outcomes above it are certified (`soft_ceilings`, `average_block`, `missing_fraction`, new); and which horizon the count describes is settled in the numbers (`cosmic_mass_beyond_nariai`, new), the `nmax_cos` docstring and this file no longer saying that a mass shortens the register's rings. The ledger holds 168 theorems.

After the fifth review (30 September, v11.2.11-11): the elastic response is formalized as the paper now states it. `differentiate_equality`, which differentiated Verlinde's equality assumed at every radius, is removed; `saturated_no_equality` (new) shows that with the response absent inside the crossing the equality fails at every radius up to it; `mean_tendsto` (new, the mean of a function that tends to a limit tends to the same limit) and `deep_regime_equality` (new) derive `v_c⁴ = a_M GM` from the equality reached in the limit alone, and the pointwise hypothesis of `elastic`, `apparent_mass` and `tully_fisher` is renamed `hsat`, the bound reached at that radius. `SinGen` and `Sin_general` (new) give Eq. (volume) in its general form, by ring distance and area, and check that it returns the horizon's entropy at the horizon. In the numbers, `ceiling_entropy_range` (new) certifies that the ceiling is `204` for any entropy per ring from `0.7218` to `2.887` bits, `rar_transition` (new) the crossing `1.73×10⁻¹⁰ m s⁻² = 1.44a₀` and the fitted radial acceleration relation's `0.43` above Newton's law there, and `constancy_numbers` the doubled `9.7×10⁻⁴` when the drift is referred to `ρ_Λ`. The README names the version the paper names, v13, and says that only the Fano model is exhibited. The ledger holds 173 theorems.

After the second-round report (1 October, v11.2.11-12): `rar_transition` certifies the crossing as `1.725×10⁻¹⁰ < c²/πR_Λ < 1.726×10⁻¹⁰ m s⁻²`, every point of which rounds to the paper's `1.73` (the earlier interval `1.72` to `1.73` did not meet that standard); no other change, 173 theorems.

After the review of the package (1 October, v11.2.11-13): `black_hole_quarter` (new, `Cosmo.lean`) certifies the one relation of the paper that had no theorem, Sec. IV.D's remark that a black hole's horizon holds `(L'²/4) ln 2 = πr_s²/ℓ_P² = A/4ℓ_P²` with `L' = 4πGM/c²ℓ_c`; this README says that the symmetry of space enters through definitions and the solution, not as a hypothesis, and that the Nariai limit is taken as known. The ledger holds 174 theorems.

After the author's review of the paper's Table II (1 October, v11.2.11-14): the five readings of the postulates are no longer definitions but derived, and the symmetry of space is a named hypothesis. `Symmetry.lean` (new) derives the strings of a sphere from the symmetry of space, `hsym`, with the geometry of the three-sphere taken as known: `tube_volume`, `tube_fraction` (the fraction of the three-sphere within angular distance `θ` of a great circle is `sin²θ`), `strings_from_symmetry` (Eq. (N), the area law of a screen), `density_uniform` (the same density on every sphere) and `tube_model` (the hypotheses can all be met). `Bridge.horizon_entropy_count` (new) counts every ring on every horizon, so the horizon's entropy is the register's count and a ring's share of its area a quotient; `Bridge.Strings` (new) states Postulate 1's one string on each ring; `Reg.entropy_per_ring` (new) gives one bit of entropy per ring. `Capacity.cells_read_alike` (new) derives Sec. IV.A's uniform reading of the cells from the exchange of a `1` and a `−1`, which is what spreads a string's bit evenly over its cells; `Capacity.jointOutcome` and `Capacity.ceiling_in_step` (new) give the ceiling with the pairing the strings supply, the bits of the earlier strings at each position, in place of a function chosen. The docstrings of `Chain.lean`, `Galactic.lean`, `Horizon.lean` and `Capacity.lean` follow. No theorem of v11.2.11-13 changed. The ledger holds 183 theorems.

After a critical pass over the derivations of v11.2.11-14 (1 October, v11.2.11-15): the docstrings of the symmetry hypothesis `hsym` (`Symmetry.lean`, the `Chain.lean` header and this file) give the argument the paper's -6-7 gives: no direction is preferred at any point, and rotations about one point after another carry any point to any other, so no point is preferred either; the earlier wording drew "no point preferred" from Postulate 2's "no cell preferred", which is a statement about the register, not about places. No theorem changed; 183 theorems.

After one string per ring was taken as the case the paper treats (1 October, v11.2.11-16): `Bridge.Strings`, `Shor`, the headers of `Bridge.lean` and `Chain.lean` and this file state one string on each ring as the case the paper treats, as the paper's -6-9 does, and no longer as Postulate 1, which puts every qubit's string on a ring but neither a string on every ring nor at most one; why every ring carries one belongs to the universe's thermal history, which the paper does not treat. No theorem changed; 183 theorems.

After the definition of a cell was found to give one string on each ring (1 October, v11.2.11-17): the paper's -6-10 defines a cell as a place on a ring holding one bit of each ring through it, so Postulate 1 puts exactly one string on each ring, and adds the bit's history and the energy check of the current epoch to Sec. II. `Bridge.Strings`, `Shor`, the headers of `Bridge.lean` and `Chain.lean` and this file follow, and the five former readings are again all derived. `Numerics.lnN_val`, `Numerics.relax_1MeV` and `Numerics.bit_per_string` (new) certify the numbers of the new paragraph, with the new inputs `1 MeV`, Planck's `±0.007` on `Ω_Λ` and DESI's `Ω_m`. The ledger holds 186 theorems.

After the author's question whether registers exist near `L_cos` (2 October, v11.2.11-18): the paper's -6-13 completes the argument of Sec. II, every plane of order `L/2 − 1` giving a register, a plane existing for every prime-power order, and primes lying close together (Dusart, cited). `Horizon.double`, `Horizon.doublePositions` and `Horizon.register_of_plane` (new, `Models.lean`) prove the first for every finite projective plane, the Fano model being the case `q = 2`; `Planes.lean` (new, which imports Mathlib's Galois fields and the cardinality of projective spaces) proves the second (`fieldPlane_order`, `register_of_prime_power`); `Numerics.prime_gap_val` (new) evaluated the width of Dusart's interval at `L_cos/2`. The ledger held 190 theorems.

After the author's review of -6-13 (2 October, v11.2.11-19): the paper's -6-14 states the doubling for any order in one clause, "every plane of order `k` gives a register of `L = 2(k + 1)`, each point doubled into a cell and its opposite", cites Veblen and Bussey and Dusart without numbers, and leaves the cyclic order of the doubled rings and the Fano case to these files. `Numerics.prime_gap_val` is removed with the number it certified, and the headers of `Models.lean` and `Planes.lean` and this file quote the new sentence. The ledger holds 189 theorems.

After the author's question how Palmer's Bloch sphere describes a state of several qubits (2 October, v11.2.11-20): the paper's -6-15 gives each block of a later string the phase Palmer's state gives it, a cyclic permutation of its bits in a reference arrangement, and says that the reference arrangement may be moved only by permutations applied to all strings that leave every string where it is on its ring. The count uses only the fractions on blocks, so no theorem changes; the docstring of `Capacity.cells_read_alike` and this file quote the new wording. The ledger holds 189 theorems.
