Released as v14 (2 October): the tree of v11.2.11-20 under a new tag, since tag v13 is the 168-theorem tree (v11.2.11-10) that version 13 of the paper (Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23073991) cites. 189 theorems.

After a referee's report on the paper's -6-20 (3 October, v11.2.11-21): the paper's -6-21 describes the strings of a sphere at one point only, a cell `P` of the sphere, and its count does not say where a ring runs in the manifold. An observer's horizon is the `L` cells that lie `L/4` steps from his cell (`⌊L/4⌋` steps since -6-25). They share the area `L²ℓ_c²/π`, and by the symmetry of space no part of the horizon is preferred, so each cell takes `Lℓ_c²/π` of area; with `L/2` rings through a cell, the strings that cross the sphere at `P` number `π/2ℓ_c²` per unit of area, and a sphere holds one string for each `4ℓ_c²/π` of its area, Eq. (N) as before. `Symmetry.lean` is rewritten on that argument: `cellArea`, `cellArea_eq`, `crossingDensity` and `crossingDensity_eq` are new, the hypothesis `hsym` of `strings_from_symmetry` and `density_uniform` is now the crossings per unit of area, `htwice` is new (a string that enters a sphere leaves it), and `symmetry_model` shows that both can be met. The derivation that placed the rings as great circles of the three-sphere is gone, with its theorems `tube_volume`, `tube_fraction` and `tube_model`, its definitions `torusArea` and `tubeVol`, and its hypotheses `hV0`, `hcont` and `hV`. `Bridge.horizon_cells` (new) joins the count to the register: the cells `L / 4` steps from a cell, a division of whole numbers, number `L`, with `L/2` rings through each. No other theorem changes, and `Symmetry.lean` no longer imports Mathlib's mean-value and trigonometric-derivative files. The ledger holds 190 theorems. The paper's -6-21 also cuts the clause that a second string on a ring would need a second bit on each of its cells, since the definition of a cell, one bit of each ring through it, already gives one string on each ring; the docstrings of `Bridge.Strings` and of `Chain.lean`'s readings and the README follow.

After two audits of the package against the paper's -6-24 (4 October, v11.2.11-22): the paper's -6-23 and -6-24 reword sentences that these files retell: Eq. (N) and Eq. (volume) are said in words, a horizon gives the area of a cell "since every cell lies on one" (replaced in -6-25 by "on some horizon"), the area shortfall is measured against "a sphere of the same area in flat space", "around" replaces "round", and Sec. IV.D names the quotients of de Sitter space. The docstrings and this README follow. An audit of every certified interval against the figure the paper prints found two that did not pin it, and both are tightened: `ceiling_entropy_range` certifies `0.72175 < (L_cos/2²⁰⁵)² < 0.7218` for the paper's `0.7218`, and `temperature_led_numbers` certifies `1.0435 < 1/(1 − e^{−√10}) < 1.0445` for the Appendix's `0.044`. `log2_planck_band` now bounds the shift of `log₂ L` itself, below `0.02` over Planck's band and above `0.015` at its two corners, for the paper's "about ±0.02"; `cosmic_mass_beyond_nariai` certifies the Galaxy's mass, `2.0×10⁴² kg`; and `constancy_numbers` certifies `q = Ω_m/2 − Ω_Λ = −0.53`. An audit of every docstring against the paper's text found one stale quotation, in the header of `Planes.lean`, and passages that retold sentences the paper had since changed; they are brought in line, and the README's table of inputs lists every theorem that takes each hypothesis. Figures that the paper no longer prints are marked as such where the files keep them (`phase_grid`, `powers_of_two`, `palmer_211`, `sun_counts`, `solar_tests`, `aM_val`, `zero_gravity_masses`), and `vacuum_orders` says that the paper prints the orders of its figures only. No theorem is added or removed: the ledger lists the same 190 names.

After a referee's item on where a body is, and after the corrections that the audits left for the paper (4 October, v11.2.11-23): the paper's -6-25 states its scope where it brings in the manifold, "How it arises from the register is beyond the scope of this paper. We relate the two only at an observer's cell and along the rings through it." (In -6-27 "How the manifold arises" replaced the first three words.) The paper's -6-25 also treats a body as a point at one cell, and it writes the cells of a horizon as those `⌊L/4⌋` steps from the observer's cell, which is the `L / 4` of `Bridge.horizon_cells`, a division of whole numbers; the docstrings of `Symmetry.lean`, `Bridge.lean` and `Chain.lean` follow. When 4 does not divide `L`, those cells lie half a step inside the horizon that `RouteB.weight` puts `L/4` cells out; the docstring of `horizon_cells` says so, and the docstring of `Chain.Nc` no longer says that a ring crosses a horizon in an opposite pair, which holds only for a ring through the observer's cell, and only when 4 divides `L`. The drift a Hubble-radius horizon would give is now printed as `6.5×10⁻¹¹ yr⁻¹`, about 650 times the lunar-laser-ranging bound, and `constancy_numbers` certifies both figures for `q = Ω_m/2 − Ω_Λ` and for the rounded `q = −0.53`; the bounds of v11.2.11-22 were those of `6×10⁻¹¹` and of six hundred, which held for the rounded `q` only. Appendix A now prints the Sun's `GM☉` in full, `1.327 124 4×10²⁰ m³ s⁻²`, the IAU's nominal value, and these files now use that value; they had `1.32712440018×10²⁰`, which agrees with it to those eight figures, and no interval changes. Appendix A also prints Planck's `±0.5` on `H₀`, and Data availability refers to these files for the standard units and solar-system constants, which the header of `Numerics.lean` lists. No theorem is added or removed: the ledger lists the same 190 names.

After the referee's D4 (5 October, v11.2.11-24, paper -6-26): the check `b = Ω_Λ/ln 2` and the bath's relaxation leave Sec. II, and the new Sec. IV.E gives every string the energy `E_c/L` of Eq. (energy), so that the heat of the `L²/4` strings, one bit each at the horizon's temperature, is `ln 2` of all the energy, `E_Λ` of `U = LE_c/4`; Table I gets the row "Vacuum's share" and Figure 1 a box. `vacuum_share` (new, in place of `Numerics.bit_per_string`) certifies the section's, the row's and Table II's numbers; `lnN_val`, `relax_1MeV` and `crossover_radius` stay and now certify figures the paper no longer prints (the comparison of the coefficients leaves Sec. IV.C too, and `coefficients` certifies Appendix A's); the header of `Chain.lean` follows. The ledger holds 190 theorems.

After the referee's points of wording (6 October, v11.2.11-25, paper -6-27): the paper's -6-27 rewords sentences that these files retell. Its Sec. II no longer says of the register's largest rings that each is a great circle of the horizon of some observer at rest; Sec. IV.D says that of the closed geodesics of de Sitter space, as before. The docstring of `Chain.Rhor` and this README follow, and the entry for v11.2.11-23 notes the new wording of the scope sentence it quotes. The count of Sec. II now adds that the ring `E` misses the opposite of `P` as well, "since the opposite of P′ is P"; the files had that step in `two_mul_card_through`, from `opp_mem_iff`, and the docstrings of `exists_loop_not_mem` and `two_mul_card_through` now say so. A reader with no part in the edits then read the package against -6-27. After his read the header of `Chain.lean` says that the count fixes `η` in units of the cell, as Sec. III now does; the docstring of `black_hole_quarter` no longer calls Sec. IV.D's passage on the shorter rings a remark; `crossover_speed` and the header of `Cosmo.lean` say that the paper has not printed the crossover radius since -6-26; and this README says that no theorem uses the totally geodesic spheres at the horizons, and that `Cosmo.lean` also holds the theorems of Sec. VI. Prose only: no statement, proof, hypothesis or import changes, and the ledger lists the same 190 names.

After the referee's D3 (8 October, v11.2.11-26, paper -6-29): the paper's -6-29 says in Sec. IV.A that by themselves the missing outcomes show nothing, that the test therefore rests on whether each string has room for the different states of its qubit, and that the random state's last qubit has a different state for each joint outcome; "Only in the random state do the missing outcomes show" is gone. Sec. V and Prediction 2 make a measured ceiling conditional on a shortfall above it larger than the error of the experiment, and Prediction 2 no longer says that a return at 205 or more falsifies Eq. (G). The header of `Capacity.lean` and the docstrings of `product_state`, `ceiling_by_outcome`, `Gof_factor_four` and `ceiling_above_204` follow, and this README says how `product_state` stands to the test. Prose only: no statement, proof, hypothesis or import changes, and the ledger lists the same 190 names.

After the referee's remark on the clause of Postulate 2 that was then its last, "no ring holds every cell" (8 October, v11.2.11-27, paper -6-30): `Horizon` takes the clause "no ring holds every cell" as `two_loops`, the existence of two different largest rings. `last_clause_iff` (new) proves the two equivalent, given the other clauses, `L ≥ 4` and one largest ring: with two different rings every ring misses a cell (`exists_cell_not_mem`), and a ring that misses a cell gives a second ring, the ring through that cell and a cell of the first that is not its opposite. The header of `Horizon.lean` and the docstrings of `two_loops` and `exists_cell_not_mem` follow. No other statement, proof, hypothesis or import changes. The ledger holds 191 theorems.

Released as v15: the tree of v11.2.11-27 under a new tag, since tag v14 is the 189-theorem tree (v11.2.11-20) that version 14 of the paper cites. 191 theorems.

After the review against SciPost Physics's criteria (9 October, v11.2.11-28, paper -6-32): the paper's -6-32 removes Sec. IV E, the row "Vacuum's share" of Table I and its Table II, and keeps one sentence in Sec. IV D, that the vacuum's share of the energy inside `R_Λ` today is close to `ln 2`, the heat `E_Λ` as a fraction of `LE_c/4`, which it notes and does not derive; the former Table III is Table II. Its Sec. IV A names the conditional state of a qubit, its state given the outcomes of the earlier qubits, as what a block holds, and states the limit for a cluster state followed by one layer of generic single-qubit rotations, which has as many conditional states as a random state, so that the return test runs on physical qubits. The docstring of `vacuum_share`, the headers of `Chain.lean`, `Numerics.lean` and `Capacity.lean`, the docstrings of `product_state` and `ceiling_by_outcome`, and this README follow. Prose only: no statement, proof, hypothesis or import changes, and the ledger lists the same 191 names.

10 October (v11.2.11-29, paper -6-34; the tree of v11.2.11-28 was never tagged): the paper's -6-34 restores what -6-32 removed, Sec. IV E with its Table II, the row "Vacuum's share" of Table I and the heat box of Figure 1, as they stood in -6-31, so the table of the pins is Table III again; and it restores the three sentences of the bit's history on the bath's relaxation, as they stood up to -6-25, with their figures in Appendix A. Its Table I and Introduction say where the measured `G` and `Λ` enter, and that nothing is fitted. The header of `Chain.lean` and the docstring of `vacuum_share` are again as in v15, and so is the header of `Numerics.lean` but for its clause on `relax_1MeV`, whose figure the paper prints again; the docstrings of `lnN_val` and `relax_1MeV` and their section say that the paper prints their figures again, and the section names which theorem uses which input; this README follows. Prose only: no statement, proof, hypothesis or import changes, and the ledger lists the same 191 names.

10 October, the bath (v11.2.11-30, paper -6-35; the tree of v11.2.11-29 was never tagged): the paper's -6-35 takes the time in which the bath of the early universe brings the strings to equilibrium from the accepted scattering rate of a hot plasma, about `α²k_BT/ℏ` [J. García-Bellido, arXiv:astro-ph/0502139, Sec. 2.6], in place of the thermal time `ℏ/k_BT`, and prints `3.5×10⁻¹⁵ s` for `282` such times at `1 MeV`; the hedge on a bath `10¹⁸` times slower and the sentence on the growing margin go. `scatter_1MeV` (new) certifies the figure, with the fine-structure constant `α = 7.2973525643×10⁻³` (CODATA 2022) as a new input; `Numerics.relax_1MeV`, whose figures the paper no longer prints, is removed; the docstring of `lnN_val`, its section, the header of `Numerics.lean` and this README follow. One statement is new and one is gone, so the ledger lists 191 names.

Released as v16: the tree of v11.2.11-30 under a new tag, since tag v15 is the tree of v11.2.11-27 that version 15 of the paper cites. 191 theorems.

After the third review against SciPost Physics's criteria (11 and 12 October, v11.2.11-31, paper -6-36): the paper's -6-36 writes the fine-structure constant as `α_e`, apart from the mass in cell masses `α = M/m_c`; drops from Sec. II the reading of the string as a wave, the energy `h/τ*` of one step of phase per tick; and has Sec. IV.E name the energy of a free string as the paper's fifth input from outside the postulates. That energy is the zero of energy that the hidden global phase leaves open (Postulate 1), which gravitation does not hide; the paper takes it from the one period a free string and the horizon share, the lap, and gives each free string the horizon's thermal energy, `ℏ` over the lap, `k_BT = E_c/L`, which is `E_c/L²` for each of its `L` bits. `Cosmo.heat_share` (new) takes it as the hypothesis `hE` and returns the strings' energy `U = LE_c/4` and their heat `U ln 2 = E_Λ`. `Cosmo.heat_share_iff` (new) shows that the heat is `ln 2` of the energy exactly when each string holds `k_BT`, so the measured `Ω_Λ` tests `hE`. `Chain.senses_equal_weight` (new) shows that the horizon's Boltzmann weights give the two senses of a string equal probability exactly when their energies are equal, the step from Sec. II's equal energies to one bit (`one_bit`). The table of inputs gains the row for `hE`; the headers of `Chain.lean`, `Cosmo.lean` and `Numerics.lean`, the docstrings of `Shor` and `vacuum_share`, and this README follow; the README's first paragraph now gives the development number of v16 as v11.2.11-30, where it had said -29. No other statement, proof, hypothesis or import changes. The ledger holds 194 theorems.


After the author's derivations of 12 to 14 October (v11.2.11-32, paper -6-37): the paper's -6-37 adds to Postulate 2 the clauses "No cell differs from another: some map of the cells that keeps the largest rings, and the positions along them up to one common shift, carries any cell to any other and leaves every law unchanged. No law depends on the direction in which a ring passes through a cell", followed by "Every largest ring is a closed geodesic of space", and a last sentence to Postulate 1, and with them no longer needs two of the inputs that -6-36 took from outside the postulates. The first is the symmetry of space, for the strings of a sphere. They are now counted ring by ring, with no hypothesis beyond the drawing of the largest rings as great circles of the three-sphere: a given ring crosses a sphere of areal radius `r` from the fraction `(r/R_Λ)²` of the sphere's positions, Archimedes' hat-box theorem in four dimensions, so a sphere holds, averaged over its position, the number of rings times `(r/R_Λ)²` (`Bridge.sphere_strings`); `Bridge.sphere_strings_model` (new, with the register `Bridge.regSix`) shows that the hypotheses of the count can be met together. `HatBox.lean` (new) proves the hat-box theorem (`disk`, `annulus`, `core`, `haar_ratio`, `plane_finrank`, `plane_norm`, `cone_eq`, `cone_measure`, `hatBox`), and `Crossing.lean` (new) proves when a great circle crosses a sphere about a point (`crosses_iff`, `exists_outside`), that the sphere is round, of areal radius `r` (`sphere_round`), and the count (`Drawing`, `Drawing.crosses_center`, `Drawing.crossing_fraction`, `Drawing.meanStrings_eq`). `Geodesics.lean` (new) derives the drawing and the horizon's radius from a ring as a closed geodesic of space: only de Sitter space has a closed spacelike geodesic, all of length `2π√(3/Λ)`, and in its space at an instant such a geodesic is a great circle (`harmonic_zero`, `harmonic_eq`, `IsGeodesic.speed_const`, `IsGeodesic.acc_eq`, `IsGeodesic.circle`, `IsGeodesic.hasLength`, `IsGeodesic.not_periodic_of_neg`, `IsFlatGeodesic.not_periodic`, `ring_forces_deSitter`, `IsGeodesic.greatCircle`, `Crossing.Drawing.ofRings`, `ofRings_circle`, with the models `circleCurve`, `circleCurve_geodesic`, `circleCurve_spacelike`, `hasRing_model`); `antiDeSitter_model`, `flat_model`, `hypCurve` and `lineCurve` show that the other two spacetimes have spacelike geodesics, none closed. `DeSitter.horizon_radius_of_rings` (new) derives `hΛ` and `hgeo` of `horizon_radius`, with `rings_model`, and `Bridge.sphere_strings_of_rings` and `Bridge.sphere_strings_deSitter` (new) the count with the drawing derived. `TwoCells.lean` (new) proves the geometry behind the last sentence of Postulate 2, which matches the register on pairs of cells; drafts of this release instead drew one ring, through two cells that are not opposite, as the geodesic through them, in Sec. II, and took the drawing as a hypothesis. One geodesic of space, closed, passes through two points that are not antipodal (`greatCircle_through`, `greatCircle_subset`, `greatCircle_unique`, `geodesic_through_two`). It is the line that every rotation about them leaves in place, point by point (`greatCircle_fixed`, `greatCircle_fixed_by_rotations`, `fixed_eq_greatCircle`, `mem_greatCircle_of_perp`, with `rot`, `IsRotationAbout`, `inner_self_rot` and `exists_orthonormal_complement`), and the only closed curve on the sphere through them that does not cross itself and that every rotation about them carries onto itself (`eq_greatCircle_of_rotations`, `greatCircle_only`, with `not_injOn_of_periodic`, `ClosedCurve`, `ClosedCurve.range_eq_of_subset`, `circle` and `orbit`). An isometry of space carries a closed geodesic of space to one of the same length (`mapCurve`, `mapCurve_isGeodesic`, `mapCurve_spacelike`, `mapCurve_hasLength`, `mapCurve_range`). `Bridge.ring_through_two_cells`, `Bridge.sphere_strings_drawn` and `Bridge.sphere_strings_drawn_model` (new) join it to the register and the count. `Quotients.lean` (new) proves the exclusion of the quotients of the three spacetimes, which `Geodesic.HasRing` had taken from the text: with every spacelike geodesic a ring of one length (`HasRings`, `ClosesIn`, `Returns`), flat and anti-de Sitter space have no quotient (`ProperOn`, `not_spread`, `flat_no_rings`, `adsCover`, `form_adsCover`, `antiDeSitter_no_rings`), and a quotient of de Sitter space that keeps the direction of time is trivial (`DSQuotient`, `finite_of_proper`, `exists_fixed_time`, `boost`, `form_boost`, `mapLin`, `mapLin_isGeodesic`, `planeCurve`, `planeCurve_isGeodesic`, `exists_rotation_plane`, `exists_angle_pos`, `deSitter_rings`); `rings_force_deSitter`, `HasRings.hasRing`, `dsQuotient_bot`, `closesIn_bot` and `hasRings_model` join it to the chain, `antipodalGroup` and `antipodal_quotient` show that the definition admits a quotient other than the trivial one, `ellipticGroup`, `elliptic_rings` and `elliptic_quotient` that without the direction of time elliptic de Sitter space would have rings, and `DeSitter.horizon_radius_of_rings` takes `HasRings` as its hypothesis. `Symmetry.lean`, with `strings_from_symmetry`, `density_uniform`, `symmetry_model` and the hypothesis `hsym`, is removed, and so is `Chain.Nc`, the crossings of a sphere. Drafts of this release derived the area a cell takes in the horizon, first from Postulate 1's hidden origin, through a law that reads a cell's place on a ring (`moveOrigin`, `ring_alike`, `cells_alike`, whose hypotheses an audit found equivalent to its conclusion), then from a claim after the postulates that with thermodynamics and elasticity they fully describe gravitational phenomena (`hinv`, `hom`), and then from the clause (`hlaw`, `Positions.LawAlike`, `Bridge.cell_area`); they read the strings of a sphere from that area at one of its cells, and proved that a string crosses a sphere exactly twice (`Crossing.Drawing.crossings_eq`), with three statements about the sphere's cells as hypotheses (`hshare`, `hcount`, `hthrough`) and tangent directions as a null set (`Directions.lean`). The area of a cell they used, `Lℓ_c²/π`, is the share of the `L/2` crossings at a cell, not an area at the scale of `ℓ_c`; the count ring by ring replaces them, and they are removed. `Homogeneous.lean` (new) states the clause (`Positions.Symmetry`, `Positions.Homogeneous`, `Symmetry.map_opp`), whose maps exist for every prime-power order: `DiffSet.lean` (new) builds a register with them from a perfect difference set (`ofDiffSet`, `ofDiffSetPositions`, `dsSym`, `ofDiffSet_homogeneous`, `register_of_diffSet`, `fano_diffSet`), and `Singer.lean` (new) proves Singer's theorem, a perfect difference set of `q + 1` residues mod `q² + q + 1` for every finite field of `q` elements (`exists_pds`, `homogeneous_register`, `homogeneous_register_of_prime_power`). The Fano model now takes its lines in Singer's form and has the clause's maps (`fanoSym`, `fano_homogeneous`). Since a plane without such a symmetry no longer gives a register, `Planes.lean` (`fieldPlane_order`, `register_of_prime_power`) and the doubling of an arbitrary plane in `Models.lean` (`double`, `doublePositions`, `register_of_plane`, `six_le_double`) are removed. `Horizon.last_clause_iff` is renamed `fourth_clause_iff`, since "no ring holds every cell" is now the fourth clause of Postulate 2. The second is the energy of a free string, with one input in place of a value. Postulate 1 gains a last sentence, that a lap changes a string's global phase by a whole multiple of `2π/n` on a ring of `n` cells, and its sentence on the hidden global phase now reads "no law depends on its value"; the paper now defines the global phase. A free string's energy, the rate of its global phase, is then a whole number of steps `δ = 2πE_c/L²` (`Cosmo.ladder_of_lap`, new, with that sentence as `hlap`), and the horizon weights each step from `0` to `L² − 1` by its Boltzmann factor alone (`hW`, in place of `hE`). `Thermal.lean` (new) evaluates that mean, Planck's less the term from the cut (`ladderMean_eq`, with `geom_weight` and `geom_energy`), and bounds it (`ladderMean_bounds`, `ladder_cut_le`, `two_sub_mul_exp_le`); `Cosmo.string_energy` (new) gives a free string `k_BT = E_c/L` to within `π/L + 1/πL`, and `Numerics.string_energy_precision` (new) shows that to be less than `10⁻⁶¹`; `Cosmo.heat_share` now takes `hW` and returns the heat `E_Λ` exactly and the energy `U = LE_c/4` to the same precision. A draft of this release took the energy as continuous, from zero up to `2πE_c`, with an integral in place of the sum; the last sentence of Postulate 1 replaces it. The symmetry clause's common shift now rests on the origin, which the postulates do not give, and no longer on the hidden global phase. New imports: `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv` in `Thermal.lean`, `Mathlib.FieldTheory.Finite.Extension` in `Singer.lean`, `Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Constructions.HaarToSphere`, `Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Measure.Lebesgue.EqHaar`, `Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Measure.Prod` and `Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Projection.FiniteDimensional` in `HatBox.lean`, `Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Integral.Bochner.Set` in `Crossing.lean`, and `Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Continuous`, `Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.FDeriv.Bilinear`, `Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.Deriv.Shift`, `Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue`, `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Trigonometric.Deriv`, `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Trigonometric.Inverse` and `Mathlib.Tactic.Module` in `Geodesics.lean`; `Galactic.lean` also imports `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Trigonometric.Deriv`, for `flat_not_from_equality`, and `Center.lean` (new) imports `Mathlib.Analysis.Quaternion`. The equality in the limit of Sec. IV.C now enters as the strain itself tending to the value the equality gives, `ε²A → dV_M/dr`, and `Galactic.deep_regime_flat` (new) derives from it, with the strain not negative (`hpos`, the positive root that Verlinde's assumption takes), the flat rotation curve, `v_c² = gr → √(a_M GM)` with the total field, and `deep_regime_flat_model` (new) shows that its hypotheses can be met; the earlier form, the ratio of the two sides tending to one, does not make the curve flat (`Galactic.flat_not_from_equality`, new, with a strain that oscillates), so the paper had taken the flat curve as derived where it was not, and it now takes it as observed only for the bound (`deep_regime_upper_bound`). `Bridge.coefficient_from_count` (new, with `horizonWeight` and `horizonWeight_quarter`) joins the count to the law, which comments alone had joined: with the count's horizon taken to be the solution's, a quarter lap out, the share factor is `4` and no other, the volume `V_M` built on it grows at the rate the law uses, and the deep-regime law has `a_M = πc²/12R_Λ`. It takes as `hx` that a ring's cells, of length `ℓ_c`, measure proper distance along it (Postulates 1 and 2) and that the count's horizon is the solution's; `coefficient_from_count_model` (new) shows that its hypotheses can be met, and `Bridge.lean` now imports `DeSitter.lean`. `Capacity.cells_read_alike` now takes the paper's principle in place of the exchange it took before: the arrangement of the outcomes over the cells is no part of the state (`hst`), and the probability of an outcome depends on the state alone (`hP`), for one string or for strings measured jointly. `Capacity.read_alike_model` (new) shows that reading every cell alike meets the principle, so for the state that forgets only the arrangement the two say the same thing; what the principle adds is its sources, Postulate 1 and the quantum-mechanical state. `Capacity.swap_form` (new) derives from it the exchange of two cells' outcomes, which the theorem had taken as its hypothesis `hinv`, and `Capacity.share_read` (new) the probability of reading a set of cells; `Galactic.string_share`, which was arithmetic, now derives from the principle the share of the cells inside the horizon that a set of them holds, given that the cell read lies inside, and `Galactic.lean` imports `Capacity.lean`. `Galactic.removed_fraction` (new) shows that the entropy a mass removes within a sphere, `S_M(r)`, is the fraction `2GM/c²r` of the strings' entropy `N_s(r) ln 2`, so to first order a sphere about the mass holds that fraction fewer strings than the count without a mass, Eq. (N). The README now also lists the premises of `Capacity.cells_read_alike` (now `hst` and `hP`), which it had left out, and four docstrings say "at most" where a bound is not strict. The headers of `Horizon.lean`, `Projective.lean`, `Chain.lean`, `Cosmo.lean`, `Capacity.lean`, `RouteB.lean`, `Galactic.lean`, `Bridge.lean`, `Models.lean`, `Crossing.lean`, `Geodesics.lean`, `DeSitter.lean` and `Ledger.lean`, the docstrings of `senses_equal_weight`, `Ns`, `bulk_counts`, `ratio_of_areas`, `vacuum_share`, `Crossing.Drawing`, `Geodesic.HasRing` and `DeSitter.horizon_radius_of_rings`, and this README follow. Four audits of the package against -6-37 then found no statement in error, and gaps that are now closed or named: the steps the paper argues in words are named where they enter. `Horizon.four_le` (new) derives `L ≥ 4`, which the count takes, from the fourth clause in the paper's words. `Capacity.ceiling_logical` and `Numerics.logical_ceiling_cos` (new) give the ceiling for logical qubits and its `203`; `Capacity.block_fraction_read` (new) a block's fraction as a conditional probability; `Galactic.massive_horizon_count` (new) the `L_M²/4` bits of the horizon with a mass inside, to first order; `Bridge.horizon_entropy` (new) the bit per ring, from the equal energies of the two senses (`hsenses`, the clause on directions) and the horizon's Boltzmann weighting; and `Numerics.string_energy_precision_of_le` (new) the precision of a free string's energy for every whole `L` near `L_cos`. `Galactic.temperature_floor` now takes Deser–Levin alone: its other conjunct took as its premise, `hLL`, what it concluded. `Numerics.rar_transition` also certifies the fraction at the computed crossing, `Models.fano_counts` states `ρ = 3`, and `Bridge.coefficient_from_count_model` takes `G` from Jacobson's step, so that it meets the hypothesis of the law in the last conjunct. The docstrings and this README name the clauses of Postulate 2 in the paper's order, name the steps that the paper argues in words and the files do not formalize, state the naming convention, and correct the statements the audits found inexact. `Crossing.Drawing.strings_between` (new) bounds the count about a given position by the averages at `s − δ` and `s + δ` when the rings run alike about every point to within `δ` (`halike`), in place of the reading that the body sits in no preferred position, with `Drawing.isOpen_crossers`, `Drawing.strings_eq_sum`, `Drawing.integrable_strings`, `Drawing.crosses_of_lt`, `Drawing.strings_of_lt` and `Drawing.strings_of_nonpos`; `Bridge.strings_between_model` (new) shows that its hypotheses can be met, with the six rings of `Bridge.hopfSix`, which run alike to within half the radius (`Bridge.hopfSix_alike`, with `hopfSign`, `hopfMap`, `hopfMap_sq`, `hopfA`, `hopfB`, `hopfSix_P`, `hopfSix_crosses`, `sphere_coords`, `hopf_pair_sq_le`, `hopfSign_sq_lt`, `hopfSign_sq_le` and the counts `hopf_card_le_one`, `hopf_one_le_card`, `hopf_card_le_three`, `hopf_three_le_card`, `hopf_card_le_five`, `hopf_five_le_card`). `Center.lean` (new) averages the count over the rotations `x ↦ qx` of the drawing by unit quaternions, with the three-sphere in `ℍ`: `strings_rotation_mean` gives the number of rings times `(r/ρ)²` about every point, for every drawing, and `sphere_strings_centered` the chain's `N_s(r)` times `1 − 2/L + 4/L²`, with `star_mul_unit`, `mul_star_unit`, `leftRot`, `leftRot_apply`, `leftRot_symm_apply`, `leftRot_transitive`, `starRight` and `sphere_strings_centered_model`; it carries both to any four-dimensional space through an isometry onto `ℍ` (`exists_isometry_quaternion`, `rotOf`, `rotOf_symm_apply`, `rotOf_transitive`, `strings_rotation_mean_of`, `sphere_strings_centered_of`) and to the drawing of the rings as closed geodesics of space (`sphere_strings_of_rings_centered`, `sphere_strings_of_rings_centered_model`), and `Crossing.Drawing.rotate` now carries a drawing by any linear isometry, into the same space or another; `Crossing.lean` adds `greatCircle_map`, `sphereEquiv`, `sphereEquiv_coe`, `map_isometry`, `toSphere_preserving`, `Drawing.rotate`, `Drawing.crosses_rotate` and `Drawing.strings_rotate`. `Center.lean` then derives Eq. (volume), to relative order `1/nL`, as an average over the rotations of the drawing, a step the paper had argued in words and the definition `Sin` encoded: `Crossing.lean` adds the weighted count (`Drawing.wstrings`, `Drawing.wstrings_eq_sum`, `Drawing.meanWeighted_eq`, `Drawing.wstrings_rotate`), `RouteB.lean` a ring's share of the sphere and their sum over the rings (`Positions.share`, `Positions.shares_sum`), and `Center.lean` the averages (`wstrings_rotation_mean`, `wstrings_rotation_mean_of`, `sin_rotation_mean`, `sin_rotation_mean_of`, `sin_rotation_mean_of_rings`), with `regEight`, `three_le_card_diffSet13`, `sin_rotation_mean_model` and `sin_rotation_mean_of_rings_model`; `DiffSet.lean` adds the perfect difference set `{0, 1, 3, 9}` mod 13 (`diffSet13`, `card_diffSet13`). The docstrings and this README now say that the drawing is no part of the state, so that with every rotation weighted alike the count about a body, and the entropy its strings hold inside a sphere, are defined as averages over the rotations; that step is no longer named as one the files do not formalize, and of the two steps from the paper's former Table II that it argues in words one remains, that the horizon counts every ring; these additions need no new imports. `Weighting.lean` (new) shows that the averages do not depend on weighting the rotations `x ↦ qx` by the unit sphere's measure: every weighting of any group of rotations that contains them, if it prefers no rotation, spreads the point evenly over the three-sphere and gives the same averages (`eq_toSphere_of_invariant`, `eq_toSphere_of_invariant_of`, `strings_mean_invariant`, `wstrings_mean_invariant`, `strings_mean_invariant_of`, `wstrings_mean_invariant_of`, `isMulRightInvariant_of_left`, `moved_point_invariant`, `moved_point_invariant_rotOf`, `moved_point_even`, `strings_weighting_mean`, `wstrings_weighting_mean`, `ring_crossing_weighting`, `sphere_strings_weighting`, `sin_weighting_mean`, `sphere_strings_weighting_of_rings`, `sin_weighting_mean_of_rings`), with `mul_eq_sphereEquiv`, `measurable_strings`, `measurable_wstrings`, `toSphere_isMulLeftInvariant`, `toSphere_isHaarMeasure`, `integral_normalized`, `sphereMap`, `sphereMap_coe`, `sphereMap_rotOf`, `isMulLeftInvariant_map`, `wstrings_single`, `sin_of_average`, `rightRot`, `rightRot_apply`, `mul_right_eq_sphereEquiv`, `rotOfHom`, `biRot`, `evenWeight`, `evenWeight_isProbabilityMeasure`, `evenWeight_isMulRightInvariant` and the models `weighting_model`, `biRot_model` and `sin_weighting_mean_of_rings_model`. New imports: `Mathlib.Analysis.Normed.Field.UnitBall`, for the group of unit quaternions, and `Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Measure.Haar.Unique`, for the uniqueness of Haar measure, both in `Weighting.lean`. `Horizon.Positions.CrossesHorizon`, `Horizon.Positions.crossesHorizon`, `Horizon.Positions.weight_nonneg`, `Horizon.Positions.weight_le_one`, `Bridge.horizonEntropy` and `Bridge.horizon_counts_every_ring` (new) close one of the two steps the paper argued in words: a horizon counts the strings that describe it, those that define its physical laws, which by the postulates all strings together do, so its entropy, defined as that of every string (`horizonEntropy`), is `card Loop · ln 2`; and every ring crosses every observer's horizon, part inside and part outside, since half its cells lie inside; no new imports. `Coding.lean` (new) proves the coding step for logical qubits, which the paper argued in words: in a code of two cosets a physical bit, given any other bits of its block, is fixed, balanced, or equal or opposite to the logical bit, and whether it is balanced depends on the positions read alone (`css_conditional`, `css_conditional_cases`); in a repetition code it is only fixed or equal (`repetition_conditional`); and in a code read as the parity of a line (`LineCode`), the last bit of the line is the logical bit plus the parity of the rest (`LineCode.bit_eq`), and so the logical bit or its opposite, given the rest of the line where every reading of the rest occurs in both cosets (`LineCode.bitGiven_rest`) and given the parity of the rest in every such code (`LineCode.two_mul_card_cosetRest`, `LineCode.lastGivenRest_eq`). The surface code of every distance is such a code with every reading of the rest in both cosets (`surfaceCode`, `surfaceCode_match`); at distance 3 it is written out with its `Z` checks (`surfaceThree`, `surfaceThree_checks`, `surfaceCode_three`), and given all the other bits of its block the last bit of the row is fixed (`surfaceThree_fixed`). For `N` logical qubits each in its own block, the last block given the earlier ones is a code state with the logical weights of their outcome (`jointBitGiven_eq`), so the same holds given all the earlier physical bits (`css_conditional_joint`, `LineCode.jointBitGiven_rest`); with the blocks read in order, a physical string has up to `2^N` conditional states to hold, in a repetition code up to `2^(N−1)`, and the last bit of a line, in a code that meets `LineCode.Match` and in a random state, `2^N` (`card_condStates_le`, `card_condStates_repetition`, `LineCode.card_lineStates`); and a string needs two cells for each conditional state it holds (`two_mul_card_states_le`), which gives `2^(N+1) ≤ L` where both cases occur (`ceiling_both_cases`, `ceiling_line_code`). The file also has `Word`, `parity`, `parity_add`, `parity_neg`, `zmod2_add_self`, `zmod2_not_eq_iff`, `zmod2_eq_zero_of_add_one`, `zmod2_eq_zero_of_ne_one`, `zmod2_zero_or_one`, `zmod2_eq_of_add_eq_zero`, `word_add_self`, `weight`, `bitGiven`, `bitGiven_counts`, `agreeing`, `kernel`, `card_agreeing`, `bits_balanced`, `bits_constant`, `ratio_cases`, `ratio_half`, `sum_weight`, `filter_agreeing`, `filter_agreeing_bit`, `caseProb`, `caseProb_pos_lt_one`, `LineCode.v_not_mem`, `LineCode.rest`, `LineCode.rest_add`, `LineCode.rest_flip`, `LineCode.exists_rest`, `LineCode.Match`, `LineCode.coset`, `LineCode.cosetRest`, `LineCode.card_cosetRest`, `LineCode.card_coset`, `LineCode.two_le_card_coset`, `LineCode.lastGivenRest`, `LineCode.bit_of_mem`, `LineCode.bit_of_mem_v`, `LineCode.filter_rest`, `LineCode.filter_rest_zero`, `LineCode.filter_last_zero`, `LineCode.filter_last_v`, `jointWeight`, `jointBitGiven`, `condStates`, `lineTwo`, `lineTwo_match`, `wordOf`, `wordAt`, `Surface.plaq`, `Surface.top`, `Surface.bot`, `Surface.xChecks`, `Surface.rowCheck`, `Surface.logicalX`, `Surface.row`, `Surface.rowCheck_mem`, `Surface.rowCheck_apply`, `Surface.parity_row`, `Surface.parity_row_xCheck`, `Surface.parity_row_logicalX`, `modelWeights` and `ceiling_line_code_model`. Its imports, `Mathlib.Algebra.Group.Subgroup.Basic`, `Mathlib.Data.ZMod.Basic` and `Mathlib.Algebra.BigOperators.Ring.Finset`, add no Mathlib module the package did not already use. `Symmetry.lean` (new; it takes the name of the file removed above and shares nothing with it) derives the symmetry of space, which the files had named as a step they do not formalize, by the arguments of 16 and 17 October: the drawing of the rings is no part of the state, so the laws are functions of the register, and the two clauses make them the same at every crossing, so the law at a slot does not depend on the drawing; and the rings describe everything physical, so a place of space, with a direction there, that no ring could run through would be no part of what the postulates describe. Four definitions carry these, as `Bridge.horizonEntropy` carries the thesis: a law is a function of the register, of the crossing of a ring with a cell, and its value at a slot of space is that of a crossing some drawing puts there (`lawAt`); space is the slots some drawing reaches (`described`); the drawings the last sentence of Postulate 2 allows run every ring along one curve that closes with a largest ring's length (`admissible`); and the manifold of the register without a mass is a solution among the three maximally symmetric spacetimes or their quotients whose space the rings describe (`RegisterManifold`). With the clause of Postulate 2 that no cell differs from another, as a symmetry of the register that carries any cell to any other and leaves every law unchanged (`hsym`), and the clause on directions (`hdir`), every law has the same value at every crossing (`laws_same`), so the law at a slot does not depend on the drawing (`lawAt_eq`) and is the same at every place and in every direction (`lawAt_same`, with `laws_same_model` and `homogeneous_of_sym`); the entropy density of a local horizon is such a law, so Jacobson's step gives `G = c³k_B/4ℏη` with the count's `η` (`one_density`, `jacobson_one_density`); a slot lies in the space the admissible drawings describe exactly when the geodesic through it closes with a largest ring's length (`mem_described_admissible`), so the manifold of the register is `Quotients.HasRings` (`registerManifold_iff`), and it is de Sitter space itself with `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` (`cosmological_constant`, `registerManifold_trivial`). `Bridge.horizonEntropy` is restated (17 October), the premise being that the horizon's entropy depends only on strings, not that it is that of every string: it is now the Shannon entropy of the horizon's thermal state, a distribution on the senses of the strings and of nothing else (the thesis), the strings independent (`Entropy.jointProb`, with each string's Boltzmann weights `thermalProb`; `Entropy.lean`, new), and `horizonEntropy_eq_sum` proves that it is the sum of the strings' binary entropies (`Entropy.shannon_jointProb`, with `senseProb`, `jointProb`, `shannon`, `sum_senseProb`, `sum_prod_senses`, `sum_jointProb_mul`, `sum_jointProb`), so that the sum over every string is derived, the state being the whole register's by its type; `horizon_entropy` is restated on it. After an audit the state is a parameter of `horizonEntropy`, and its product form (`hfree`) and Boltzmann form (`hBoltz`) are named hypotheses of `horizon_entropy`, `horizon_counts_every_ring` and `Cosmo.vacuum_energy`, as `hW` is for a string's energy; `vacuum_energy` takes the state at the horizon's own temperature, `β k_BT = 1`. `Entropy.lean` imports three Mathlib modules the package already used. The premise being the paper's thesis (the same day), that gravitation follows from the two postulates, thermodynamics and elasticity, applied by contradiction (a quantity that depended on anything else would be a physical law the strings do not describe), the docstrings of `Symmetry.lean`, `Bridge.lean`, `Center.lean`, `Chain.lean`, `Cosmo.lean`, `DeSitter.lean`, `Quotients.lean` and `Ledger.lean`, and this README, name the thesis where it enters, as a premise in what a state, a law or a space is a function of, in place of calling these definitions Lean takes as given; `Symmetry.RegisterManifold` is restated as the solution's space being determined by the rings, its spacelike geodesics exactly the slots along which the admissible drawings run a ring (`dsSlots`, `flatSlots`, `adsSlots`, `eq_described_iff`), from which `registerManifold_iff` derives that every one closes with a largest ring's length; and `Cosmo.vacuum_energy` (new) derives the vacuum's energy from the entropy of the strings' thermal state and the horizon's temperature, so that it too depends only on strings. `Cosmo.lean` now imports `Bridge.lean`. No hypothesis beyond the two clauses enters `laws_same`, `lawAt_eq` and `lawAt_same`; `jacobson_one_density` adds the thermodynamic step `hJac` and the count `hcount`, and `cosmological_constant` takes `hpi`, that `R.pi` is the real `π`; `registerManifold_trivial` takes only `RegisterManifold`. The definitions carry the rest, and Lean takes them as given: `admissible` the last sentence of Postulate 2, and `RegisterManifold` Hawking and Ellis's cases and Postulate 1's direction of time. Hawking and Ellis are now cited at Secs. 5.2 and 5.3, since vacuum solutions with maximally symmetric sections take the Robertson–Walker analysis of Sec. 5.3. The docstrings of `Chain.lean`, `Homogeneous.lean`, `Bridge.lean`, `TwoCells.lean`, `DeSitter.lean`, `Geodesics.lean`, `Quotients.lean` and `Ledger.lean`, and this README, no longer name the symmetry of space as a step the files do not formalize, though the step from it to the solution's maximally symmetric sections is still argued in words, and they no longer say that the symmetry of space carries one ring onto every ring. `Bridge.sphere_strings_drawn`, which took one ring as a closed geodesic and isometries that carry it onto every ring, and its model are removed, with `TwoCells.mapCurve`, `mapCurve_inSpace`, `mapCurve_isGeodesic`, `mapCurve_spacelike`, `mapCurve_hasLength` and `mapCurve_range`, which only it used: the paper uses none of them, and the count takes the last sentence of Postulate 2 for every ring. `Bridge.lean` no longer imports `TwoCells.lean`. `Symmetry.lean` imports only files of the package. The ledger holds 411 main theorems. An audit of 17 October checked every hypothesis of the 411 main theorems, those inside their conclusions too, against this README. The table of inputs now names energy as the generator of the tick (`hE` of `Cosmo.ladder_of_lap`), the zero of energy (`h0`, `hturn`), a free string as a doublet, the sources of `hfree`, the temperature `hβ`, and every theorem that takes `I1`, `hsym` or the last sentence of Postulate 2; the paragraph on hypotheses that are not inputs names each one. The docstrings of `Chain.lean`, `Horizon.lean`, `DeSitter.lean`, `Quotients.lean`, `Cosmo.lean` and `Ledger.lean` give the thesis as the reason that the horizon counts every ring and that every spacelike geodesic of the solution closes with a largest ring's length. An independent check then named what definitions carry: `Ahor` and the cosmological form of `I1` rest on `hdS`; `Shor`, `Ns` and `Sin` are the closed forms that the thesis's results reach, to stated orders; the drawing of seven theorems is derived in their `_of_rings` versions; and the dimension four of the space that holds the three-sphere is general relativity's three dimensions of space. The table gained a row for measurement (`Capacity.outcomeProb`, `Capacity.jointOutcome`, the Born rule of `Coding.lean`, `hhold`), and `Symmetry.lean` calls Hawking and Ellis's classification general relativity. No statement changed. The same day, `Solution.lean` (new) formalizes the step from the symmetry of space to the solution without a mass, which the files had left in words: without a mass the state is the same at every crossing (`state_same`, with `crossingState`; the new premise `hborn`, that a measurement reads a `1` with the probability of the first sense, Postulate 1), so with the thesis applied to the geometry (`hgeom`) the geometry is the same at every slot (`geometry_same`, with `lawAt_eq_of`, `lawAt_const` and `described_univ`), and Hawking and Ellis's classification, a named hypothesis (`hHE`), gives the solution's cases (`solution_cases`); `solution_deSitter` joins every link, and `determined_deSitter` and `solution_deSitter_model` show that its hypotheses can be met together. `Symmetry.RegisterManifold` is restated as one of Hawking and Ellis's cases (`Symmetry.Case`, new) whose space is determined by the rings (`Symmetry.Determined`, new), equivalent to the old definition, and `Symmetry.case_deSitter` and `Symmetry.cosmological_constant_of_case` (new) take its two parts by name. The docstrings of `Symmetry.lean`, `Geodesics.lean`, `Quotients.lean`, `Chain.lean` and `Ledger.lean`, and this README, no longer call the step argued in words. No statement of an earlier theorem changed. The ledger holds 422 main theorems. Two independent checks of the new step followed. The first asked for the strings' energies in the state at a crossing. The second found that this contradicted Sec. IV.E, where each string has its own energy and the energy adds no state, and that the state left out the string's phase without saying so. `crossingState` is now the string's thermal state with the reading of the cell: the probability of its first sense, the number of its cells that show a `1`, and the probability that the cell is read. Its docstring names what it leaves out and why: the arrangement of the outcomes (`hst`), the phase (a thermal state is a function of the energy alone, and the two senses have one energy), and the string's own energy (it adds no state). The docstrings and this README now say that `hHE` also asserts the direction of time that a case of de Sitter space keeps (Postulate 1), and that nothing in Lean ties the geometry to the solution. The places that cite the thesis for the solution's space cite `Symmetry.Determined`, and those that name the step's hypotheses name `hgeom` with `hHE`. `solution_deSitter_model` now lists `hpi` and `hβ` too. A third check then asked for the string's correlations with other strings, which the state also leaves out: without a mass the strings are free (`hfree`), so there are none, and the docstring of `crossingState` now says so, with the premises `hW`, `h0` and `hturn` behind the energy's weighting. The ledger then still held 422 main theorems. On 18 October the step from the two senses of a free string to its one bit, which the paper had argued in words, became a theorem: a string is one qubit, two states, so it holds at most one bit; its sense is a property of its state; and the thermal state weights the two senses equally, so it holds exactly one bit, each of its states with the probability `1/2` (`Bridge.string_one_bit`, new), and the bit per ring holds on the strings' own states (`Bridge.horizon_entropy_states`, new). The premise `hborn` of `Solution.state_same` and `Solution.solution_deSitter` now reads a `1` with the probability `1/2`, the Born rule for a qubit at one bit, and no longer says that a measurement returns the string's sense. The docstring of `Cosmo.ladder_of_lap` takes the global phase from the tick, the shift up to one phase, not from the two senses. The ledger holds 424 main theorems. Later on 18 October, since steps argued in words are premises in all but name, `Qubit.lean` (new) models the qubit of a free string, and three of them became theorems: the shift changes no law, since it shifts every position alike (`Qubit.law_tick`, Postulate 2); the tick's phase is one for all the string's states (`Qubit.Tick.phase_unique`, linearity); and a free string's thermal state is half the identity, which reads a `1` with the probability `1/2` and holds one bit (`Qubit.Tick.state_eq`, `Qubit.Tick.bornProb_state`, `Qubit.Tick.entropy_state`). `hborn` of `Solution.state_same` and `Solution.solution_deSitter` now equates the probability of reading a `1` with the Born probability of the string's thermal state, Postulate 1's clause, and the `1/2` is derived; `Bridge.horizon_entropy_states` is restated on the strings' thermal states and `Bridge.string_one_bit`, whose premise that the thermal state is a mix of states of one sense each was a hypothesis in all but name, is removed. The ledger holds 436 main theorems; two of them, `Qubit.tick_physical` and `Qubit.law_tick`, need no `Classical.choice`. Later still on 18 October, the zero of energy: the author ruled that it must come from the postulates. The tick's factor is a phase (`Qubit.Tick.scalar`, `norm_scalar`), derived from a lap returning the string to itself up to a phase (`hlap`, a field of `Tick`, Postulate 1, with `shift_pow`), so that unitarity is no longer assumed; a phase fixes an angle only up to whole turns, and a full turn leaves the register as it was (the author's point); the phase gained per tick is that angle counted in the sense of time, in which the shift runs (Postulate 1), from no change (`Qubit.Tick.phase`, in `[0, 2π)`: `phase_nonneg`, `phase_lt_two_pi`, `exp_phase`). So a string at rest has zero energy and the energies count up to a turn, and `Cosmo.ladder_of_lap` now takes the phase from the tick, with `h0` and `hturn` theorems rather than hypotheses (`ladder_of_phase` keeps the arithmetic). `Numerics.zero_of_energy_tested` (new) shows what the zero changes, the vacuum's share `ln 2/(1 + ε₀)` for a rest state of energy `ε₀ k_BT_dS`, and that Planck's and DESI's `Ω_Λ` put `ε₀` within a few hundredths of zero, against a turn of `2πL = 2.7×10⁶²`. `Tick.scalar_pow` and `Tick.phase_lap` carry the lap's quantization of the factor and the ladder. Then, for the paper's sentences on the zero: `Tick.turn_same` (a whole turn per tick is the same tick as none), `Tick.eq_phase` (counted from no change up to a turn, the phase is one) and `Tick.energy_of_eq`, with `Tick.scalar_of_eq` and `Tick.phase_of_eq` (a string whose global phase does not change, whose tick is the shift alone, has zero energy). Then the shift was made concrete: `Qubit.shift L = diag(1, e^{2πi/L})`, the turn of the qubit's phase, the string's position, by `2π/L` (Postulate 1, with the qubit's state in the paper's form, Eq. (qubit)), so that a lap returning every bit to its cell is the theorem `shift_pow` and `Tick` has two fields, `htick` and `hlap`, both clauses of Postulate 1. Since the tick on a free string must come from a postulate's words, Postulate 1's shift clause now reads "The shift moves every bit of a string one cell along its ring per tick; whatever else a tick does to a string's bits is an interaction", and `htick` cites it. Then the constancy in time, which the paper argued in words (Secs. IV.D and VI) and `w_exact` took as its premise: for a register `Rt s` at each time `s`, `horizon_constant`, `cell_constant`, `G_constant`, `rhoΛ_constant` and `GLambda_constant` derive that the horizon's radius, the cell, `G`, `ρ_Λ` and `GΛ` are constant from `L` one number (Postulate 1), `Λ` a constant of Einstein's equations (general relativity: the de Sitter horizon is constant in time, as Sec. IV.D says), the horizon at `R_Λ` (`hdS`), Jacobson's step (`hJ`) and the constants of nature and the numbers the same at every time (`hconst`); `w_exact_in_time` then writes the first law for `ρ_Λ(s)` and gives `w = −1`, and `Lambda_rate_in_time` gives `Λ̇/Λ = −Ġ/G` with the `hK` of `constancy_of_Lambda` derived. Then a review of the whole package for loose ends and dead code, by a metaprogram over every declaration of the files: no theorem has a hypothesis its proof does not reference; no hypothesis is used only through a projection of an iff or a conjunction whose other part alone needs it, the pattern of `hβ` (so `hβ : 0 < β`, which did no work, is gone from `Bridge.horizon_entropy`, `horizon_counts_every_ring`, `Cosmo.vacuum_energy`, which also loses `β k_BT = 1`, `Solution.state_same` and `solution_deSitter`; `Reg.senses_half_of_equal`, new, gives each sense `1/2` at any `β`, and `senses_equal_weight` keeps `β > 0` for its converse); no field of any structure is unused; no two theorems state the same thing; and thirteen declarations that nothing used are removed: `Bridge.Strings` (the one string per ring is the type of the horizon's state), `Bridge.thermalProb_mem`, `Horizon.Positions.ringDist_eq_val`, `weight_nonneg`, `weight_le_one`, `Horizon.pt_eq_pt_iff`, `pt_surjective`, `Quotients.boost_symm_one`, the `NeZero (nPts K)` instance of `Singer.lean`, `Capacity.block_of_injective`, `Qubit.weight_add`, `Qubit.shift_mulVec` and `Geodesic.eform_comm`. The ledger holds 458 main theorems, and every `#print axioms` line still shows at most `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Then the author asked what the weighting of a free string's energy rests on: `hW`, "the paper's input", is split into the Gibbs distribution over the values of the energy (`Thermal.ladderProb`, the hypothesis `hGibbs`: thermodynamics, the canonical state at the horizon's temperature, each value counted once since every value leaves the string one qubit, Postulate 1) and the mean of that distribution (`hEs`), which `Thermal.ladderMean_eq_sum` shows is `ladderMean`; `string_energy` and `heat_share` take the two in place of `hW`. `Thermal.lean` imports `Mathlib.Algebra.BigOperators.Field`, already built, for `Finset.sum_div`. The ledger holds 459 main theorems.


After two double checks of 18 October, late: (1) the whole package for premises beyond the postulates, thermodynamics and elasticity, by a census of all 173 hypothesis names of the 459 ledger theorems, each tagged; what remains beyond the three is known mathematics, general relativity's known results, quantum mechanics as the framework of Postulate 1, the thesis, the constants of nature, and two readings of Postulate 1 that the paper names as hypotheses, the direction of time (`time`, `htime`) and the reading principle of Sec. IV.A (`hst`, `hP`, `hhold`). (2) The whole text against the Lean, by an independent audit, which found no false claim of a proof and no wrong section, equation or number, and nine points of attribution; those on the Lean side are fixed here: `ladder_of_lap` states the ladder of the definition `Qubit.Tick.energy` itself, so the former hypothesis `hE` is gone; the docstrings of `Qubit.Tick.energy` and `Qubit.Tick.phase` say that leaving the shift out of the energy and taking the phase in `[0, 2π)` are choices of those definitions, justified by Postulate 2 (`law_tick`, which no proof uses) and by Postulate 1's sense of time, and not theorems, while the zero of energy at rest is a theorem (`energy_of_eq`); the `Tick.phase` docstring no longer invokes the thesis; and three stale names (`Bridge.Strings`, `hW`, `β > 0`) are corrected. For an hour the constancy chain of item 12 ran from the cell as a constant of Postulate 1, with `Λ`'s constancy a theorem; the audit noted that Sec. IV.D derives the cell's constancy from the horizon's, so the chain runs that way, as above.

19 October, the direction of time: the paper's definition of a tick now says that time runs one way, from each tick to the next (Sec. II), so `time` of `Quotients.DSQuotient` and `htime` of `Quotients.exists_fixed_time` are that definition's words with the shift of Postulate 1 per tick, no longer a reading of Postulate 1 that the paper named as a hypothesis in the note of its Table I; the table above has a row for it, and the docstrings of `Quotients.lean` cite the definition. No theorem changed; the ledger holds 459 main theorems.

19 October, the reading of the cells: Postulate 1 says that the bits are the possible outcomes of one measurement of the qubit, not that a measurement reads one cell by a law that the state sets. So each cell's bit is one possible outcome among the `L`, the outcome is the bit of the cell `x` with the probability `1/L` (`halike`), and the probability of an outcome is its share of the bits, Eq. (raqm) of the paper; this replaces the two hypotheses `hst` (the arrangement of the outcomes is no part of the state) and `hP` (the probability of an outcome depends on the state alone), the reading principle of Sec. IV.A that the paper named as a hypothesis, and the theorems that derived the uniform reading from them, `Capacity.swap_form`, `Capacity.cells_read_alike` and `Capacity.read_alike_model`, are gone. `Capacity.share_read`, `Capacity.block_fraction_read`, `string_share`, `Solution.state_same` and `Solution.solution_deSitter` take `halike`, and `hsum` is no longer needed. For the ceiling, `hhold` (a conditional state strictly between `0` and `1` is held on a block with both bits) is restated as `hshare`: such a conditional state is the share of 1s on its block, the same reading of Postulate 1 for the possible outcomes given a condition; `Coding.two_mul_card_states_le`, `ceiling_both_cases` and `ceiling_line_code` take it, and `ceiling_line_code_model` meets it. The ledger holds 456 main theorems.

19 October, the thesis: Postulate 1 now begins "Everything physical is qubits, and quantum mechanics is granular", so the thesis of the paper, which the files apply as what a state, a law, a space or its geometry is a function of (`hgeom`, `hDet`, `Symmetry.Determined`, the types), is Postulate 1's first sentence and no longer a premise beyond the postulates; the headers of `Symmetry.lean`, `Solution.lean`, `Entropy.lean`, `Center.lean`, `Bridge.lean`, `Chain.lean`, the Ledger and this file say so. No statement changed.

19 October, the constancy of `Λ`: the chain of constancy in time now runs from the register's constants, as Sec. VI's "two facts of the register" say. `horizon_constant` derives the horizon's radius `R_Λ = Lℓ_c/2π` constant from `L` and the cell, the constants of Postulate 1 (`hL`, `hℓc`), and `Lambda_constant` (new, in place of `cell_constant`) derives `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` constant from it with the horizon at `R_Λ` (`hdS`); `G_constant`, `rhoΛ_constant` and `w_exact_in_time` take `hℓc` in place of `hΛ`, which is gone. The constancy of `Λ` that general relativity asserts is thereby derived, and the paper's Sec. IV.D derives it the same way, from `L` and the cell. The ledger holds 456 main theorems.

19 October, quantum mechanics: what the files take of it is in the paper's own words, and the tags say so. A qubit is a two-level quantum system (the definition of Sec. II), so its states are linear combinations of its two outcomes and a tick is a linear map on them (`Qubit.Op`, `Qubit.Tick.phase_unique`); the amplitude of an outcome is the square root of its probability with the phase (Eq. (qubit)), and the probability is the outcome's share of the bits (Postulate 1), so the Born rule `Qubit.bornProb` and the code weights `Coding.weight` are definitions, not inputs; and the energy of a state that a tick multiplies by a phase is `ℏ` times that phase over the tick, the paper's definition (Sec. IV.E, `Qubit.Tick.energy`). No statement changed.

Then, with claims proved in Lean rather than tagged as definitions: the Born rule is no longer a hypothesis anywhere. The state at a crossing (`Solution.crossingState`) carries the string's thermal state itself in place of the count of its 1s, `state_same` and `solution_deSitter` take `hthermal`, that the qubit of each string is in the thermal state of its tick (thermodynamics), in place of `hborn`, and the state at every crossing is `(1/2, 1/2 · 1, 1/L)` by `Qubit.Tick.state_eq`. The paper's "half the bits of a string in the horizon's state are 1s" (Sec. II) is now a theorem: `Qubit.stringState` is the state of a string with `m` ones at the position `n`, Eq. (qubit) with the share `m/L` as the probability of the outcome `1` and the position as the phase (Postulate 1, Eq. (raqm)); `Qubit.share_stringState` gives `1` the probability `m/L` in it; `Qubit.expected_share` shows that under any mixture of strings the expected share is the Born probability of `1` in the mixture's density matrix; and `Qubit.half_bits_thermal` gives `1/2` for the thermal state, with `Qubit.half_bits_model` meeting its hypothesis. `Capacity.outcomeProb`, which no theorem used any more, is removed. The ledger holds 493 main theorems. With the author's text of the same day, Postulate 1 says "A state is a linear combination of outcomes of its qubits", which is the linearity the files use (`Qubit.Vec`, `Qubit.Op`, `Qubit.Tick.phase_unique`), and Sec. II defines energy, in the paper's review of the same evening "A string whose tick is the shift (Postulate 1) times a phase has energy ℏ times that phase over the tick", which is `Qubit.Tick.energy` to the word, the shift's turn left out by the definition and Postulate 2 given as the reason in Sec. IV.E; the docstrings and the rows above cite them, and no definition of the paper is tagged as an input any more.

After the whole package was gone over once more (19 October, night) for anything hanging and any extra premise: the machine checks were rerun on every entry (no unused or idle hypothesis, no dead code, no two hypotheses that contradict each other, no conclusion that restates a hypothesis), two independent referees read every file against the paper and this README, and their 30 findings were verified one by one. What changed: (1) the CI's ledger check counted only the lines that show all three axioms, so the two entries on `[propext, Quot.sound]` would have failed it; it now checks that no other axiom appears and that every entry is reported. (2) `π` and `ln 2` are Mathlib's (`Reg.pi`, `Reg.ln2`), so the hypotheses `hpi` and `hln` and the `π`, `ln 2` parts of `hconst` are gone from twenty theorems; `hconst` names the constants of nature `c`, `ℏ`, `k_B` alone. (3) Five hypotheses that followed from the others are removed: `4 ≤ L` of `route_B_fraction`, `Sin_from_count` and `coefficient_from_count` (from `1 ≤ n` and `4n < L`), and `a t ≠ 0` of `w_exact` and `w_exact_in_time` (from `ȧ/a ≠ 0`). (4) Every step of the energy ladder is a tick (`Qubit.Tick.ofStep`, `Tick.phase_ofStep`), so the values of a free string's energy are exactly the `L²` steps (`ladder_values`), which `hN` of `string_energy` had taken. (5) `Lambda_constant` takes `L` and the cell (`hL`, `hℓc`) and derives the radius's constancy itself, in place of taking it. (6) `Solution.chain_from_sources` derives the three results that the chain's theorems keep as hypotheses for brevity, the horizon at `R_Λ` (`hdS`), Jacobson's step with the count's density (`hJ`, from `hJac`, the two clauses of Postulate 2 and `hcount`) and the lap form of the horizon's temperature (`I1`), and applies them to `G`, `Λ`, `GΛ`, `k_BT_dS` and the vacuum's energy. (7) The range of the phase, `[0, 2π)`, is the choice of the definition `Qubit.Tick.phase`, which no postulate makes; the docstrings and the rows above said it was justified by the sense of time, which does not pick the turn, and the README said the zero was derived. They now say what is so: the choice sets the zero and the top of the ladder, and the measured `Ω_Λ` fixes it, every other turn putting a string at rest at `2πmL k_BT_dS` (`Qubit.Tick.phase_turn`, `rest_energy_turn`, `Numerics.other_turn_excluded`, with `zero_of_energy_tested`). (8) `hGibbs` was tagged thermodynamics alone. With the author's reading of the same night, that the Boltzmann weighting applies to energies and the states of a level share it, the weighting is thermodynamics' canonical distribution over the string's energy levels (`Thermal.canonProb`), with one multiplicity for every value by Postulate 1 (`Qubit.Tick.energy_eq_ofStep_iff`), so that the Boltzmann factor stands alone (`canonProb_const_eq_ladderProb`); what the paper adds, its fifth input, is that the weighting carries no entropy of the string's, which keeps one bit, though the weighting's Gibbs entropy would exceed 200 bits; the docstrings and the rows say so. (9) The hypothesis of `Crossing.Drawing.strings_between` is renamed `hspread`, since it shared the name `halike` of Postulate 1's hypothesis and is a premise of none of the sources; nothing of the chain rests on it. (10) Docstrings that called energy the generator of the tick now cite Postulate 1's definition of energy, Sec. II; the README and the docstrings say that the register's horizon is `L/4` cells out where the solution puts it (`coefficient_from_count`), that the share of a string's bit is over the half the observer can read (general relativity's horizon, with Postulate 1), that the area law is derived without a mass and had been applied with one (derived too in (11) below), and that the third parts of the `_of_rings` models are the algebra of `sin_rotation_mean`; a stale Ledger comment on isometries, the model docstring of `solution_deSitter` and the source label of the direction of time are corrected. The definitions of Sec. II (cell, ring, tick, largest ring, qubit, global phase, energy) are part of the postulates, since the postulates are stated in their terms and nothing can be postulated in a vacuum; the rows and docstrings that cite the definition of a tick or of energy call them Postulate 1's. (11) The area law with a mass (Secs. IV.C and IV.D), which the files had taken as a step of the paper in the definitions `Ns` and `ringShare` applied at the horizon's and the spheres' areas, is derived: the entropy density is one law on every local horizon (`Symmetry.one_density`, Postulate 2), the count's `η`, to which Jacobson's step ties `G` (`hJ`), and the areas are the solution's; at that density the horizon drawn in by the mass holds `L_M²/4` bits, a sphere's shortfall in area about the mass is the entropy removed, and a black hole's horizon holds `L'²/4` bits (`Symmetry.massive_horizon_density`, `shortfall_density`, `black_hole_density`, new). (12) The author's reading of 19 October, late, that the `L²` values are energy levels and not quantum states of the string, is proved: every value's thermal state is half the identity, so under any weighting of the values the string's state is half the identity and holds one bit (`Qubit.Tick.weighted_state`, `Reg.weighted_one_bit`, new); the docstrings had said, with no theorem, that the weighting's own Gibbs entropy would exceed `200` bits, and `Numerics.ladder_entropy_bits` (new, with `Thermal.ladderEntropy`, `ladderEntropy_ge_log`, `partition_ge`, `ladderProb_sum`) proves it. What stays the paper's step, its fifth input, is that the Boltzmann factor weights the values, which both states share, rather than states. The ledger holds 478 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes; the fresh-unpack build gives the same ledger.

After the finding of 20 October, night, that nothing about a free string's energy depends on whether the string reverses: `hlap` is a statement about `L` ticks of a free string's tick, in which the shift carries the string once around, and a reversal is not the shift, so it is an interaction (Postulate 1) and no part of a lap. A string moving the other way, written in the same frame, is carried by the backward shift (`Qubit.shiftBack`, `shiftBack_eq`, `shiftBack_pow`), and its tick's factor is on the same ladder as a string moving forward, `c^{L²} = 1` (`Qubit.ladder_of_shift`, `Qubit.ladder_back`, `Qubit.ladder_back_step`, `Qubit.Tick.ladder`, new). That the two senses have the same factor stays `hsenses`, Postulate 2's clause on directions; the row of `hsenses` had said that `Qubit.lean` gives it too, which it does not, and now says so. The ledger holds 484 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes.

21 October: a string's energy is ℏ times the change of its global phase and not its value, on which Postulate 1 says no law depends. Sec. II now reads "A string whose tick is the shift (Postulate 1) and a change Δ of its global phase has energy ℏΔ over the tick". `Qubit.Tick.energy` was already ℏ times the angle of the tick's factor, the change per tick, over `t_c`; its docstring, the header of `Qubit.lean` and the row above take the new words. No statement changed.

22 October, a free string's energy taken as one Landauer energy and the weighting dropped: the paper's fifth input, the Gibbs weighting of the values of a free string's energy (`hGibbs`), is removed with what rested on it: `Cosmo.string_energy`, `weighted_one_bit`, `heat_share` and `heat_share_iff`, `Qubit.Tick.weighted_state` and `Qubit.Tick.energy_eq_ofStep_iff` (with `Qubit.Tick.phase_eq_of_scalar`, which only it used), the whole of `Thermal.lean`, and `Numerics.string_energy_precision`, `string_energy_precision_of_le`, `ladder_entropy_bits`, `other_turn_excluded`, `zero_of_energy_tested` and `vacuum_share`. In their place, from Postulates 1 and 2 with the solution without a mass: the vacuum's energy is one Landauer energy per string (`vacuum_per_string`); a free tick's energy lies within half a step of it (`landauer_step`); only the turn of no change comes within a step of it (`turn_by_gravity`); so, with the free strings holding the Misner–Sharp energy as closely as the ladder allows (`hnearest`), the turn is that of no change and each string holds `k_BT ln 2` to within half a step (`string_landauer`, with `landauer_model` for its premise; `Numerics.landauer_precision`). The Hubble sphere of a flat universe holds one Landauer energy per string it counts (`fFRW_metric`, `hubble_misner_sharp`, `hubble_deSitter`), and the vacuum's share is the Hubble sphere's share of the strings, `Ω_Λ = (R_H/R_Λ)² = N_s(R_H)/(L²/4)` (`vacuum_share_hubble`), measured, not derived; `Numerics.energy_today` keeps the energy inside `R_Λ` today. The paper's text still states the weighting and the share `ln 2`; its changes wait for the author. The ledger holds 472 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes.

After two independent reviews of that change (23 October): the premise of `string_landauer` had been tagged "Postulates 1 and 2" and the outside inputs counted as four. Wrong: it is the paper's fifth input in place of the weighting, of the same standing, and not reduced (the row above; with the Komar energy in place of Misner–Sharp the same premise forces `m = −1`, scratch). The README's reason for it, that Jacobson's step has the energy crossing horizons as its source, contradicted Sec. III (the `Λ` term has no flux) and is withdrawn. The premise is weakened to what the proof uses, `hvac` (the strings hold the Misner–Sharp energy to within half a step each; `hnearest`, the nearest value of the ladder, implied it), and the "alike" half is derived from Postulate 2 (`Solution.energy_same`, new, as `Symmetry.one_density`). The ledger holds 473 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes.

23 October, the definition of energy made compatible with quantum mechanics, by the reading below ("Route B"): in quantum mechanics energy is the generator of the tick, and the free tick of earlier versions, `e^{iθ} · diag(1, e^{2πi/L})`, has the eigenstrings of all 1s and all −1s `2πE_c/L = 2πk_BT_dS` apart, so "the shift's own turn is no part of the energy" was not a reading quantum mechanics allows, and the thermal state of that qubit at `T_dS` would hold `0.02` bits, not one. `Qubit.lean` is rewritten on the reading that the two outcomes of a free string's qubit are the two senses of its ring (the register paper's doublet, Sec. 6.1 there), which the paper's -6-38 states in Sec. II: the free tick keeps the string of all 1s up to a factor (`hkeep`, Postulate 1's shift clause), the reversal of the ring's direction exchanges the senses and leaves the tick unchanged (`hdir`, Postulate 2, exactly, the factor being the energy), and the lap clause is taken for the string of all 1s (`hlap`); `swap`, `swap_mul_swap`, `swap_mulVec_e₁`, `eq_diagonal_of_keeps`, `Tick.comm_swap`, `Tick.senses_equal`, `Tick.unitary` and `prob_eq_bornProb` are new, and `Config`, `commonShift`, `Physical`, `tick`, `tick_physical`, `law_tick`, `eq_smul_one_of_forall`, `shift`, `shift_pow`, `shift_exists_inv`, `shiftBack`, `shiftBack_eq`, `shiftBack_pow`, `ladder_of_shift`, `ladder_back`, `ladder_back_step` and `Tick.ladder` are gone. The sign is that of quantum mechanics, `U = e^{−iθ} • 1` with `E = ℏθ/t_c`, and Sec. II of -6-38 defines energy with it. Every theorem outside `Qubit.lean` keeps its statement and its proof; only the Fano model of `Solution.solution_deSitter_model` changes its tick to `1`. Two independent referees read the new file against the postulates' words. The first found no error; its documentation findings (the exactness of `hdir`, the reading to be stated in Sec. II, the sign to be worded in Sec. II, two inherited docstrings that overclaimed, unitarity claimed but not stated) are applied. The second found no error either, and two reductions, applied: the string of all −1s is the reversal's image of the string of all 1s, so its field (`hkeep₂`) is a theorem (`Tick.senses_equal`), and the lap clause is needed for the string of all 1s only, so `hlap` takes the postulate's sentence for that string verbatim; `Tick` now takes three clauses, once each. Its documentation points (the hypothesis `U = 1` is "no change of the global phase", not "the state left as it is"; the linearity of the tick is quantum mechanics, which Postulate 1 takes as given; the same tick acts at each tick of a lap; `prob` is the Born probability, now `prob_eq_bornProb`; `swap_diagonal_swap` was dead code and is gone; the `DotProduct` import was unused and is gone) are applied too. The ledger holds 470 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes.

After the author's correction of 23 October, night, that `hvac` is not an input beside the postulates: "everything physical is qubits" leaves no other holder for the vacuum's energy, and which energy is not a choice, the energy of the solution's interior being its density times its volume, `ρ_Λ V`, the Misner–Sharp energy, while the Komar quantity `ρ + 3p` is the gravitating mass. `hvac` is retagged as Postulate 1's first sentence, the thesis applied as a premise as for `hgeom` and `hDet`, with general relativity's `ρ_Λ V` taken as known; the rows, docstrings and this README no longer call it the paper's fifth input or unreduced. With it the zero of energy, which `turn_by_gravity` derives from `hvac`, rests on the same sources. No statement changed.

After the question of 23 October, night, why the Boltzmann weighting of the senses was tagged thermodynamics, the thermal state then taken once (item 26 of the premises list): the senses of a free string are the two outcomes of its qubit (Sec. II), so their probabilities are the diagonal of the thermal state of its tick, `Qubit.Tick.state`, and the former hypotheses `hBoltz` (the senses weighted by Boltzmann factors, with the definition `Bridge.thermalProb`) and `hsenses` (the senses' energies equal) are replaced by `hq : ∀ l, q l = Qubit.prob ((T l).state β ℏ tc) 0` in `Bridge.horizon_entropy`, `horizon_counts_every_ring`, `Cosmo.vacuum_energy` and `Solution.chain_from_sources`, and dropped from `Solution.state_same`, `solution_deSitter` and `chain_from_sources`, whose crossing state `Solution.crossingState` loses its separate sense entry, now the diagonal of the qubit's state (`Op × ℝ` in place of `ℝ × Op × ℝ`); the equality of the senses' energies is the theorem `Qubit.Tick.senses_equal`, from `hdir` of `Qubit.Tick`, Postulate 2's clause taken once. `Reg.senses_half_of_equal` and `Reg.senses_equal_weight` are gone with them. No conclusion changed. The ledger holds 468 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes.

24 October, how Postulate 1 is to be read (item 27 of the premises list): Postulate 1 keeps its words, which are the register paper's. Its shift is a translation along the ring; a translation along a ring can go two ways, with the ring or against it, and reading which sense a string has is a measurement with two values, the register paper's doublet (Sec. 6.1 there), so the two outcomes of a string's qubit are its two senses, derived in Sec. II of the paper's -6-38-3 rather than taken as a reading. No bit moves: a string of one sense has the same bit on every cell, so the shift leaves it as it is up to its global phase, and that phase changes alike in either sense, so a free tick changes nothing of a horizon string but the global phase common to both senses, which is what `hkeep`, `hdir` and `Qubit.Tick.phase_unique` say. The docstrings of `Qubit.lean`, `Cosmo.lean`, `Chain.lean`, `Ledger.lean`, `Capacity.lean`, `Galactic.lean`, `Symmetry.lean` and `RouteB.lean`, and the rows of this table, no longer speak of bits moving one cell per tick or of a reading the files take; `hkeep` and `hlap` are Postulate 1's shift and lap sentences for the string of all 1s, and `hdir` is Postulate 2's clause on directions. No statement, proof or hypothesis changed. The ledger holds 468 main theorems; `lake build --wfail` passes.
