**v18-19 (27 October 2026).** General relativity enters only as published theorems whose inputs the files prove or take from the postulates. Hawking and Ellis's classification (`GR.classification`) is gone: it drops the completeness that the Killing–Hopf theorem needs, since the steady-state half of de Sitter space is homogeneous, of constant curvature and incomplete. In its place the solution without a mass is found from the space at an instant of an observer at rest (`Space.lean`): the Killing–Hopf theorem for that space, complete since every geodesic of it closes with a ring (`GR.space_form`); the slice's rings, which exclude the flat and hyperbolic forms (`Space.flat_space_no_rings`, `Space.hyperbolic_space_no_rings`, `Space.slice_sphere`); Einstein's constraint, `3K = Λ` (`GR.constraint`); the slice a Cauchy surface of the solution, from Postulate 1 (`P1.cauchy`); the development of Choquet-Bruhat and Geroch (`GR.development`); and the solution's rings, which fill de Sitter space and make the group trivial (`Space.fills_deSitter`, `Space.development_deSitter`, with `Quotients.deSitter_rings`). Jacobson's step is taken at every point (`Thermo.clausius`) in place of its conclusion on `G` (`Thermo.jacobson`), and without the conservation law Jacobson also takes: `Einstein.lean` proves that a symmetric form vanishing on the null vectors is a multiple of the metric, so the step gives the field equations up to a multiple of the metric; without matter Einstein's constancy theorem makes the multiple one constant (`GR.einstein_constancy`, `Einstein.vacuum_einstein`), and the static weak field with Newton's law gives `G = c³k_B/4ℏη` (`GR.weak_field`, `Einstein.G_of_clausius`). Removed: `Solution.solution_cases`, `solution_deSitter`, `determined_deSitter` and `solution_deSitter_model`, with the premises `Thermo.jacobson`, `GR.classification`, `P1.space_qubits` and the unused `P2.rings_geodesics`. New: `Einstein.lean`, `Space.lean`, and in `Solution.lean` `geometry_same_slots`, `vacuum_without_mass`, `jacobson_G_from_sources`, `cosmological_constant_from_sources` and `cosmological_constant_model` with its lemmas. `Main.jacobson_G`, `Main.chain` and `Main.deSitter_horizon` take the new premises; `Main.cosmological_constant` is unchanged, and its docstring now says what it shows, that de Sitter space with `Λ = 3/R_Λ²` is the one maximally symmetric spacetime with the rings. The ledger has 523 entries; the premise ledger checks the same twenty results.

**v18-20 (27 October 2026).** The first law applies to every small region and for every observer, as the paper's thermodynamic input states it. `Thermo.first_law_local` states it: the divergence of every observer's energy current vanishes, so `∇^aT_ab = 0` (`Einstein.eq_zero_of_timelike`). With the contracted Bianchi identity (`GR.bianchi`, in place of Einstein's constancy theorem, `GR.einstein_constancy`), Jacobson's step now gives Einstein's equations with one constant `Λ` at every point, with matter and without (`Einstein.einstein_equations`, `Einstein.FieldEquations`, `Main.einstein`); where there is no matter they are the vacuum equations (`Einstein.vacuum_of_field_equations`). `Einstein.Spacetime` carries the divergence of the stress tensor (`divStress`). The first law with the Leibniz rule for a flow (`GR.leibniz`) moves matter on geodesics (`Einstein.flow_geodesic`, `Main.matter_geodesic`) and, with Postulate 1's light that crosses a cell in one tick (`P1.light`), light on null geodesics (`Einstein.null_dust_of_current`, `Einstein.null_geodesic`, `Main.light_rays`). `Motion.lean` (new) proves `v² = gr` on circular orbits, and `Main.rotation_curve` derives it for the bodies about a galaxy, with the Newtonian limit of geodesic motion (`GR.newtonian_motion`); `Main.galactic` takes these speeds (`Derived.circular_speeds`) and adds that `v²` tends to `√(a_M GM)`. `Schwarzschild.lean` (new) solves the vacuum equations for a static, spherically symmetric metric whose curvature is the textbook's (`GR.static_spherical`), and `Main.schwarzschild_deSitter` derives that about a body at rest, alone, the metric function is `Reg.fSdS`; the solution is no longer quoted. New conditions: matter moving as dust (`Condition.dust_flow`, `Condition.dust_orbits`), circular orbits, no matter outside the body, and Schwarzschild's unit of time. Removed: `Einstein.ric_eq_smul_eta_of_vacuum`, `Einstein.vacuum_einstein` and the premise `GR.einstein_constancy`. The ledger has 542 entries; the premise ledger checks twenty-five results.

Along a family of solutions about a body at rest whose mass grows from zero (`Condition.body_family`), each Schwarzschild–de Sitter with its own constant, the observer's cosmological horizon is thermal at the temperature of its period (`Thermo.horizon_thermal_family`) and obeys `d(Mc²) = −T dS` (`Thermo.horizon_clausius`); that relation is `dm = (r/2)f′(r)dr` (`Reg.clausius_geometric`), the horizon condition gives `dm = (r/2)f′(r)dr − (r³/6)dΛ` (`Reg.deriv_lambda_zero`), so `Λ` does not change (`Reg.lambda_with_mass`) and has its value without a mass (`Reg.lambda_with_mass_deSitter`, `Main.lambda_with_mass`, with the field about the body `GM/r² − c²r/R_Λ²`). `Main.horizon_drawn_in` (new) states that the cosmological horizon about the body is drawn in by `GM/c²`. `Derived.deSitter_horizon` now covers the constant about a mass, and the docstrings of `push`, `zero_gravity`, `sds_horizon_shift` and `sds_outer_horizon`, which take it for the solution with the mass, say so. **v18-28 (28 October 2026).** Item 49 of the premises list: Jacobson's step at every point, `Sources.Thermo.clausius`, held three premises in one definition under thermodynamics. It is now three premises, each under its own source, and a result derived from them. `Sources.Geometry.raychaudhuri`: the local Rindler horizon through a point, generated by a null `k`, changes its area as `δA = −∫ λ R_ab k^a k^b dλ dA` [Wald (1984), Sec. 9.2]. `Sources.P2.local_entropy`: its entropy is the register's, the density `η₀` of the count's law times its area. `Sources.Thermo.local_clausius`: the energy that crosses it, the boost-energy flux of matter, `−(a/c²)∫ λ T_ab k^a k^b dλ dA` for the boost of acceleration `a`, is heat at its Unruh temperature, `δQ = T dS`. `Main.jacobson_step` derives from the three `R_ab k^a k^b = (2πk_B/ℏcη₀) T_ab k^a k^b` for every null `k`, the acceleration cancelling, as `Sources.Derived.clausius`, which the results take in place of the old premise. The derivation needs `η₀ ≠ 0`, which the old premise had hidden: for `η₀ = 0` it asserted `R_ab k^a k^b = 0`. So `Main.einstein`, `Main.schwarzschild_deSitter` and `Main.deSitter_horizon` take the density positive as a condition, `Solution.vacuum_without_mass` and `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources` take it not zero, and `Solution.chain_from_sources` derives it from the count's density. The ledger has 563 entries. The paper's -6-38-18 carries the text: Table I's footnote gives the ledger count, 563; Sec. III already named the three inputs of Jacobson's step.

(7) `Premises.lean` allows the classes `NeZero` and `Nonempty` and the object types `Geodesic.Curve`, `Quaternion`, `Finset`, `AddSubgroup`, `ZMod`, `Coding.Word`, `Positions.Symmetry`, `Coding.LineCode` and `Entropy.gibbs`. New internal imports: `Sources.lean` imports `Weighting.lean` (in place of `Center.lean`) and `Coding.lean`; `Main.lean` imports `TwoCells.lean`. The ledger has 529 entries (444 theorems, 61 lemmas, 24 definitions), and the premise ledger checks eighty results. Unused by any proof and kept: the field `keeps_order` of `Positions.Symmetry`, the positions part of the clause that no cell differs from another (the premises list records it). The paper's -6-38-26 carries the text: Sec. III's fourth source as the mathematics named above, with its references; the totally geodesic spheres at the horizons, used nowhere, removed; items 77–79.

**v18-41 (30 October 2026).** The positions part of Postulate 2's clause that no cell differs from another dropped, since no theorem used it (item 80: the words "and the positions along them up to one common shift", carried as the field `keeps_order` of `Positions.Symmetry`). The symmetry is now a structure of the register alone, `Horizon.Symmetry` (a map of the cells with a map of the largest rings that keeps which cells lie on which ring, `mem_iff`), and the clause's register part is `Horizon.Homogeneous`; `Sources.P2.homogeneous` no longer takes the positions, and the ten results of `Main.lean` that took the positions only for the symmetry (`jacobson_G_density`, `jacobson_G`, `jacobson_G_exact`, `chain`, `deSitter_horizon`, `landauer`, `einstein`, `schwarzschild_deSitter`, `massive_horizon`, `black_hole_horizon`) no longer take them. The models prove the symmetry without the field (`fanoSym`, `dsSym`), and `fano_pos_shift` and `dsPos_shift`, which proved the field, are gone; `register_of_diffSet`, `Singer.homogeneous_register`, `Singer.homogeneous_register_of_prime_power` and `Main.register_in_band` state the positions as `Nonempty H.Positions` beside `H.Homogeneous`. No proof of any theorem changed: the build passes as it was, the axiom ledger is unchanged (529 entries), and the premise ledger differs only by the ten `P` objects and the shorter type of `hsym`. Every declaration of the files is now reached by a result of `Main.lean`, a certified number, a model or a theorem the paper cites by name, except two `rfl` lemmas that `simp` uses and the tactic `reg_facts`. The paper's -6-38-27 drops the words from Postulate 2 and from Sec. II's sentence on the register of a prime-power order.

**v18-42 (30 October 2026).** No declaration changed. The docstrings of `Qubit.lean` and `Symmetry.lean` and this README no longer cite the author's rulings as the ground of a premise or of a closed item of the premises list; each gives the ground itself: the definitions of Sec. II are part of Postulate 1's content because the postulate is stated in their terms; the first sentence of Postulate 1 applied to a named thing asserts of it only what the sentence asserts of everything physical, that it is read off the register; the first law applies to every small region and observer as the paper's thermodynamic input states it; the Clausius relation on the cosmological horizon is Sec. III's input as written, so it has no work term; the tolerance of `hvac` is the accuracy to which the vacuum's energy is measured, the only sense in which an equality with a measured value can be claimed, and no result uses its value (`Reg.turn_by_gravity`); `c`, `ℏ` and `k_B` are constant as the defining constants of the SI; the fourth source is mathematics because Einstein's equations are derived (`Main.einstein`); the positions part of Postulate 2's clause is dropped because no theorem used it. The entries of this changelog from v11.2.11-16 on are reworded the same way, and those of v18-22 to v18-41 are in order of version. The ledger keeps its 529 entries and `#premises` its 80 results.

**v18-43 (30 October 2026).** Three premises that a docstring tied to their source are tied to it in Lean. (a) `Qubit.Tick` takes the paper's sentence on a free tick in its words (Sec. II: the shift leaves a string of one sense as it is up to its global phase, Postulate 1, and that phase changes alike in either sense, since no law depends on the ring's direction, Postulate 2): the field `hsense`, that the tick keeps the string of all 1s and the string of all −1s up to one and the same factor, replaces `hkeep` and `hdir`; `hdir` had read the clause on directions as the tick along the reversed ring, `swap * U * swap`, being the tick, exactly and not up to a phase, a reading argued in the docstring, from which `comm_swap` and `senses_equal` derived the equal factors. `phase_unique` now follows from `hsense` alone; `swap`, `swap_mul_swap`, `swap_mulVec_e₁`, `Tick.comm_swap` and `Tick.senses_equal` are removed, no result reaching them. (b) `Sources.P1.rings_along` (`Symmetry.ringsAlong`) states once that a largest ring runs along every geodesic of a space, for a set of geodesics and a closing predicate; `P1.slice_qubits`, `P1.slice_form_qubits` and `P1.solution_qubits` are its applications to the space at an instant, to its space form (`Space.SpaceForm.slots`, `Space.SpaceForm.closes`, new, in place of the match of `Space.SliceIsQubits`) and to the solution (`Space.solCloses`, new, in `Space.SolIsQubits`), so that the three premises are one sentence applied three times, which unfolding checks rather than a docstring asserts. (c) `Sources.P1.qubits` states Postulate 1's first sentence once, a quantity read off what the register holds at its items (`Symmetry.IsQubits`, which it is); `P1.geometry_qubits` is its application to the geometry of space, and `P1.no_rotation_preferred` its application to the weighting of the drawing's rotations, with nothing as the content: the weighting seen from any rotation is the weighting, so it is left invariant (`P1.no_rotation_preferred.invariant`, new, in place of taking invariance as the premise), and Haar uniqueness does the rest as before. The premise ledger prints as before; the axiom ledger has 526 entries, all on the three standard axioms or none; `#premises` checks its 80 results; every declaration is reached. The paper's -6-38-28 prints the count.

**v18-44 (30 October 2026).** The last sentence of Postulate 2 at the level of cells: "Every largest ring is a closed geodesic of space, made of its cells as segments of length `ℓ_c`" (the paper's -6-38-29), the sentence that joins the register to the manifold now placing the cells on the geodesic and not only the ring. No statement changes: `P2.rings_geodesics` takes the ring as a closed spacelike geodesic of length `Lℓ_c` as before; `P2.ring_distance`, that a ring's cells measure proper distance along it, is now the sentence's own words rather than its ring-level form with Postulate 1's cell length; `P1.light` reads "light crosses a cell in one tick" with the cell a segment of length `ℓ_c` of a geodesic, so light moves at `c`; `P1.local_entropy` quotes Sec. III.B's new sentence, that a local Rindler horizon is crossed by largest rings whose cells are segments of the geodesics there and that its state is a state of those strings, so that its entropy per unit area is a law, and names what it still takes: `dS = η₀ dA` with the uniform `η₀`, the files having no map from the manifold's points to the cells; `P1.vacuum_energy_held` names the strings inside the horizon through their cells. The docstrings that quote the sentence (`Homogeneous.lean`, `Horizon.lean`, `Chain.lean`, `Geodesics.lean`, `Ledger.lean`, `Symmetry.lean`, `TwoCells.lean`) and this README quote it in full. The ledger keeps its 526 entries and `#premises` its 80 results.

**v18-45 (31 October 2026).** Postulate 1's first sentence reworded in the paper's -6-38-30, "Every physical law and quantity is defined by qubits" in place of "Everything physical is qubits", so that it quantifies over what the files can give a form to, and taken in Lean in three forms, each the sentence for one kind of thing it speaks of, so that every premise joining a law or a quantity to the register is the sentence applied and checked by unfolding. A law is a function of the register's crossings, the type every law has. `Sources.P1.qubits` (unchanged) is the sentence for a quantity that varies over the register's items. `Sources.P1.physical manifold register`, new, is the sentence for a quantity to which another description assigns a value: the two values are equal, a difference being a physical quantity not defined by qubits; `physical_within ε` is its form for a register value on the ladder. `P1.local_entropy` is restated as `physical (dS p k) (η₀ dA p k)` and `P1.vacuum_energy_held` as `physical_within ε (EMS) (Σ Es)`, each unfolding to its former statement, so no theorem changes (`Main.jacobson_step` names the unfolding in one `show`). The docstrings of `Sources.lean`, `Symmetry.lean`, `Solution.lean`, `Bridge.lean`, `Chain.lean`, `Center.lean`, `Cosmo.lean`, `Entropy.lean` and `Ledger.lean` quote the new sentence; `hvac`'s docstring gives the ladder as the ground of its tolerance, `k_BT_dS ln 2` needing `j = L ln 2/2π` steps, never a whole number. The ledger keeps its 526 entries and `#premises` its 80 results; every declaration is reached.

**v18-46 (1 November 2026).** The solution dropped from the premises of the chain: the space at an instant alone fixes `Λ`, and no Cauchy surface, no development and no rings of the solution are taken. `Space.sphere_rings`, new, proves that the rings of the space at an instant exclude every quotient of the three-sphere. Every great circle closes in the quotient with one length, so every map of the group is `±1` on space: a map that is neither turns some plane by an angle below `2π` (`Quotients.exists_rotation_plane`), and the circle of that plane would close sooner than the circle of a plane that no map but `±1` turns onto itself, which `Quotients.exists_plane_not_turned`, new, gives (of the planes of `cos ψ e₀ + sin ψ e₁` and `e₂`, `0 ≤ ψ < π`, each map carries at most one onto itself). And `−1` on space is excluded by the register: every ring has half its cells inside every observer's horizon, a quarter lap out (`Bridge.horizon_counts_every_ring`), so by Postulate 2's last sentence every great circle comes within a quarter of a ring's length of every point, `Space.WithinQuarter`, the new premise `hhor` (`Sources.P2.ring_within_horizon`, Postulate 2's last sentence for the count's theorem; renamed in v18-47), which the circle of a plane orthogonal to a point does not with the antipodal points identified. `Space.cosmological_constant_of_sources`, `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources`, `Solution.chain_from_sources`, `Main.chain` and `Main.deSitter_horizon` take `hhor` in place of `hcauchy`, `hdev` and `hsol`, whose definitions `Sources.P1.cauchy`, `Sources.Geometry.development` and `Sources.P1.solution_qubits` are removed with `Space.dsQuotient_of_sphere`, `Space.fills_deSitter`, `Space.development_deSitter`, `Space.solSlots`, `Space.solCloses`, `Space.SolIsQubits` and the model's solution `Solution.modelSol`; `Quotients.deSitter_rings` stays, for `Symmetry.cosmological_constant` on the maximally symmetric spacetimes. The model `Solution.cosmological_constant_model` meets `hhor` on the three-sphere of radius `R_Λ`, where `Lℓ_c/4` is a quarter turn and a great circle and its opposite point cannot both lie beyond a point. The references to Choquet-Bruhat and Geroch and to Ringström go with the development; Einstein's constraint stays, as Lorentzian geometry of the slice. No conclusion changes; the hypothesis lists of `Main.chain` and `Main.deSitter_horizon` do. The ledger keeps 526 entries (439 theorems, 62 lemmas, 25 definitions) and `#premises` its 80 results; every declaration is reached. Goes with the paper's -6-38-31.

**v18-47 (1 November 2026).** The independent review of v18-46 (1 November) applied. The premise `hhor` justified the distance `L/4` by the observer's horizon, which the files place from the solution (`Horizon.Positions.weight`, `Main.volume_law`), a circle in the justification though not in any proof; the register fact needed is horizon-free, and is now a theorem: every largest ring has a cell within `L/4` steps of any cell, since its cells come in opposite pairs at the distances `d` and `L/2 − d` (`Horizon.Positions.cell_within_quarter`, from `dist_opp`; restated from the sources as `Main.cell_within_quarter_lap`, from Postulate 2's incidence and the positions). The premise is renamed `Sources.P2.ring_within_quarter_lap` (`hquarter`) and its docstring grounds it on that theorem, states that where the solution puts the horizon is Sec. IV.D's result and no input, and adds that a point of space is a cell, the bound holding to within a cell for points taken inside their segments. The README's stale lines on the solution `sol` and `embedU` are gone, "its rings alone" reads "its rings, with a theorem of the register", and "no result changes" reads "no conclusion changes". The ledger has 528 entries (441 theorems, 62 lemmas, 25 definitions) and `#premises` 81 results; every declaration is reached. Goes with the paper's -6-38-32 (T40: Sec. IV.D names the one-parameter family of planes with its reason and splits two long sentences; Sec. III says what Sec. IV.D derives of the three-sphere; the horizon paragraph names the solution without a mass).

**v18-48 (1 November 2026).** The premise on light, `hlight` (`Sources.P1.light`), written in the first sentence's `physical` form and grounded in its docstring. Its two parts are two sentences of Postulate 1 read with Postulate 2's last sentence. The ray: light crosses a cell, a segment of length `ℓ_c` of a geodesic, in one tick, so it moves at `c` along a geodesic in every direction, a null direction in the frame the files use at a point; the words themselves, as `hx` is. The energy: a quantity defined by its qubits (the first sentence), which the files read as going with the light, from cell to cell, one per tick along the ring, so that the manifold's energy current of the light for every observer, `T_ab u^b`, is along the ray; that reading is the step the premise takes, since Sec. II defines the energy of a state, the generator of its tick, and places it in no cell, so the register's side enters as the ray's direction only, not as a register object as in `hent` and `hvac`; the point is one where this light alone passes, as `Condition.dust_flow`'s is one where matter moves as dust, and "for every observer" holds since the events of the ray are the same for every observer (the independent review of 1 November named these three). The fourth conjunct of `P1.light` is now written as `physical (T u) (s • η k)`, for which `Sources.P1.physical` takes values of any one type instead of reals (`hent` and `hvac` unchanged; every use unfolds as before). `Main.light_rays` concludes `IsNull k ∧ ∃ l, A = l • η k`, the null geodesic the paper states, the null conjunct of the premise, which the theorem did not use, restated in its conclusion. `Symmetry.lean`'s list of the sentence's instances names the energy of light. Two docstrings of `Einstein.lean` cite Sec. III for light, where the paper has it, not Sec. V. No conclusion changes beyond the added conjunct; the ledger keeps 528 entries and `#premises` its 81 results; every declaration is reached. Goes with the paper's -6-38-33 (T41: Sec. III's sentence on light gives the ground of its energy current, "its energy is defined by its qubits (Postulate 1), so it is at the cells the light crosses and moves with them, along the ray for every observer", and the lists of the first sentence's applications in Sec. III and the Data availability name the energy of light).

**v18-49 (2 November 2026).** The premise `hquarter` replaced by the postulates' words at the observer's cell, from which the exclusion of the antipodal quotient is now a theorem. `Space.RingsDrawn` (`hdrawn`, `Sources.P2.rings_drawn`) states, for the observer's cell `x₀` on the space at an instant once it is a quotient of the three-sphere, what Sec. II's ring and cell and Postulate 2's last sentence say of the rings through it: each largest ring through `x₀` runs along a great circle closing in the quotient with the length `Lℓ_c`, its cell `j` steps from `x₀` placed at the arc length `jℓ_c`, where its segment begins; and the cells of the drawn rings are places, Sec. II's "a cell is a place" read as an identity in both directions, the same cell drawn on two of the rings at one place and two cells at two places. The cells that two of the rings share are `x₀` and its opposite, one cell halfway around every largest ring through `x₀` (Postulate 2's first clause, `Horizon.Positions.offset_opp_self`, new, with `offset_self`, `offset_inj` and `exists_offset`; `Main.opposite_halfway` states it from the sources). At the observer's cell the files so take a cell's place to be a point of the manifold, for the rings through it. Every cell lies on a ring through `x₀`, so every cell is drawn, at one place; nothing is said of the rings off `x₀`, which, drawn through the places of their cells as well, would draw the register's incidence with its cells as points and its rings as great circles, which the Sylvester–Gallai theorem forbids for `L ≥ 6`. `Space.sphere_rings` derives from the drawn rings, with the one length of every great circle and the register's incidence, that `−1` on space is excluded: were it in the group, every great circle would close after half a turn and by `±1` at its least period, so `Lℓ_c = π√k`; the opposite cell, `L/2` steps on, would sit a quarter turn from `x₀` on every ring through it; two different rings through `x₀` (`Horizon.two_mul_card_through`), drawn through the one place of `x₀` and the one place of its opposite up to sign, would lie on one great circle, and the cell one step from `x₀` on one would fall on the place of the cell one step on, or one step back, on the other, two cells at one place, so one cell on both rings, while two rings through a cell share only it and its opposite (`Horizon.unique_loop`). `Space.sphereSlots_pos`, new, writes a great circle of the waist in its places. The drawn rings close with `Lℓ_c` and every great circle with `R.L ℓ_c`, so `hdrawn` with `hslice` gives `L = R.L`; the docstrings of the three results that take both say so. `Space.WithinQuarter`, `Sources.P2.ring_within_quarter_lap`, `Horizon.Positions.cell_within_quarter` and `Main.cell_within_quarter_lap` are removed; `Space.cosmological_constant_of_sources`, `Solution.cosmological_constant_from_sources`, `Solution.chain_from_sources`, `Main.chain` and `Main.deSitter_horizon` take the positions `P` and `hdrawn` at `x₀`. The model `Solution.rings_drawn_model` draws the three rings of the Fano register through `(0, false)` along the great circles through `R_Λe₀` in the directions `e₁`, `e₂`, `e₃`, their cells at the angles `jπ/3`, and proves that distinct cells sit at distinct points (`B0`, `e4`, `fanoDir`, `modelRing`, `modelPlace`, `fano_shared`, `fanoDir_inj`, `eq_of_cos_sin_eq`, `sin_mul_pi_div_three_eq_zero`, `angle_mem_Ico`). `Space.lean` imports `RouteB.lean` for the positions. The independent review of 2 November found the derivation sound and the model genuine, and these fixed: the docstrings had said that the premise places no cell off the rings through `x₀`, where every cell lies on one; the reading behind the direction `two cells are two places` and its scope are now named; Sylvester–Gallai is stated for `L ≥ 6`; the convention of a cell's place at its segment's beginning is named as a convention; the consequence `L = R.L`; the README's list of definitions, its model sentence for `Solution.lean`, and the phrase `with a theorem of the register` left from v18-47; two section comments of `Ledger.lean`; and `Horizon.lean`'s sentence that nothing draws the register. No conclusion changes. The ledger has 548 entries (441 theorems, 76 lemmas, 31 definitions) and `#premises` its 81 results; every declaration is reached. The paper's -6-38-33 still argues the exclusion by the quarter lap and says that the manifold does not describe the register's incidence (Secs. II and IV.D); its next version must carry the drawing argument and qualify those sentences, with the first clause's wording "the same cell" for its "the cell".

**v18-50 (2 November 2026).** No declaration changed. The docstrings and this README follow the paper's -6-38-34 (T42, item 92 of the premises list): Postulate 2's first clause now reads "Every cell has one opposite, the same cell halfway around every largest ring through it", and the docstrings that quote it (`RouteB.lean`, `Sources.lean`, `Space.lean`, `Main.lean`) quote the new words; Sec. II now states the scope that `Space.RingsDrawn` takes, "For an observer at rest, the manifold describes the register's incidence, that is, which rings share a cell, only for the rings through his cell. These rings are closed geodesics through his place, and they meet again at the opposite of his cell, the same cell halfway around each of them", which `Sources.P2.rings_drawn` and `Space.RingsDrawn` quote in place of v18-49's note that the paper still denied it; Secs. II and IV.C say that the drawing of the rings as great circles does not show which cells two rings share, except for the rings through an observer's cell; Sec. IV.D argues the exclusion of the antipodal quotient from the rings through an observer's cell, as `Space.sphere_rings` proves it, and Table I's footnote counts the 548 statements of the ledger. The independent review of 2 November found the argument of Sec. IV.D correct; its points on the Lean side are applied here: "None exists" for a drawing of the register is stated for `L ≥ 6`, with the reduction to one great two-sphere, in `Horizon.lean`; the files and the paper place cells, and so show which rings share a cell, only on the rings through the observer's cell, while `Crossing.Drawing` draws all the rings, as great circles, without their cells; the step from the two rings on one great circle to one shared cell is stated as `sphere_rings` proves it; `Horizon.lean` loses a stale paper version, and three docstrings lose stray line breaks. The paper no longer cites the Sylvester–Gallai theorem, since it states the scope and not why no more can be drawn; `Horizon.lean`, `Space.lean`, `Sources.lean` and this README cite it directly ([P. Erdős, Problem 4065, Amer. Math. Monthly 50, 65 (1943); R. Steinberg, solution, ibid. 51, 169 (1944)]) as the files' reason to place cells only on the rings through the observer's cell, and no longer say that the paper cites it. The ledger keeps its 548 entries and `#premises` its 81 results; every declaration is reached.

**v18-51 (2 November 2026).** Item 93 of the premises list: the count of a sphere's strings, Eq. (N), is the average over the drawing's rotations without a mass, and an observer at rest on the sphere or inside it can count more. For him the `L/2` rings through his cell pass through his place, so they are not turned with the rest of the drawing, and the other rings, whose position relative to his place is no part of the state, are turned with the weighting of the whole drawing and keep the average. `Crossing.Drawing.restrict` (new) draws some of the rings as in a drawing, and `Crossing.Drawing.strings_le_card` (new) bounds a sphere's strings by the number of rings. `Center.observer_strings_mean` (new) proves that his count is the average plus the number of his rings that cross the sphere, less `(L/2)(r/ρ)²`, and `Main.observer_count` (new) states it from the sources: his count exceeds `N_s(r)(1 − 2/L + 4/L²)` by at most `(L/2)(1 − r²/R_Λ²)`. `Reg.observer_rings` (new, `Chain.lean`) and `Main.observer_rings` (new) prove the arithmetic: that is the fraction `(R_Λℓ_c/πr²)(1 − r²/R_Λ²)` of `N_s(r)`, at most `R_Λℓ_c/πr²`, and zero at the horizon; `Numerics.observer_fraction_1m` (new) certifies `1.26×10⁻⁹` at `r = 1 m`. The docstring of `Approx.volume_law` says that `S_in` is the average over the rotations, and that of `Approx.removed_entropy` that the body is a point on spheres far larger than it; both now list `Main.saturated_no_equality` among the results that take the step. No other declaration changed. The ledger has 555 entries (446 theorems, 77 lemmas, 32 definitions) and `#premises` 83 results; every declaration is reached. Goes with the paper's -6-38-35 (T43: a paragraph after the area law of Sec. II states the observer's count and its bound, and that Sec. IV.C treats the body as a point, on spheres far larger than it; the hat-box sentence names the sphere's area and says "exactly when"; Sec. III reads Newton's law against the `N_s(r)` strings a sphere holds on average; Table I's footnote counts 555 statements).

**v18-52 (6 November 2026).** The last sentence of Postulate 1, the lap clause ("After a string has gone once around a ring of `n` cells, its global phase has changed by a whole multiple of `2π/n`"), dropped with the paper's -6-38-58: under the Schrödinger equation a string of energy `E` gains the global phase `Ent_c/ℏ` per lap, so the clause was a quantization rule on `E`, restricting it to the ladder `j·2πE_c/n²`, and nothing in Table I used it. The field `hlap` of `Qubit.Tick` is gone, and `hsense` now says of the factor what Sec. II's sentence says, that it is a global phase, a factor of modulus one by Sec. II's definition (`‖c‖ = 1`); the unitarity of the free tick, which `hlap` had supplied through `Tick.scalar_pow`, is now `hsense`'s (`Tick.norm_scalar`, by `Tick.scalar_eq` and `eq_smul_one_of_keeps`, new). Removed with the clause: `Tick.scalar_pow`, `Tick.phase_lap`, `Tick.ofStep`, `Tick.phase_ofStep`; `Reg.ladder_of_phase`, `ladder_of_lap`, `ladder_values`, `landauer_step`, `ladder_step_over_temperature`; `Main.ladder`; `Numerics.landauer_precision`, `ladder_entropy_bits`, `ladder_entropy_nats`, `ladder_entropy_exceeds_bit`; `Entropy.shannon_geometric_ge`. In their place: `Tick.ofPhase` and `Tick.phase_ofPhase` (new), the tick of any angle `θ` and its phase for `θ` below a turn, so a free string's energy, counted from no change, takes every value from `0` up to `2πE_c` (`Reg.tick_energy_range`, new); `Reg.turn_by_gravity` keeps its statement and is proved from that range alone; `Reg.landauer_tick` (new) gives the tick of phase `ln 2/L`, whose energy is exactly `k_BT_dS ln 2`, and `Reg.landauer_model` now says that `hvac` can be met exactly, at `ε = 0`, in place of "to within `10⁻⁶¹`"; the third conjunct of `Main.landauer` states that exact tick. The tolerance `ε` of `hvac` stays, as the accuracy to which the vacuum's energy is measured (item 47(b)); its docstrings no longer give the ladder as its reason. `Tick.exp_phase`, `turn_same` and `eq_phase` no longer need `[NeZero L]`. `Numerics.porter_thomas_bound` (new) certifies the numbers of the return bound above the ceiling that the paper's -6-38-57 added: under the Porter–Thomas distribution the lightest `15%` of the outcomes carry `1.2%` and the lightest `58%` carry `21%`, and the overlap with the random state is at most `0.988` at `205` qubits, `0.79` at `206`, `0.54` at `207` and `0.12` at `210`, the logarithm bounded by six terms of its series (`Real.abs_log_sub_add_sum_range_le`). `Cluster.lean` (new) carries the derivation of Sec. IV.A for a linear cluster state with one layer of generic rotations, which the paper's -6-38-46 added: the recursion `r_N = (1 − t r_{N−1})/(1 + t r_{N−1})` as the definition `moebius`, each map one to one (`moebius_injOn`), a collision of the two maps only from values in the ratio `−|t₀|²` (`moebius_collision`, `ratio_eq_neg_normSq`), a generic step doubling the distinct values and keeping them nonzero (`Generic`, `step_card`, `step_ne_zero`), and `2^n` distinct nonzero values after `n` generic steps (`chain_card`); the docstrings of `Capacity.lean` point to it. The ledger has 553 entries (438 theorems, 83 lemmas, 32 definitions) and `#premises` 82 results, `Main.ladder` gone; every declaration is reached. **v18-57 (10 November 2026).** The author's decision of 10 November: Penrose's relation leaves the inputs, and a string's energy comes, on average, from thermodynamics. The paper's -6-38-63 takes as its thermodynamic input that the strings' energies are spread at the horizon's temperature and do not change, the second half being the case the paper has treated throughout, matter that stays put with interactions negligible. A string's energy is one for all its states and differs from string to string with the mass the string holds, from zero up; with the Gibbs weight `e^{−E/k_BT}` on the energy `E` the mean is `∫₀^∞ E e^{−E/θ} dE / ∫₀^∞ e^{−E/θ} dE = θ`, `θ = k_BT_dS` (`Law.gibbsMean`, `Law.gibbsMean_eq`, new), and on a ladder of equal steps `ε` it is `ε/(e^{ε/θ} − 1)`, within one step below `θ` (`Law.gibbsLadder`, `Law.gibbsLadder_eq`, `Law.gibbsLadder_within_step`, new). The heat of a string, the horizon's temperature times its one bit, is derived separately, as before (`Reg.vacuum_per_string`, `Reg.string_split`). New premise: `Sources.Thermo.energies_thermal` (`hGibbs`), the strings' mean energy the mean of the Gibbs distribution at the horizon's temperature. Removed: the namespace `Sources.Penrose` (`energy_over_reduction_time`, `strings_energy`, `hDP`), `Sources.P1.reduction_clock` (`hClock`), and `Solution.energy_same`, which derived that the free strings have one energy and which no result of `Main.lean` used. The weighting of 22 October's fifth input, removed then, weighted the values of one string's energy as states of the string, which on the ladder of the lap clause held more than 200 bits per string against the one bit that fixes `G`. Here the energies do not change: the spread is a difference between strings, not a change in time, so the horizon's entropy, which counts what can change, the sense, does not count it, and each string remains one qubit with one bit. Carriers.lean imports `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecificLimits.Normed` and `Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Gamma.Basic` for the two integrals and the geometric series. **v18-59 (11 November 2026).** The author's request of 10 November: to examine the files so that everything is derived mathematically from the postulates and the three sources, with the paper's text saying what the files prove. Three steps that a definition or a condition carried are now proved, and what the paper no longer states is removed. (1) The one energy of a free string's two senses. `Qubit.Tick.state` was the thermal state written with one energy for both senses, so that the step from `hsense` to the one energy was carried by the definition, as the entry for v18-58 says. The state is now the Gibbs state over the two senses, each at its energy, and the energy of a string of one sense is the energy of the factor by which the tick multiplies it (`Tick.factor`, with `Tick.mulVec_e₁` and `Tick.mulVec_e₂`; `Tick.energy`), as Sec. II defines the energy of a state by the factor of its tick. The energy is a function `En` of the factor that every statement leaves open, since a factor fixes its phase only up to whole turns. `Tick.factor_eq` derives from `hsense` that the two senses have one factor, `Tick.energy_eq` that they have one energy, and `Tick.state_eq` that the state is half the identity; `Tick.entropy_state` is the bit. `Main.string_one_bit` and the second part of `Main.gibbs` are stated on a tick, so that they rest on `hsense` too, and eight results of `Main.lean` use `Tick.energy_eq` in their proofs (the table of inputs names them). In the statements that take the thermal state of a tick the energy `ε` is replaced by the function `En`, and `Complex`, the type of a factor, joins the object types of `Premises.lean`, which lose the seven that no result takes (`Int`, `Horizon.cells`, `Matrix`, `Subgroup`, `Set`, `Entropy.gibbs` and `Decidable`): every type of the list is now the type of an object of some result. (2) The count's exact density. `Sources.Condition.count_density_exact` stated the entropy density of the cosmological horizon with the count in it, as a condition of `Main.jacobson_G_exact`. That result now takes Postulate 1's first sentence for the entropy of the cosmological horizon (`Sources.P1.horizon_entropy`, a `physical`: the horizon's entropy, its density times its area, is `k_B` times the entropy of the state of the register's strings) with the thermal state of free strings (`hfree`, `hq`), and derives the density from `Bridge.horizon_entropy`, one bit for each largest ring; the condition is gone. (3) The horizons with a mass. `Main.massive_horizon` and `Main.black_hole_horizon` concluded their identities for the definition `Reg.η`, which their premises on the law `η` did not enter; they now conclude them for the law at every crossing, from the first parts of `Symmetry.massive_horizon_density` and `Symmetry.black_hole_density`, which give the law one value. (4) The classification of the maximally symmetric spacetimes and their quotients, which v18-58 kept as beyond the paper's present argument, is removed with everything that only it reached, 48 declarations: `Main.cosmological_constant`; the section `Manifold` of `Symmetry.lean` (`dsSlots`, `flatSlots`, `adsSlots`, `Case`, `Determined`, `RegisterManifold`, `eq_described_iff`, `registerManifold_iff`, `cosmological_constant`); `Reg.horizon_radius`, `Reg.horizon_radius_of_rings` and `Reg.rings_model` of `DeSitter.lean`; of `Quotients.lean` the quotients and their exclusion (`form_neg_of_future`, `form_one_zero`, `DSQuotient`, `finite_of_proper`, `exists_fixed_time`, `dsQuotient_bot`, `boostLin`, `inner_snd_self`, `form_boost`, `boost_self`, `eq_zero_of_form`, `boost_injective`, `boost`, `boost_apply`, `form_boost_symm`, `hasDerivAt_pos`, `hasDerivAt_vel`, `hasDerivAt_map`, `mapLin`, `mapLin_isGeodesic`, `mapLin_spacelike`, `fst_eq_of_fix`, `deSitter_rings`, `not_spread`, `flat_no_rings`, `adsCover`, `antiDeSitter_no_rings`, `HasRings`, `rings_force_deSitter`, `hasRings_model`); and of `Geodesics.lean` the geodesics of flat spacetime and the models of the exclusions (`IsFlatGeodesic`, `hypCurve_spacelike`, `antiDeSitter_model`, `lineCurve`, `flat_model`). With them goes the one use the files made of time running one way, the field `time` of `DSQuotient`: no result takes the direction of time. The chain is unchanged; it finds `Λ` from the space at an instant (`Space.lean`). `DeSitter.lean` no longer imports `Geodesics.lean` or `Quotients.lean`, and `Symmetry.lean` imports `Galactic.lean` in place of `Quotients.lean` and `DeSitter.lean`, since they now use nothing of the files they dropped; `Space.lean` imports `Quotients.lean` directly. `scripts/Reach.lean` counts `Tick.mulVec_e₁` and `Tick.mulVec_e₂`, which say what `Tick.factor` is, among the statements of the paper proved where they stand. **v18-60 (11 November 2026).** Text only: no statement, proof or definition changes. Sec. IV.C of the paper named the cell where a string's ring crosses the sphere about a body as the string's receiving cell, and gave the threshold of the galactic law to that cell. A largest ring that enters a sphere also leaves it, so it crosses the sphere at two cells, and nothing picked one of them. The author's decision of 11 November: one threshold for each string, with both crossing cells its receiving cells. The premises state no threshold of their own: `Sources.P1.carriers_by_amplitude` gives the carriers as the fraction `b = 1 − surv(√f)` of the sphere's strings, and `Sources.Thermo.max_entropy_threshold` gives the survival function `surv`. With one threshold for each string, the fraction of the sphere's strings whose threshold lies below the amplitude is that `b`, so the law and every result stand as they are. The text that gave the threshold to a receiving cell, or spoke of one such cell, now speaks of a string's threshold and of its two receiving cells: the docstrings of `Sources.P1.energy_in_carriers`, `Sources.P1.carriers_by_amplitude` and `Sources.Thermo.max_entropy_threshold`, the header of `Carriers.lean` with the docstrings of `Law.share_of_equipartition` and `Reg.radial_acceleration_relation`, the header of `Chain.lean`, and four sentences of this README. Ledger 519 and premise ledger 81 results, as v18-59. Goes with the paper's -6-38-67.

**v18-61 (11 November 2026).** The author's question of 11 November, on a page that draws the derivation as boxes: `Main.area_in_cells`, "The horizon's area in cells", named no source, and many results were like it. Eighteen of the eighty-one results took nothing from a source: no premise of the postulates or of the three other sources, no derived statement and no object of a structure that carries clauses of the postulates. (A nineteenth, `Main.register_in_band`, takes nothing by its nature: it states that a register exists.) Each of the eighteen was true by the definitions of the files and its conditions alone: the horizon's area was the arithmetic of `Reg.RL` and `Reg.Ahor`, the ceiling counted the elements of a type with `L` of them, and the limits of the law were facts about a function. What made each a statement about the register, the horizon or the law stood in a docstring, where the premise ledger does not look. Each now takes premises of the paper's derivation, and the build checks that no result is without one. (1) Secs. II and IV.D. `Main.area_in_cells`, `Main.L_from_horizon` and `Main.hubble` take the constant of the solution without a mass (`Derived.deSitter_horizon`) and speak of the radius `r_h` of its horizon and of its field. `Main.area_in_cells` states `r_h = R_Λ` and `4πr_h² = A_hor = L²ℓ_c²/π`. `Main.L_from_horizon` states `L = 2πr_h/ℓ_c` with `r_h = √(3/Λ)`. `Main.hubble` states the field `−H_∞²r` of the solution without a mass, with `H_∞ = c/R_Λ` and with `H_∞ = 2π/Lt_c`. `Main.observer_rings` takes the premises of `Main.observer_count` and bounds the observer's count itself. `Main.sphere_areal_radius` takes Postulate 2's last sentence (`Sources.P2.rings_geodesics`), which gives the radius of the space at an instant, and states the round sphere in that space, the points `x` with `‖x‖² = 3/Λ`. (2) Sec. IV.A. The paper's derivation of the ceiling takes three sentences of Postulate 1, and the results now take them as two premises. The first is "No ring has more than `L` cells" (`Sources.P1.ring_cells`, new, in place of the condition `card_cells`, so that a string on a shorter ring is covered, as the paper says). The second joins "The bits are the possible outcomes of one measurement of the qubit" and "Any two qubits can be measured jointly", as Sec. IV.A reads them: "A joint measurement is one measurement, so its possible outcomes are the `L` positions", and "The outcome at a position is the bits there". The premise is that what occurs in the state that the register holds, with a positive probability, is the outcome at a position (`Sources.P1.outcomes_at_positions`, new). The ceilings state the random state by the probabilities of the joint outcomes, all of them positive (`Sources.Condition.random_state`), where they stated it by the fractions of 1s on the blocks. The premise then puts a cell that reads 1 and a cell that reads −1 on the block of every joint outcome of the other qubits, and the count is that of `Capacity.lean` (`Capacity.frac_pos_lt_one` is a new lemma for the step). `Main.one_cell_block` takes the same premise: a joint outcome whose block has one cell does not occur with both values of the string's bit. The premise does not fix the probabilities, and nothing else in a statement does. The docstrings read `pr` as the probabilities of the state that the register holds, and no statement says so. In the ceilings and the results on codes `pr` occurs in the premise and in one condition. Each outcome of a set has a cell of either bit exactly when some `pr` meets both the premise and the condition that each outcome of the set occurs with either value of the bit (`Capacity.outcomes_iff_cells`, a new lemma). `Main.block_fraction` proves the paper's sentence "The fraction of 1s on a block is the probability of the outcome 1 given the block's joint outcome" for the conditional probability built from the cells' probabilities (`Capacity.condProb`, new), from the bits as the possible outcomes with equal weights on one ring (`Sources.P1.ring_bits_alike`, new). The ceilings do not take those weights. `Main.coding_step`, `Main.repetition_code`, `Main.line_code` and `Main.surface_code` add to the coding step the room it asks, counted by conditional state, two cells for each. The paper's count for codes agrees with it: the fullest physical string "has up to `2^N` conditional states to hold", and "Where the equal and the opposite case both occur and the `2^N` conditional states are superpositions that all differ in probability, as in a random state, that string needs `2^N` blocks of at least two cells". They take the same two premises, the second for the conditional states: a conditional state that occurs with a value of the bit is at a position with that value. They do not take it with a reading of the earlier physical bits as the outcome. Taken so, the premise asks a cell for each reading and value of the bit that occur together, more than two for each conditional state where several readings give one state; the results on codes state the count by conditional state and no other. Their condition is that, in the state that the register holds, each conditional state that is a superposition occurs with either value of the bit (`Sources.Condition.conditional_states`, new). A physical string then has two cells for each of its conditional states (`Coding.shows_of_outcomes`, a new lemma for the step), so `2^(N+1) ≤ L` in a line code that meets `LineCode.Match`, the surface code among them. `Coding.two_cells_each_model`, new, meets the two hypotheses with two cells for each conditional state strictly between `0` and `1`, so they ask no more cells than the room concluded. The theorem states only that the two hypotheses can be met with that number of cells. Its proof builds the model with weights `0` and `1`, and each block of the model shows its state and holds it only if the state is `1/2`. The paper's ceiling for logical qubits in the surface code was proved in `Coding.lean` only, outside the premise ledger. There the counting theorems `Coding.two_mul_card_states_le`, `Coding.ceiling_both_cases` and `Coding.ceiling_line_code` asked that the share of 1s on the block of a conditional state equal the state, which no finite block does for an irrational state, so that for such states they held with a hypothesis that nothing meets. They now ask that the share lie strictly between `0` and `1`, which is all the count uses. (3) Sec. III.C. `Main.komar_mass` takes the solution about the body (`Sources.Derived.schwarzschild_deSitter`, new, which `Main.schwarzschild_deSitter` now concludes by name) and speaks of its metric. (4) Sec. IV.C. `Main.near_limit` and `Main.deep_limit` take the law (`Sources.Derived.galactic_law`, new, which `Main.galactic_law` concludes) and speak of its acceleration. `Main.temperature_led` takes the Deser–Levin temperature and the thermal horizons, and its condition is the rejected alternative in temperatures. (5) Sec. IV.D. `Main.G_drift` takes `L` constant, the units and `G` at every time, and its condition is the rejected alternative, the horizon at the Hubble radius. `Main.zero_gravity` no longer takes `G` from Jacobson's step, which its conclusion does not need: the radius follows from the constant and the vanishing of the field. Four derived statements were concluded by no result as stated. `Sources.Derived.fill_fraction` and `Sources.Derived.share_acceleration` are now the conclusions of `Main.fill_fraction` and `Main.carriers_share` by name, and `Sources.Derived.deSitter_horizon_in_time` and `Sources.Derived.jacobson_G_in_time` are by definition `Sources.Derived.deSitter_horizon` and `Sources.Derived.jacobson_G` at every time; a result that takes one of the two supposes the premises of the derivation at every time, which the build does not check. `#premises` enforces three rules (`Premises.lean`). A result must take a premise from a source, a derived statement or an object of a structure that carries clauses of the postulates; `Main.register_in_band`, which takes nothing, is listed as stating that such a structure has an instance. A derived statement must be the conclusion of a result that takes something from a source and whose own derived premises are concluded before it, or by its definition such a statement at every time. Every theorem of the namespace `Register.Main`, or of a namespace under it, that stands before the command in `Main.lean` must be among the results that the command is given. The ledger names what each result rests on and the result that concludes each derived statement. The rules read what a result takes, and Lean's unused-variables linter reports a hypothesis that neither the statement nor the proof mentions. Whether a conclusion needs a premise depends on the statement, and no check tests it. Independent reads before release, and an audit of the sixty-two results that this version did not restate, looked for premises of the sources that are taken and not needed, and for texts that say more than the statements. The ceilings went through three earlier forms. They first took the cells' probabilities as equal, a premise that the count did not use. They then took the block's fraction as the probability given its outcome, as a derived statement, which `Main.block_fraction` concluded for other probabilities than those the ceilings spoke of. They then took that equality as a premise, which fixed the state's probabilities as fractions, so that a state with an irrational probability could not meet it. The premise above takes from the postulate's sentences only what the count uses. The results on codes went through one form more. They took the premise for every reading of the earlier bits, which asks two cells for every reading where the paper counts two for every conditional state, and no ring at the paper's ceiling met their hypotheses. `Main.sphere_areal_radius` had a premise that served only a conjunct added for it, and `Main.zero_gravity` took `G` as said; both are restated. `Main.register_even` keeps the positions along the rings, by which the paper argues, where the clauses on incidence suffice (`Horizon.even_L`), and `Main.register_finite` states a finiteness that the structure `Horizon` also has among its fields (`Horizon.finite_of_clauses` is the theorem that the clauses force it); the docstrings of both say so. A result may still state, beside what its premises give, a step of mathematics or an identity between definitions that needs none of them. The docstrings mark such parts where they are known, in `Main.horizon_entropy`, `Main.ring_through_two_cells`, `Main.rings_cross_horizon`, `Main.string_one_bit`, `Main.share_of_bits`, `Main.L_from_horizon`, `Main.entropy_quarter`, `Main.newton`, `Main.entropy_removed`, `Main.entropy_removed_sphere`, `Main.vacuum_density`, `Main.black_hole_quarter`, `Main.fill_fraction`, `Main.scale`, `Main.zero_gravity`, `Main.gibbs`, `Main.coding_step`, `Main.repetition_code`, `Main.line_code` and `Main.surface_code`. The side conditions were not held to the test of the premises. A condition states the case that the paper treats, a positive radius or at least one qubit (`1 ≤ N`), and some conclusions hold beyond their conditions, as the field of `Main.hubble` does at every `r`. The conditions `card_cells`, `nonempty_set`, `random_in_step` and `injective` are gone, and `between`, `hubble_horizon`, `line_match` and `conditional_states` are new. Outside `Main.lean` five theorems change: `Capacity.block_fraction_read` loses the hypothesis that the block is not empty, the three counting theorems of `Coding.lean` take the weaker hypothesis, and `Reg.zero_gravity` loses `G` from Jacobson's step. `Coding.two_cells_each_model` is new. `Main.G_mul_eta` has a title that says what it proves. Ledger 520, one more than v18-60, with the model; premise ledger 81 results, as v18-60. Goes with the paper's -6-38-68.

**v18-62 (11 November 2026).** The author's answer of 11 November; no statement and no proof changes. While v18-61 was prepared, an independent read set two sentences of the paper's Sec. IV.A side by side. One says that "no pairing of `L` positions shows more than `L` joint outcomes, so none shows every joint outcome of a state that has more than `L` of them". The other gives the ceiling for logical qubits in the surface code as 203, counted by conditional states. A random state of `N` logical qubits in a surface code of distance `d` has at least `2^(dN)` joint outcomes of its physical qubits, since each logical basis state is an equal superposition of at least `2^(d−1)` bit strings on its block; they outnumber `L` from 69 logical qubits at `d = 3` at the latest. The results on codes of `Main.lean` count by conditional state and by nothing else. The reading under which they were stated was put to the author, who confirmed it, and the paper's -6-38-69 says it after "or 203 logical qubits": "Below it the encoded state is held with its strings exact and its pairing approximate, as a product state is, even where the joint outcomes of its physical qubits outnumber `L`." No declaration states that sentence. The docstring of `Sources.P1.outcomes_at_positions` quotes it where it says that the results on codes do not take the premise with a reading of the earlier physical bits; the Lean files differ from v18-61 in that docstring alone. Ledger 520 and premise ledger 81 results, as v18-61. Goes with the paper's -6-38-69.

**v18-63 (11 November 2026).** The author's question of 11 November, of the premise named for Schrödinger's equation: why does it not come from the definition of energy that stands with Postulate 1? Sec. II of the paper defines energy: "A state that one tick multiplies by `e^{−iΔ}` has energy `ℏΔ` over the tick, as in quantum mechanics, where a state of energy `E` gains the factor `e^{−iEt/ℏ}` in the time `t`." The free tick multiplies every state of a string by one factor (`Qubit.Tick.phase_unique`), so by that definition the tick of a string of energy `E` is the factor `e^{−iEt_c/ℏ}`. Since v18-54 the files carried that statement as a premise of quantum mechanics, `Sources.QM.schrodinger_tick`, and the paper's Sec. III listed Schrödinger's equation for the tick of a free string as a source beyond the postulates. The definition, which is that of quantum mechanics, says the same in Sec. II, so the separate source is not needed. The premise is now `Sources.P1.string_energies`, under Postulate 1 with Sec. II's definitions, with the same body, and the namespace `QM` is gone. `Main.string_energy` takes it as `hEs`, where it took `hSch`; no other statement changes, and no proof but in that name. The statement reads the definition from the energy to the factor: the energies are given to it, since a factor fixes an energy only up to whole multiples of `2πℏ/t_c`, and it names no turn. The command `#premises` counts as sources the postulates with Sec. II's definitions, thermodynamics and the cited geometry, and its error for a result that takes nothing from a source no longer names quantum mechanics. Beyond the postulates the paper takes from quantum mechanics the gates of Sec. IV.A, which enter no theorem. The paper's -6-38-70 gives the definition as the reason in the three places that gave the equation (Sec. II, Sec. IV.E and Data availability), and its Sec. III no longer lists the equation among what the paper takes from quantum mechanics. Texts changed with it: the headers of `Sources.lean`, `Main.lean`, `Chain.lean`, `Qubit.lean`, `Premises.lean` and `Ledger.lean`, the docstrings of `Thermo.energies_thermal`, `Main.string_energy`, `Reg.string_tick` and `Qubit.Tick.energy`, and this README's account of the inputs with the row of the free tick, which no longer lists `Qubit.Tick.energy` among the definitions removed with the turn reasoning: a definition of that name, a function of the factor, came back in v18-59. Ledger 520 and premise ledger 81 results, as v18-62; the block of `Main.string_energy` in the premise ledger names the new premise. Goes with the paper's -6-38-70.

**v18-64 (12 November 2026).** The author's answers of 12 November and what two independent reads of the new wording found; no statement and no proof changes. The independent read of v18-63 found that the words of Sec. II's definition, "energy `ℏΔ` over the tick", can be read as "during the tick", and that `ℏΔ` alone is an action. The proposal went to the author with v18-63, and he agreed. The paper's -6-38-71 says: "A state that one tick multiplies by `e^{−iΔ}` has energy `ℏΔ/t_c`, as in quantum mechanics, where a state of energy `E` gains the factor `e^{−iEt/ℏ}` in the time `t`". It gives the symbol `t_c` where it defines the tick, two sentences earlier, so that the definition of energy uses a symbol already defined. The files quote the sentence as it now stands: the docstring of `Sources.P1.string_energies`, the header of `Qubit.lean`, the docstring of `Qubit.Tick.energy`, and this README above its history. The entries of the history keep the words that the paper had at their dates. The meaning is the one the files had: with `Δ = Et_c/ℏ`, the tick of a string of energy `E` is `e^{−iEt_c/ℏ}` on every state, and the docstring of `Sources.P1.string_energies` now writes the step so. A first independent read of the new wording found the like in the paper's Sec. IV.E, "`ℏ` over the lap of a largest ring", where "the lap" names no time. The paper's -6-38-71 says "the average is `ℏ` over the lap time of a largest ring", as its Table I and its Sec. IV.D say "over the lap time"; the docstring of `Main.string_energy` follows, and this README above its history in three places, two of which, "`ℏ` over one lap" and "with `τ` the lap", a second read found. The first read also found that the paper's Secs. II and IV.E said that a string's global phase advances, while by Sec. II's own definitions it decreases: "multiplying a state by `e^{iΔ}` changes its global phase by `Δ`", and the tick multiplies a string of energy `E` by `e^{−iEt_c/ℏ}`, so the global phase changes by `−Et_c/ℏ` per tick. Data availability and the files said the same. The paper's -6-38-71 says "decreases" or "decrease" in the four sentences: Sec. II ("the global phase decreases by `Et_c/ℏ` per tick"), Sec. IV.E twice ("decreases by `Eτ/ℏ` in a time `τ`", "decreases by one radian in `L` ticks") and Data availability ("the mean decrease of the global phase per tick `1/L`"). The files follow: the docstrings of `Reg.string_tick` and `Carriers.string_energy`; the docstring of `Main.string_energy`, whose header said "the mean phase of a tick" and whose clause on the factors `e^{−iφ}` called `φ` their phases, with a comment in its proof (the second read); a comment of `scripts/Reach.lean`; and this README above its history, where `Qubit.Tick.ofPhase` is now the free tick of factor `e^{−iθ}`. On 12 November the author asked whether "the cell's tick, energy, mass, and acceleration" of Sec. II are values or units. The tick is a value, the time light takes to cross a cell. `E_c`, `m_c` and `a_c` are the units that `ℓ_c` makes with `ℏ` and `c`: the footnote of the paper's Table I lists them as units, every use gives a quantity as a multiple of one of them, and no sentence gives a cell that energy, mass or acceleration. The paper's -6-38-71 says "The tick is then `t_c = ℓ_c/c`, and the cell's units of energy, mass, and acceleration are `E_c = ℏc/ℓ_c`, `m_c = ℏ/cℓ_c`, and `a_c = c²/ℓ_c`", and the docstrings of `Reg.mc`, `Reg.Ec`, `Reg.ac` and `Reg.ρc` in `Chain.lean` say "the cell's unit of mass" and the like, where they said "the mass of the cell"; the docstring of `Reg.vacuum_ratio` gives the Planck density as `π² ln²2 ρ_c` "in the cell's unit", where it said "in the cell" (the follow-up of the second read). Ledger 520 and premise ledger 81 results, both byte for byte those of v18-63. Goes with the paper's -6-38-71.

**v18-65 (12 November 2026).** The author's two requests of 12 November. The three-page site of the derivation marks each sentence of the paper that no declaration states, and each part of a sentence that the declarations linked to it do not state. The author asked that the files state them. Seven files are new. `Pairing.lean` counts what a pairing of strings measured jointly shows, with the probability of a joint outcome as the product of the frequencies on its blocks (`Pairing.freq_chain`), the state that the strings and their blocks' phases are (`Pairing.state_of_strings`) and the overlap bound (`Pairing.overlap_le`). `Conditional.lean` counts the conditional states of product and cluster states and of a stabilizer measurement, with `Conditional.condState` and, for the stabilizer, `Conditional.stabState`, which is `condState` up to a sign (`Conditional.stabState_spec`); it proves that a string with room for its conditional states leaves no joint outcome without its conditional state on a block, with either bit (`Conditional.room_holds`), and that a block of any size holds the fixed bit of a stabilizer measurement and then shows the product of the neighbors' bits at every position (`Conditional.stabilizer_held`). `Strings.lean` proves Sec. II's sentences on a ring's one string, the lap, the two senses, the sense as a property of the string's state, the bit that a string keeps and the strings not yet scattered. `Receiving.lean` proves that a ring which crosses a sphere meets its surface at two points. `Constant.lean` proves that the inputs of Einstein's equations hold with every value of `Λ`, that Jacobson's step does not see a vacuum term of the stress tensor, the limit up to which the solution has a horizon, which was a number only, and that an accelerated observer's horizon is his own where his acceleration exceeds `c²/R_Λ`. `Matter.lean` names the matter a string holds on average (`Reg.matter`), and the premise for the carriers and Eq. (f) are stated with it (`Sources.P1.carriers_by_amplitude`, `Sources.Derived.fill_fraction`); it also proves that the proper distance and the areal radius agree near the observer and that unequal energies of the two senses would take part of a string's bit. `Interpolation.lean` proves that Milgrom's simple interpolating function lies within `5%` of Eq. (law) at every acceleration. Four premises are new, each a sentence of the paper: the gates of Sec. III, the paper's input from quantum mechanics, with the gate fidelity as the probability of a run without error (`Sources.QM.gates`, whose namespace `#premises` now counts among the sources); Postulate 1's sentence on the hidden global phase (`Sources.P1.phase_hidden`); Postulate 2's clause on directions for a law of a string and the two directions of its ring (`Sources.P2.direction_free`); and Postulate 1's possible outcomes read by conditional state, for what the register holds (`Sources.P1.outcomes_on_blocks`), a reading that Sec. IV.A used and that the paper's -6-38-72 states in words. Sixteen results enter `Main.lean`: `Main.ring_one_string`, `Main.lap`, `Main.two_senses`, `Main.sense_of_state`, `Main.free_tick_hidden`, `Main.bit_kept`, `Main.joint_frequency`, `Main.product_state`, `Main.strings_in_step`, `Main.overlap_bound`, `Main.return_bound`, `Main.vacuum_energy_unseen`, `Main.G_Lambda_bits`, `Main.frame_of_law`, `Main.pull_and_push` and `Main.simple_interpolation`. `Main.bit_kept` states the bit's history for the thermal state of a bath at any temperature, so the hot early universe, the paper's observation, is the premise of no result. `Main.overlap_bound` and `Main.return_bound` take the last of these, Postulate 1's bits as the possible outcomes by conditional state, as the results on codes do, with the conditional states those of the state that the circuit calls for. The bound on the overlap takes the condition that no two joint outcomes give the last qubit one conditional state in that state (`Sources.Condition.all_differ`), which a random state meets and a product state of several joint outcomes does not (`Conditional.product_one_state`); where the register holds the state that the circuit calls for, the return is the gate fidelity. `Main.carriers_share` also concludes the carriers' share `E/b`, at least `E` and two Landauer energies at the sphere's temperature, `Main.fill_fraction` that the body's energy per string exceeds the matter a string holds exactly where `g_N > a₀`, and `Main.constants_in_time` that `a₀` is constant with `G` and `Λ`. `Numerics.lean` gains `Numerics.shots_fraction` and `Numerics.zero_gravity_radius`. `#premises` makes a fourth check: no result names a constant of `Numerics.lean`, a decimal or a whole number above 100, in its statement or in a definition under it, the fields of structures included, but for `Main.register_in_band`, which states an instance; the largest whole number that a result names is the 21 of `Main.simple_interpolation`. The frame of the law, which the files called a reading of the premises, is `Main.frame_of_law`: a field leaves a proper acceleration as it is exactly where it is a common acceleration of the body and of free fall at the observer's place (`Sources.Condition.carried_alike`), and under such a field the acceleration, the temperature of the sphere, the share and Eq. (law) stand as without it. That the law is read for the proper acceleration, and that a uniform external field is such a common acceleration, stay the paper's; the files do not derive them. Three independent readings of drafts of this version led to corrections. The first found that the statement of the frame took its conclusion in a condition, that the check of the numbers read the statements alone, that two definitions of a conditional state stood side by side, and that the pairing results varied the strings where the paper fixes them. The second found that the bound on the return held for a product state too, against the paper, and that the stabilizer result defined the bit that it should derive. The third found that the labels of the conditional states were not tied to the state, so that the bound still followed for a product state; that the paper read Postulate 1's possible outcomes in two ways and did not say which governs the return; and that the frame of the law took the law with the field as a hypothesis. The statements above are the corrected ones. The reading by conditional state is a premise of its own, with the conditional states those of the state that the circuit calls for, and the frame of the law concludes from the uniform field, which is its condition. The ledger has 598 entries (480 theorems, 79 lemmas, 39 definitions) and the premise ledger 97 results. The paper's -6-38-72 carries the text.

**v18-66 (12 November 2026).** The author's answer of 12 November. v18-65 took Postulate 1's possible outcomes by conditional state as a premise of its own, `Sources.P1.outcomes_on_blocks`, with two sentences that the paper's -6-38-72 added to Sec. IV.A, and the author was asked to confirm them. He answered that no premise is to be added: a joint outcome is not a conditioned outcome, and strings that are measured jointly are not thereby conditioned. The premise is removed, with `Conditional.readings_agree`, which compared the two readings, and the paper's -6-38-73 drops the two sentences. `Main.overlap_bound` and `Main.return_bound` now take the premise that the ceilings and the results on codes take, `Sources.P1.outcomes_at_positions`, for the last qubit's conditioned outcomes: a conditional state that occurs with a bit in what the register holds has that bit at a cell of a block that holds the state. As in the results on codes, the conditional states are those of the state that the circuit calls for, and the probabilities those of the state that the register holds. A label stands for each conditional state, by the new condition `Sources.Condition.state_given`: two joint outcomes of the other qubits have one label exactly where the state that the circuit calls for gives the last qubit one conditional state given either. Where the conditional states all differ (`Sources.Condition.all_differ`), a conditioned outcome is one joint outcome, the premise is the premise by joint outcome for what the register holds, and the bound on the overlap follows as before. A product state gives the last qubit one conditional state, however many joint outcomes of the other qubits occur, and the bound does not follow for it (`Conditional.product_one_state`). `Conditional.room_holds` is stated by label: a string with two cells for each conditional state has a pairing and bits with which the premise is met whatever the register holds. `Main.lean` no longer imports `Conditional.lean`. An independent reading of a draft of this version asked whose conditional states the labels stand for, and the texts now say it. Against v18-64 three premises are new, where v18-65 had four: `Sources.QM.gates`, `Sources.P1.phase_hidden` and `Sources.P2.direction_free`. The ledger has 597 entries (479 theorems, 79 lemmas, 39 definitions) and the premise ledger 97 results. The paper's -6-38-73 carries the text.

**v18-67 (12 November 2026).** Two changes of the paper's Sec. IV.C, in the paragraph on which strings carry the body's information, both at the author's word. The paper's -6-38-74 adds one sentence after the sentence that defines the string's threshold: an analog, the locking of a laser to an injected signal, in which the laser takes its phase from the signal once the signal's amplitude, measured against the laser's own, exceeds a threshold set by the difference between their frequencies. The defining sentence had given the threshold no meaning. The paper adds the two references of the sentence. The paper had also said that Postulate 1 does not fix how the threshold varies from string to string, which claimed more than is known, since no derivation from the postulate was tried. It now says that the variation is not derived, and that Postulate 1 fixes the threshold's unit. The analog is not a step of the derivation, and the files state the threshold and its distribution as before (`Sources.P1.carriers_by_amplitude`, `Sources.Thermo.max_entropy_threshold`). No statement, no docstring and no premise changes: the tree differs from v18-66 in this README alone. The ledger has 597 entries (479 theorems, 79 lemmas, 39 definitions) and the premise ledger 97 results, as in v18-66. The paper's -6-38-74 carries the text.

**v18-68 (13 November 2026).** The paper's -6-38-75 adds one sentence to Data availability, at the author's word: the sentences of the paper are linked to the statements of these files at https://papers.alphalatitude.com/sphere-string-ring/v18-68/. The pages there are built from the paper's source and from this tree. They are a way to read the two together, and the files here remain what the paper's results rest on. No statement, no docstring and no premise changes: the tree differs from v18-67 in this README alone. The ledger has 597 entries (479 theorems, 79 lemmas, 39 definitions) and the premise ledger 97 results, as in v18-67. The paper's -6-38-75 carries the text.
